and a fifth on 2026-09-20 **pulled the lit controls' bloom in to a tight halo** — measured against the reference, ours threw 10–19px of glow where the mockup throws 2–4px, so the outer stops went `4/13/28 → 3/7/12` (hover `4/9/15`, step disc `3/8`) while the rim, mottle fill and Bebas caps were left exactly as approved (R72); and a sixth on 2026-09-20 **stopped the film page's metadata falling into the right-hand gutter on a phone** — at a true 390px viewport the 6rem label column left each value only 74px, so the poster + title now form a centred plate and every field stacks label-over-value across the full 358px column, taking a long cast list from 13 wrapped lines to 3 (R73). Only M6 item left: real-domain deploy, **unblocked 2026-09-20** — the owner supplied the target (`https://reeltalk.minnix.dev`, Cloudflare DNS-only → nginx proxy manager on `192.168.1.141` terminating TLS → `192.168.1.138:3030`, invite-only signup, fresh database) and the pre-flight check came back clean: explicit CNAME to a public IP, valid LE wildcard cert held by NPM, 80 redirects, 3030 not publicly exposed. **Deploy-readiness increment 1 landed the same day (R74):** the forwarded TLS scheme is now believed only from the operator's terminator, cookies carry `Secure` per transport with LAN HTTP deliberately still working, and dead `BASE_URL` is gone. **Deploy-readiness increment 2 landed the same day (R75):** the last two ungated reads of `X-Forwarded-Proto` — the ones behind every published ActivityPub URL and behind inbound `@target-uri` verification — now read `request.scheme`, which only the gate may raise, leaving exactly one scheme source in the app; proven live over real TCP that a trusted terminator yields `https://reeltalk.minnix.dev/…` with no `:3030`, and that the same header from any other peer does not. **Deploy-readiness increment 3 landed the same day (R76):** documentation only — `.env.example` now states the firewall expectation for the `0.0.0.0`-published `WEB_PORT`, including that R74's forwarded-scheme gate is **not** an access control and cannot substitute for keeping the cleartext port closed. **Deploy-readiness increment 4 landed the same day (R76):** the four policy-blind signup CTAs (header, home landing, Getting Started, login) now follow the instance policy through one new `signup_open` context processor — closed **hides** the CTA rather than dangling it onto the "not accepting new signups" notice, with `Log in` untouched everywhere; verified live on the deployed container by flipping the policy to `invite` and back (signup links 2/2/2/1 → 0/0/0/0, login links unchanged). Probing the public domain during this increment also confirmed, over the real path rather than `/proc/net/tcp`, that **NPM presents to us as `192.168.1.141`** — the address `TRUSTED_PROXIES` must match — with the request reaching our container and 400ing only at `ALLOWED_HOSTS`. Increments 5–6 (restore drill + `DEPLOYING.md`, then the cutover itself) remain. **Deploy-readiness increment 5 landed the same day (R77):** there was no restore path anywhere in the repo — `backup_database` was the only management command and `pg_restore` appeared in no non-test code — so the restore had to be built before it could be drilled. `restore_database` never defaults its target (`--into` is required; naming the live database additionally needs `--force`; the target must already exist), and the drill ran end to end against a throwaway database in the same cluster: **24 public tables matching live row for row** (1385 films / 1385 shelf rows / 9 statuses / 2 users), the app booted against the restored DB reporting `No migrations to apply` and served restored content over HTTP, and teardown left the cluster back at four databases with **live diffing identical to its pre-drill baseline**. `DEPLOYING.md` documents the whole procedure; the same round trip now runs in the suite. The same increment fixed a live defect sitting directly on the invite-only path: the user admin was a bare `ModelAdmin`, so its add form exposed the `password` column as a plain **visible** text input and wrote whatever was typed straight into it **unhashed** — the account could not log in and a plaintext secret was left in the row. **Deploy-readiness increment 6 — THE CUTOVER — landed 2026-09-21 (R78): the instance is live at `https://reeltalk.minnix.dev`.** The `X-Forwarded-Proto` link that R74/R75/R76 each had to leave unverified was **proved over real HTTPS before any destructive step**, by applying the cutover env to the existing container with the old database intact: actor id `https://reeltalk.minnix.dev/user/minnix/` with no `:3030`, and the same spoofed header from the LAN still returning `http://`. Fresh database by **rename, not drop** (`reeltalk_legacy_20260921` holds the whole pre-cutover DB, so rollback is a rename back), instance B retired with its volumes kept, `signup_policy=invite` set immediately after migrations, the owner's account created through the `/setup/` wizard, and `watchlist.csv` imported through the service layer — **1670 rows → 1378 created / 292 skipped (TV)**, backfilled to **1367/1378 posters**. All five §11 checks green, including LAN plain-HTTP still served. The cutover itself changed no tracked files (`.env` is gitignored). The same increment's gate surfaced that **three security tests were passing on an ambient default rather than on the control** (R79) — fixed to model the real middleware chain, with non-vacuity proven by mutation. **Deploy-readiness increment 7 landed the same day (R80): the whole search surface is members-only, superseding the anonymous half of R29.** `/search/` takes `@login_required`; `/search/suggest/` deliberately does **not** — it answers an XHR that parses JSON, so an anonymous call gets an explicit `401 {"error": "login required"}` instead of a redirect that would deliver the login page's HTML where the client expects a payload — and the header search box is not rendered at all for anonymous visitors, which required pinning `.header-user` to the last grid column or "Log in" would have landed centered in the vacated 34rem search column. The reason is money, not secrecy: every anonymous read spends the instance's single shared, rate-limited, paid TMDB quota, and with signup closed that cost falls entirely on the owner. `DEPLOYING.md` §12's "search_suggest has no login gate" bullet is deleted because it stops being true. Final gate: `ruff check` / `format --check` / `makemigrations --check` clean, pytest **842 passed + 5 skipped in 365.39s**, verified live over real HTTPS (`suggest` → 401 JSON, `/search/` → 302 to `/login/`, anonymous header carries no `.global-search`) with plain-HTTP LAN access re-checked on the same deploy and still served. **R81 followed the same day on the owner's request:** the home rail stays visible to strangers — the trending titles and popular genres are what help someone decide whether to join — but those rows no longer link anywhere for them; a member gets `<a>`, a stranger gets the same text in a `<span>`. The pill's styling moved from the element selector `.genre-pills a` to a `.genre-pill` class so the tag swap doesn't drop it, and both hover rules were scoped to `a.` because `:hover` matches spans too and would otherwise make dead text light up like a link. **Deploy-readiness increment 8 landed the same day (R82): invite links.** A member clicks **Invite** on their own profile, gets a single-use URL to paste wherever they already talk to people, and the invitee lands on the signup page prefaced with who invited them. Who may send is a site setting — `invite_scope`, **Admins only** by default, **All members** on a flip in Django admin. The link is treated as the credential it is: 32 chars from `secrets.token_urlsafe`, a 7-day window, and a redemption that holds a `select_for_update` row lock across creating the account and marking the invite spent, because check-then-mark is only true until two strangers open one link in the same second. A valid link is deliberately independent of `signup_policy`, so flipping signup open can never silently invalidate links already sent. Same commit also de-underlined every `<a class="btn">`, which had been rendering as underlined text while its `<button class="btn">` siblings did not. **The finding that outranks the feature: reading the live policy instead of trusting the record showed `signup_policy = open` / `openRegistration = true` — the public instance was accepting anyone.** Owner confirmed it was deliberate at the time (opened to see the anonymous signup page) and had it set back to Invite-only. Durable lesson recorded: a security-relevant value living in a database row is invisible to code review, so a deploy check must read the live value rather than restate the recorded one. Gate: ruff / format / `makemigrations --check` clean, pytest **882 passed + 5 skipped** (35 new); verified live end-to-end over real HTTPS, mint → redeem → second stranger refused. `DEPLOYING.md` §10 rewritten (it claimed there were no invite links, and had `POST /signup/` as 403 when it is 200). **Increment 8b followed on the owner's report:** the R82 template comment was printing itself as visible page text above the invite box, because `{# … #}` is single-line only and mine was wrapped — split to two lines and guarded repo-wide by `test_no_template_has_an_unterminated_hash_comment`, proven non-vacuous against the shipped file. Gate: pytest **883 passed + 5 skipped**. **M6's deploy item is DONE; remaining M6 work is the parked design polish (R73) and the PLAN.md §5 feature list.** See §2 M6 increment plan. |

## 2. Execution record

### M1 — increment plan (forward-looking; ~7 increments across sessions)

M1 = "core film domain, first working version, part 1" (PLAN.md §5). It is built as small verified increments; **each ends in a committed green checkpoint** (pytest + ruff clean) with this file updated, then the session stops. Scope per increment:

1. ✅ **Core app + Film domain** — `reeltalk.core`: Film + MergedFilm models, `sort_title`, D7 dedup (`find_match`), tsvector trigger. *(done 2026-09-06, `cbf72c2`.)*
2. ✅ **Social app + User + auth** — `reeltalk.social`: custom `User` model set as `AUTH_USER_MODEL` (R10), signup/login/logout views, first-run setup wizard. *(done 2026-09-06, `a41c87b`.)*
3. ✅ **Shelf/ShelfFilm + binary defaults** — Shelf + ShelfFilm models; default shelves (`to-read`=Watchlist, `read`=Watched) created on user save; the full merge/absorb *logic* (re-point shelves/statuses onto a canonical Film) landed here now that related models exist. *(done 2026-09-06, `d45a1e4` + `cac1627`.)*
4. ✅ **Status/Review/ReviewRating + watch rules** — single-table `Status` with a type discriminator (R17); §3.3 rules at model level: rating required to mark watched (validated before any write), one Review per user per film (D5, partial unique index), rating-only entries; `mark_watched()` finish-flow helper; merge re-points statuses (R16). *(done 2026-09-06, `495469a`.)*
5. ✅ **Film pages + shelve controls + finish flow** — create/edit/view film views + templates; shelve/unshelve with D1 mutual exclusion; "mark watched" finish flow (rating required) wired to `mark_watched()`; edit-review per D5. *(done 2026-09-07, `313c6e1` + `4faf81b` + `2c00ba7` + `ba0b94f`.)*
6. ✅ **User films page (3 tabs) + minimal feed** — `GET /user/<localname>/films/` with exactly three tabs (All films / Watchlist / Watched per §3.3 rule 1 + D1), publicly readable; minimal home feed of own + followed users' statuses (§3.6 v0.1). *(done 2026-09-07, `dbfc206` + `f68599e`.)*
7. ✅ **Admin basics + landing/about + exit-bar verification** — site settings + link-domain allowlist (`622c34f`), landing/about pages (`75ad1fd`), admin branding + film merge/absorb tool (`ad5c465`); M1 exit bar verified live end-to-end over HTTP and this file finalized. *(done 2026-09-08.)*

**M1 exit bar (across all sessions):** owner can sign up on the local instance and run the whole watchlist→watched→review loop. **Verified 2026-09-08** with a throwaway account over live HTTP (increment 7 record below); verification data was removed afterwards, so the instance is clean for the owner's own run. No new runtime deps or services were introduced in M1 (web + db only; Django templates + vanilla JS). Conventions to follow are recorded in §4 — especially R9 (app structure), R10 (custom user model now), R11 (minimal neutral CSS; real styling deferred to M6 with the owner per D17).

### M0 (executed 2026-09-05)

**Spec + audit:** `PLAN.md` written — functional spec distilled from the legacy `PROGRESS.md` feature inventory, a walk of the legacy codebase (Film model, `tmdb.py`, AP wire types, deployment files), and the owner decision log (legacy §8 #1–34, renumbered D1–D17 in PLAN.md §2). License audit of every legacy dependency (PyPI metadata + GitHub where sparse) and every static asset/font — verdicts in PLAN.md §4. Key results: **no dependency is license-blocked**; `bw-file-resubmit` (the flagged first target) is **MIT** → compatible, but a native ~20-line widget is recommended instead; the icomoon icon font and all BookWyrm artwork are ❌ do-not-carry-over.

**Dev environment:** repo scaffolding — lean audited `pyproject.toml` (15 main deps, 9 dev), two-stage `Dockerfile`, `docker-compose.yml` (nginx :3030 / web / db postgres:17 / redis / celery_worker / celery_beat; project name `reeltalk` so volumes are `reeltalk_*`, no collision with the kept legacy `reeltalk-work_*` insurance volumes), `entrypoint.sh` (auto migrate + collectstatic on web start), `.env.example` + `setup.sh`, fresh plain-HTTP `nginx/default.conf` (no proxy cache in v0.1; `X-Forwarded-Proto $scheme` passthrough), minimal Django project (`reeltalk/` settings/urls/wsgi, `celerytalk/`) + smoke test.

**Verification:** `docker compose up -d --build` → all services healthy; entrypoint applied migrations (admin/auth/contenttypes/sessions/django_celery_beat) + collectstatic; `curl -4 http://localhost:3030/` serves the index through nginx; `/admin/login/` 200; in-container `pytest` **1 passed** (smoke); `ruff check` + `ruff format --check` green.

**Gotcha hit & fixed:** first build failed at web start — `ENTRYPOINT ["/entrypoint.sh"]` but `COPY . /app/` only placed the script at `/app/entrypoint.sh`. Fixed with an explicit `COPY entrypoint.sh /entrypoint.sh` (legacy had the same shape).

**Test baseline:** 1 passed (smoke only — real coverage starts with M1).

### Stack audit (executed 2026-09-05, commit `95b332c`)

Per AUDIT-BRIEF.md (consumed + deleted in that commit): audited the M0 stack against current best practice and applied changes in-repo. Full findings table + rejected alternatives: PLAN.md §3.9; license verdicts for new/changed deps: PLAN.md §4.4.

**What changed (applied):**
- **Python 3.11 → 3.13**, pip → **uv** with committed `uv.lock` (51 packages, resolves in <1 s).
- **Django 5.2.16 → 6.1.1** (current stable; adopted the new `MAILERS` setting — `EMAIL_*` is deprecated and cannot coexist with `MAILERS`).
- **gunicorn/WSGI → uvicorn/ASGI** (`reeltalk/asgi.py`; `wsgi.py` removed).
- **Task queue deferred to M2**: celery + django-celery-beat + redis broker removed from deps/compose; when M2 lands, **Django-Q2 on a Postgres cluster** (license-audited now, §4.4) replaces all three — no Redis service at any milestone.
- **Redis removed** entirely: LocMemCache + DB sessions.
- **nginx removed**: whitenoise serves static in-process; media via a `serve` URL pattern; web exposes :3030 directly (D14 operator TLS unchanged).
- **Dockerfile**: lockfile-first layer caching, venv at /app/.venv, **non-root `appuser`**.
- **Toolchain**: ruff 0.16.6 (config: +`I`/`UP` rules, legacy-carried ignores dropped), mypy 1.7.1 → 2.3.1 + django-stubs[compatible-mypy] 6.1.0, pytest 9.1.1 stack, responses 0.26.3; pins bumped (requests 2.34.2, mistune 3.3.4, django-csp 4.0, environs 15.2.0, psycopg 3.3.5).
- **Compose**: 6 services → **2** (`web`, `db`); M2 adds a third (`worker`, same image).

**Gotchas hit (fixed, documented in PLAN.md §3.9):** uv silently skips a custom `[dependency-groups] main` (PEP 735 reserves the name — the first build installed only the dev group; runtime deps moved to `[project.dependencies]`); Django 6.1 rejects `MAILERS` alongside any `EMAIL_*` module attribute; root-owned artifact volumes broke the non-root switch (verified `static_volume`/`media_volume` held only regenerable files, then deleted + re-initialized them, plus removed the orphaned `redis_data`).

**Test baseline:** before = 1 passed (smoke), `curl -4 /` + `/admin/login/` 200, ruff green. After = **1 passed**, all endpoints 200 incl. whitenoise-served static, ruff check + format green. Suite count unchanged by design — real coverage starts with M1.

**Decisions:** R1–R8 in §4 below (none owner-blocking; recorded for the record).

### M1 increment 1 — core app + Film domain (executed 2026-09-06, commit `cbf72c2`)

First increment of M1. New `reeltalk.core` app with the flat **Film** model (PLAN.md §3.2, D2) and **MergedFilm**. No new runtime deps — everything used was already in the audited set (`django.contrib.postgres` ArrayField is built into Django).

**What landed:**
- `Film`: title / `sort_title` (auto-derived on save — leading article stripped, lowercased) / subtitle / description (HTML) / year / runtime / `genres`+`directors`+`cast` as Postgres `ArrayField(text[])` (plain name-lists per D2) / poster / `tmdb_id`+`imdb_id` dedup identity / `origin_id`+`remote_id` (M4 federation identity, present but unused until M4).
- `Film.find_match()` — the D7 dedup order: exact `tmdb_id`, then `imdb_id`, then normalized title+year. Returns the row to match/backfill onto, else None.
- `MergedFilm` (old_id→new_id) + `resolve_film_id()` — keeps absorbed-film URLs redirecting (§3.2). The full merge/absorb *logic* (re-pointing shelves/statuses) is deferred to the Shelf increment (needs those models); only the id-mapping + resolution landed here.
- `search_vector`: a **trigger-maintained Postgres tsvector column** added by raw-SQL migration `0002` — deliberately NOT a Django model field, so the ORM never reads/writes it and can't clobber it. Weights per §3.2: A=title, B=subtitle, C=directors+cast, D=genres; `simple` text-search config (titles are proper nouns — no stemming). M2's local search will query it via RawSQL.
- Settings: registered `reeltalk.core` + `django.contrib.postgres`; added a shared top-level `templates/` dir (repo root) to TEMPLATES DIRS; the project package `reeltalk` is no longer itself an app.

**Workflow note for future sessions (this box):** the image has **no live source mount** (`COPY . /app/` at build), so every code change needs `docker compose build web` before the container/tests see it. Fast loop that works:
- Generate migrations locally (no rebuild): `docker run --rm -v /home/minnix/reeltalk:/src:z -w /src --entrypoint /app/.venv/bin/python reeltalk-web manage.py makemigrations <app>` (the `failed to resolve host 'db'` warning is harmless — makemigrations doesn't need the DB).
- Lint/format local files without a rebuild: same `/src` mount with `--entrypoint /app/.venv/bin/ruff reeltalk-web check --fix --no-cache .` (and `format --no-cache .`). `--no-cache` is required — the bind-mounted `.ruff_cache` isn't writable by the container user.
- Then `docker compose build web` and `docker compose run --rm web bash -c 'ruff check . && ruff format --check . && pytest'`.
- If a test-DB migration fails mid-run, a stale `test_reeltalk` DB is left behind: drop it with `docker compose exec -T db psql -U reeltalk -d postgres -c 'DROP DATABASE IF EXISTS test_reeltalk;'` before re-running.

**Test baseline:** before = 1 passed (smoke). After = **21 passed** (19 new: sort_title derivation, D7 dedup paths, merge-id resolution, tsvector trigger populate + recompute), ruff check + format green, `makemigrations --check` reports no drift, migrations apply to dev DB (`core_film`, `core_mergedfilm` + trigger present), site HTTP 200.

**Decisions:** R9–R11 in §4 below (engineering calls, none owner-blocking; recorded so the owner can veto).

### M1 increment 2 — social app + User + auth (executed 2026-09-06, commit `a41c87b`)

Second increment of M1. New `reeltalk.social` app with the custom **User** model now set as `AUTH_USER_MODEL` (R10 — done before any social migration existed), core auth views, and the first-run setup wizard (PLAN.md §3.7). No new runtime deps.

**What landed:**
- `User` (`AbstractBaseUser` + `PermissionsMixin`, `USERNAME_FIELD = "localname"`): localname (max 30, unique) / display_name / email (optional, not yet unique — password reset lands later in M1 and needs it) / summary (HTML-from-markdown like Film.description; no UI writes it until profile edit) / avatar / `local` flag (remote mirrors are M4) / is_staff (custom users must define it — PermissionsMixin only provides is_superuser/groups/permissions) / date_joined. Follow/block relations per R10: `follows` (M2M self, related_name followers), `blocks` (M2M self, related_name blocked_by), `blocked_films` (M2M core.Film). Server-level blocking, feed filters, 2FA, email verification deferred to M4/M5 (R13).
- `UserManager` with create_user/create_superuser; `username` property = `localname@DOMAIN` (§3.2 identity; M4 refines for remote users); `get_full_name()` falls back to localname.
- Auth: signup view + `SignupForm` (localname regex `[a-zA-Z0-9][a-zA-Z0-9._-]*`, case-insensitive uniqueness at signup, Django password validators, confirm-match), built-in LoginView/LogoutView (Django ≥5 logout is POST-only; `LOGOUT_REDIRECT_URL = "/"`), templates `base/login/signup/home` at the repo-root `templates/` dir and `social/setup.html` in the app.
- First-run setup wizard (R12): while no superuser exists, both `/` and `/signup/` redirect to `/setup/`; POST there creates the first account as admin (is_staff + is_superuser) and logs it in; afterwards the wizard redirects away and signup opens. The §3.7 open/invite-gated policy lands with site settings (increment 7).
- R11 stylesheet: `reeltalk/social/static/css/reeltalk.css` — small, deliberately neutral, hand-written (~150 lines); app-dir static picked up by collectstatic via APP_DIRS.
- Settings: `reeltalk.social` in INSTALLED_APPS, `AUTH_USER_MODEL = "social.User"`, DEBUG-conditional staticfiles storage (below).

**Gotchas hit & fixed:**
- **Strict manifest storage broke the test loop.** The entrypoint only runs collectstatic when it starts uvicorn, so `docker compose run --rm web ... pytest` rendered `{% static %}` against a stale/absent manifest → `ValueError: Missing staticfiles manifest entry`. Fix: under DEBUG use `whitenoise.storage.CompressedStaticFilesStorage` (whitenoise serves from finders in DEBUG anyway); production keeps `CompressedManifestStaticFilesStorage` — no change to the verified M0 prod path.
- **Dev DB recreated.** The M0 dev DB had been migrated with the *default* user model: orphaned `auth_user` + `django_admin_log` FK pointing at it, which would have broken admin log writes after the AUTH_USER_MODEL switch (and `makemigrations --check` reported InconsistentMigrationHistory). Verified zero user-data rows first; dropped + recreated the DB and let the entrypoint re-migrate. Also cleared M0's leftover `django_celery_beat_*` tables.
- **Django 6 custom-user details** (for future increments): AuthenticationForm always names its field `username` (label from USERNAME_FIELD) and maps it onto localname internally; session auth keys are private (`_auth_user_id`, …) — assert with the public `django.contrib.auth.SESSION_KEY` constant.

**Test baseline:** before = 21 passed. After = **49 passed** (28 new: user manager/model + relations, signup validation paths, login/logout incl. POST-only 405, setup wizard lifecycle, admin-site auth with the custom user; smoke test updated to assert the first-run redirect), ruff check + format green, `makemigrations --check` no drift, dev DB re-migrated clean (`social_user` + through tables, no orphan `auth_user`).

**Site check:** `/` → 302 `/setup/` (fresh instance), `/setup/` 200, `/login/` 200, hashed CSS served by whitenoise 200. The live dev instance is intentionally left at the first-run wizard so the owner gets the real setup flow.

**Decisions:** R12–R13 in §4 below (engineering calls, none owner-blocking; recorded so the owner can veto).

### M1 increment 3 — Shelf/ShelfFilm + binary defaults + merge/absorb (executed 2026-09-06, commits `d45a1e4`, `cac1627`)

Third increment of M1. `Shelf` + `ShelfFilm` models in `reeltalk.core` (R9), the D1 binary default shelves created on user save, and the full merge/absorb logic (§3.2). No new runtime deps.

**What landed:**
- `Shelf`: name / identifier (fixed by D1: `to-read`, `read`; unique per user) / description / user FK (CASCADE) / films M2M through ShelfFilm / origin_id+remote_id (same day-one federation-identity pattern as Film; R14). No privacy/editable fields in v0.1 — the custom-shelf UI is gone (D16); M4's wire type can add what it needs then.
- `ShelfFilm` (through row): shelf (CASCADE) / film (**PROTECT** — a film cannot be deleted while shelved; merge re-points first, so merges are unaffected) / user (the actor who shelved; non-null, defaults to the shelf owner in save()) / shelved_date; unique (film, shelf). Django 6.1 gotcha: the M2M manager's `.add()` bulk-inserts **without calling the through model's save()**, so shelving code creates rows explicitly or passes `through_defaults={"user": ...}` — documented on the model (R14).
- Default shelves on user save (R15): a new **local** user gets both D1 shelves via `User.save()` → `Shelf.create_default_shelves`; remote mirrors (M4) receive their shelves from federation instead. Import direction is social→core only; core references `"social.User"` as a string FK, so no Python or migration cycle (core/0003 depends on social/0001 via `swappable_dependency`).
- Merge/absorb (R16): `Film.merge_into(canonical)` — all-or-nothing (`transaction.atomic`): backfill empty fields onto the canonical via an explicit field list (subtitle/description/year/runtime/poster/tmdb_id/imdb_id scalars; order-preserving union for the genres/directors/cast arrays), re-point ShelfFilm rows (shelved_date + actor preserved; a film already on the same shelf absorbs its duplicate row) and `User.blocked_films`, record the MergedFilm old→new mapping, delete the absorbed row. Identity/provenance fields (title/sort_title/origin_id/remote_id/dates) are never backfilled. Status re-pointing slots in at increment 4 via `_repoint_related()` — one block per related model.
- Admin: minimal ModelAdmins for Shelf + ShelfFilm.

**Gotchas hit & fixed:**
- **Backfill vs unique constraint.** Saving the canonical *before* deleting the absorbed row made a backfilled `tmdb_id` collide with the value the absorbed row still held (`core_film_tmdb_id_key`). Order is now: re-point → MergedFilm row → delete absorbed → save canonical — all inside the transaction, so any failure rolls back wholesale.
- **`delete()` clears the instance pk.** After `merge_into`, the absorbed instance's `.id` is None (Django sets it); callers/tests must capture the id beforehand.

**Test baseline:** before = 49 passed. After = **68 passed** (19 new: default-shelf lifecycle incl. remote-user exclusion and no-duplicate-on-resave, ShelfFilm actor defaulting + one-row-per-(film,shelf) constraint + the M2M `.add()` gotcha, backfill semantics incl. D7 ID backfill and array unions, re-pointing across users/shelves/blocks, dedup paths, id-mapping + deletion), ruff check + format green, `makemigrations --check` no drift.

**Site check:** dev instance restarted with the new image; entrypoint applied `core/0003` (`core_shelf`, `core_shelffilm` present). DB has zero users — the instance is still at the first-run `/setup/` wizard (200), so the owner's admin account will get its default shelves automatically on creation; no backfill needed.

**Decisions:** R14–R16 in §4 below (engineering calls, none owner-blocking; recorded so the owner can veto).

### M1 increment 4 — Status + watch rules (executed 2026-09-06, commit `495469a`)

Fourth increment of M1. `Status` model in `reeltalk.core` (owner direction; amends R9's parenthetical) implementing §3.2's Comment/Review/ReviewRating subtypes as one table with a `status_type` discriminator (R17), the §3.3 watch rules at model level, and the Status block of merge re-pointing (R16). No new runtime deps.

**What landed:**
- `Status`: user (PROTECT) / film (PROTECT; null for standalone notes — no v0.1 composer for those) / `status_type` (comment/review/review_rating; null = standalone) / content (HTML rendered from markdown at write time, like Film.description) + raw_content (the markdown source, so an edit can pre-fill a form) / rating (Decimal 3,2 with 0.5–5 validators) / published_date (ordering: newest first) + edited_date / soft-delete (`deleted` + `deleted_date`; `status.delete()` keeps the row as a tombstone with identity intact for M4 federation consistency and clears content — queryset bulk delete is not used in our code paths) / reply_parent (threading, M4/M5) / local flag + origin_id/remote_id (day-one federation identity, same pattern as Film/Shelf).
- D5 at the database: partial unique index `unique_review_per_user_per_film` on (user, film) WHERE status_type IN (review, review_rating) AND NOT deleted — a soft-deleted review can be replaced; comments are exempt. Model-level invariants in save(): a rating-only entry must carry its rating; typed statuses must be anchored to a film.
- `mark_watched(user, film, *, rating, content="", raw_content="")` (§3.3 rules 3–4): validates the star rating **before any DB write** (`validate_star_rating`: 0.5–5 in half steps — keeps D10's rating×2 export integral), shelves onto Watched + removes from Watchlist (D1 binary model; mirrors legacy's mutually-exclusive read-status shelves), creates a written review or a rating-only entry, and re-finishing updates the existing row in place (new rating applied; empty text keeps existing content; edited_date set) — the row keeps its type even when it later gains text (legacy behavior). Posts no feed note (D3).
- Merge/absorb: `_repoint_statuses()` block added to `Film._repoint_related()` (R16) — comments/reviews/ratings move to the canonical film; a film with statuses cannot be deleted outright (PROTECT, tested).
- Admin: minimal Status ModelAdmin.

**Gotchas hit & fixed:**
- **Nested choices class namespaces.** Referencing `Status.Type` unqualified inside `Meta.constraints` and in `save()` raised NameError — `Meta` and method bodies don't see the class body's locals. Literals in the constraint (with a comment), `Status.Type.` in the method.

**Test baseline:** before = 68 passed. After = **99 passed** (31 new: ordering, soft-delete tombstone semantics + idempotency, rating-only invariant, film anchoring, rating field bounds, D5 partial index incl. deleted-review replacement and comment exemption, mark_watched paths incl. validate-before-write and watchlist removal, update-in-place re-finish, empty-text-keeps-content, post-deletion re-finish, validate_star_rating input matrix, merge re-pointing, PROTECT), ruff check + format green, `makemigrations --check` no drift, dev DB migrated (`core_status` + partial index verified in pg_indexes).

**Site check:** dev instance restarted with the new image; entrypoint applied `core/0004`. **Since then (2026-09-07) the owner completed the `/setup/` wizard in a browser**: admin account `minnix` exists (is_staff + is_superuser) with both default shelves (verified in DB), `/` serves 200, `/setup/` redirects to `/`, and signup is now open. Do not assume first-run/zero-user state.

**Decisions:** R17 in §4 below (engineering call, recorded so the owner can veto).

### M1 increment 5 — film pages + shelve controls + finish flow (executed 2026-09-07, commits `313c6e1`, `4faf81b`, `2c00ba7`, `ba0b94f`)

Fifth increment of M1. The first views/urls/templates in `reeltalk.core`: the film detail page, manual create/edit with the D4 TMDB lock, watchlist shelve/unshelve with D1 mutual exclusion, and the §3.3 finish flow (mark watched with a required star rating) + D5 edit-review. Views stay thin — all watch-state/shelving/review rules live in the model layer (`mark_watched`, `shelve_to_watchlist`, `unshelve_from_watchlist`). No new runtime deps; frontend is Django templates + ~60 lines of vanilla JS (R6). Built as four green sub-commits.

**What landed:**
- **Detail page** (`/film/<id>/`, `core/film/detail.html`): poster, metadata (year/runtime/genres/directors/cast), TMDB link when `tmdb_id` set, and **every user's** reviews/ratings with read-only stars (owner chose all-users over own-only). Absorbed-film ids resolve to the canonical row via `resolve_film_id` (§3.2).
- **Create/edit** (`/film/create/`, `/film/<id>/edit/`, `FilmForm`): manual films only — D4 locks TMDB-sourced metadata by redirecting edit (GET *and* POST) back to the detail page. Markdown description rendered to HTML at write time; comma-separated name-lists for genres/directors/cast; poster upload. `film_create` dedups on title+year via `find_match` (D7) before inserting.
- **Shelve/unshelve** (`POST /film/<id>/shelve/`, `/unshelve/`): model helpers `shelve_to_watchlist()` / `unshelve_from_watchlist()`. D1 mutual exclusion — a film already on the user's Watched shelf is refused for the watchlist (no unwatch in v0.1); `mark_watched` is the only path onto Watched and it removes from Watchlist. Idempotent + race-safe via `get_or_create`.
- **Finish flow** (`POST /film/<id>/watched/`): a modal on the detail page with a clickable half-star widget (0.5–5) + optional review. One form/endpoint serves both "Mark as watched" and "Edit review" — the latter pre-fills from the user's existing rating + raw markdown (D5); re-finishing updates the same row in place. `mark_watched_view` is thin: it hands rating + markdown to `mark_watched()` and surfaces a `ValueError` (missing/invalid rating) as a message with **no DB write**.
- **Supporting:** `core/utils.render_markdown` (mistune → bleach, allowlisted); `core/templatetags/film_tags.stars` (CSS-clipped read-only star fill); `core/static/js/reeltalk.js` (modal toggle + star widget); minimal neutral styles appended to `reeltalk.css` (R11).

**Gotchas hit & fixed:**
- **mistune appends a trailing newline** — stripped in `render_markdown` so stored HTML stays tidy.
- **DecimalField renders with 2 decimals** (`4.50`) — the modal's `data-value` uses `floatformat:"-1"` (→ `4.5`) and tests compare ratings numerically, not by string.
- **Merged-film id cleared after `merge_into`** — tests capture the old id before merging (the delete clears the pk).

**Test baseline:** before = 99 passed + … (suite had grown to 145 with the clean-room guard's per-file parametrization). After = **191 passed, 5 skipped**. New coverage: detail rendering/reviews/merge-resolution, create (fields+markdown, dedup, validation), edit (prefill, update, D4 lock, merge resolution), shelve/unshelve (helpers + views + mutual exclusion), finish flow (rating-only vs written review, watchlist removal, validate-before-write on missing/invalid rating, in-place re-finish, modal prefill). **Note:** the clean-room guard parametrizes over every project file, so each new file also adds a case — the pass count grows by (new tests + new files), not just new tests. ruff check + format green, `makemigrations --check` no drift.

**Site check:** dev web container recreated on the new image; entrypoint applied `core/0005` (`film.raw_description`) and re-collected static. The full owner-driven watchlist→watched→review loop is verified end-to-end in increment 7 (exit bar).

**Decisions:** R18–R22 in §4 below (engineering calls + the owner's UX picks from this session; recorded so the owner can veto).

### M1 increment 6 — user films page (3 tabs) + minimal home feed (executed 2026-09-07, commits `dbfc206`, `f68599e`)

Sixth increment of M1. The user's films page with exactly three tabs (§3.3 rule 1, D1) and the minimal home feed/timeline (§3.6/§3.7 v0.1). Views stay thin — all query/shelf logic lives in the model layer (`User.films_on_shelf` / `User.all_films`, `Status.feed_for`). No new runtime deps, no migrations (query API only); frontend is Django templates + neutral CSS appended to reeltalk.css (R11). Built as two green sub-commits.

**What landed:**
- **User films page** (`GET /user/<localname>/films/`, `social/user_films.html`): exactly three tabs — All films / Watchlist / Watched (§3.3 rule 1; D1's binary model leaves no room for more) — selected by a `?tab=` query param (default and unknown values fall back to all). Publicly readable. Rows show poster thumb (or placeholder), title + year linking to the film page, and the user's star rating where present. Header nav gains a "My films" link.
- **Query API on User** (`social/models.py`): `films_on_shelf(identifier)` — one shelf tab; `all_films()` — the §3.5/D10 relationship set (films on any of the user's shelves ∪ films carrying a non-deleted status), i.e. the same set the export will emit rows for; both annotated with `user_rating` via a D5-scoped subquery (one extra query, no N+1).
- **Minimal home feed** (`Status.feed_for`, `core/models.py`): the user's own non-deleted statuses plus those of followed users, newest first — §3.6's timeline for v0.1; groups join the union when they land (M5), blocks and feed filters are M4/M5 (R13). D3 stands: no automatic notes exist, so the feed shows reviews/ratings (and later comments). Home page renders it for logged-in users; anonymous visitors keep the landing.

**Gotchas hit & fixed:**
- **Home-page tests tripped the R12 wizard gate.** With no superuser in the test DB, `/` redirects to `/setup/`, so feed view tests got 302s and empty bodies — one assertion passed *spuriously* on the empty body. Fixed with an `admin` superuser fixture on all three home tests (same pattern as test_auth.py).

**Test baseline:** before = 191 passed + 5 skipped. After = **210 passed, 5 skipped** (17 new: shelf-tab scoping incl. cross-user isolation, D10 union + deleted-status exclusion + rating annotation, three-tab rendering, tab filtering, unknown-tab fallback, 404, rating display, film links, feed_for membership/ordering/deleted, home feed own+followed / stranger-exclusion / anonymous). The clean-room guard adds one case per new file (test_user_films.py + user_films.html), so the count grew by tests + files. ruff check + format green; no migrations this increment.

**Decisions:** R23 in §4 below (engineering call, recorded so the owner can veto).

### M1 increment 7 — admin basics + landing/about + exit-bar verification (executed 2026-09-08, commits `622c34f`, `75ad1fd`, `ad5c465`)

Seventh and final increment of M1. The §3.2 site-settings domain object (admin-managed, with the §3.7 signup policy gate and the link-domain allowlist), landing/about pages, the §3.7 v0.1 admin surface (branding + film merge/absorb tool), and the live end-to-end verification of the M1 exit bar. No new runtime deps; one migration (`social/0002`). Built as three green sub-commits plus this record.

**What landed:**
- **Site settings** (`social.models.SiteSettings`, singleton pk=1, R24): instance name/description (admin-managed plain text) + signup policy (`open`/`invite`). `/signup/` follows the policy once past the first-run wizard — `invite` means closed in v0.1 (an admin creates the account; the invite mechanism itself is a later step). Admin-registered.
- **Link-domain allowlist** (`social.models.LinkDomain`, R25): one row per allowed domain; `render_markdown` keeps an `<a href>` only when its host matches exactly or as a subdomain (case-insensitive, port/userinfo stripped, deny-by-default — an empty list strips every external link from user content, anchor text stays). Enforced at write time via bleach's per-tag attribute filter.
- **Landing/about** (§3.7 v0.1): the anonymous home is now the landing — site name + description (auto-escaped) over the fixed tagline and a signup CTA; logged-in users keep the feed under the same header. New `/about/` instance-info page (name, domain, software + version — new `reeltalk.__version__`, AGPL-3.0 note); every page links to it from the footer.
- **Admin basics** (§3.7 v0.1): site branding (ReelTalk administration / Site management) and the film merge/absorb tool at `/admin/films/merge/` (R26) — staff-only custom view (the ModelAdmin framework has no multi-object action), canonical + absorbed selection, batch runs `Film.merge_into` in one all-or-nothing transaction, form rejects self-merges. User management is the existing `UserAdmin`.

**Gotchas hit & fixed:**
- **Bleach attribute filters are per-tag, not per-attribute.** The nested form `attributes={"a": {"href": fn}}` was silently treated as a membership list (`"href" in {...}` → always allowed) — the filter never ran. Correct shape: `attributes={"a": fn}` with `fn(tag, attr, value)` returning None to strip. Verified against the installed bleach 6.4 source before relying on it.
- **`staff_member_required` redirects non-staff users (even logged-in ones) to the admin login** rather than raising PermissionDenied — tests assert 302 → `/admin/login/`, not 403.

**Exit bar (verified live over HTTP, 2026-09-08):** signed up a throwaway account on the running instance → created a film manually (Arrival, 2016) → added to Watchlist (control flipped to Remove; Watchlist tab listed it) → marked watched with a 4.5 rating + review text → film page showed `Reviews (1)` + Edit-review control, Watched tab listed the film, home feed carried the review. Also verified: login flow, anonymous landing (`/`), `/about/`, `/admin/login/` all 200; merge tool redirects anonymous users to the admin login. Verification data was removed afterwards — the instance is left with only the owner's `minnix` account and zero films/statuses, ready for the owner's own run.

**Test baseline:** before = 210 passed + 5 skipped. After = **241 passed, 5 skipped** (24 new tests: settings singleton/defaults, link-domain matching matrix, markdown link filtering, signup policy gate, landing/about rendering + escaping, merge-tool access control/validation/end state — plus 7 clean-room guard cases for the new files). ruff check + format green; `makemigrations --check` no drift; `social/0002` applied to the dev DB.

**Decisions:** R24–R26 in §4 below (engineering calls, recorded so the owner can veto).

### M2 — increment plan (forward-looking; ~4 increments across sessions)

M2 = "TMDB integration (first working version, part 2)" (PLAN.md §5): the TMDB client, global search as the primary add-film surface with click-through + one-click Watchlist (D6/D7), the suggest endpoint + dropdown, and the async backfill task (D11) — with the `worker` service (Django-Q2 on Postgres) joining the stack. Built as small verified increments; **each ends in a committed green checkpoint** with this file updated, then the session stops. Scope per increment:

1. ✅ **TMDB client** — `reeltalk.core.tmdb`: v3 `search_films` (paginated) / `get_film_details` (credits+images) / `download_poster` / `film_fields_from_tmdb`; `TmdbError` subtypes (auth 401 / rate-limit 429 / network); settings read `REELTALK_TMDB_API_KEY` (D8). *(done 2026-09-08, `1914534`.)*
2. ✅ **Create-or-match (D7) + backfill function** — find-or-create the local Film for a TMDB hit (tmdb_id → title+year fallback that backfills a manual film), plus the D11 backfill loop (fetch details + poster, fill empty fields, 0.25 s pacing, per-film skip-on-error). *(done 2026-09-08, `26756be`.)*
3. ✅ **Worker service** — `django-q2` dep, `Q_CLUSTER` (Postgres backend, no Redis), a `worker` compose service (same image, `qcluster`) that starts after web; the backfill task wrapper. Live check the worker comes up. *(done 2026-09-09, `c5c611f`.)*
4. ✅ **Search views + suggest + dropdown** — global search page (TMDB-backed with local fallback, D6), click-through create-or-match route, one-click Watchlist POST per row, `/search/suggest/` JSON endpoint + header dropdown JS. **Exit bar: live-verify "search Blade Runner → add to watchlist → mark watched with a rating"** (the TMDB key is now configured in `.env`). *(done 2026-09-09, `c2a76d7`; exit bar verified live against the real TMDB API.)*

### M2 increment 1 — TMDB client (executed 2026-09-08, commit `1914534`)

First increment of M2. The server-side TMDB v3 client in `reeltalk.core.tmdb` (R27) and the settings hook for the operator's key (D8). No model changes, no new runtime deps (`requests` was already in the audited set).

**What landed:**
- **Client** (`reeltalk/core/tmdb.py`): `search_films(query, page)` → one page of results with total counts; `get_film_details(tmdb_id)` (credits + images appended); `download_poster(path)` from the image CDN; `film_fields_from_tmdb(details)` mapping a detail payload onto Film field values. A `_get` helper centralizes HTTP + error mapping.
- **Error types (R27):** `TmdbError` base with `TmdbAuthError` (401 bad key), `TmdbRateLimitError` (429), `TmdbNetworkError` (network failure / unexpected status) — so a view can show a user-facing message per §3.4 instead of a stack trace.
- **Field mapping:** the TMDB overview is plain text — kept verbatim in `raw_description` and rendered to sanitized HTML for `description` via the existing `render_markdown`, so every description passes through one code path; cast capped at ten names (§3.4).
- **Settings:** `TMDB_API_KEY = env.str("REELTALK_TMDB_API_KEY", default="")` (D8); `is_configured()` helper. Unset key means search degrades to local-library search (D6) rather than erroring — that degradation logic lands with the search views (increment 4).

**Gotchas hit & fixed:**
- **Patching settings in tests.** `monkeypatch.setattr("reeltalk.settings.TMDB_API_KEY", …)` was a silent no-op: under the test harness the settings module resolves to two distinct objects, so Django's `LazySettings` (`settings._wrapped`) is not the `reeltalk.settings` module. Use `override_settings(…)` — it patches the wrapped module Django actually reads. (Production is unaffected: uvicorn loads settings once.)

**Test baseline:** before = 241 passed + 5 skipped. After = **258 passed, 5 skipped** (15 new client tests — config, search success/empty/401/429/network/500, details, poster download, field mapping incl. cast cap — plus 2 clean-room guard cases for the two new files). ruff check + format green; `makemigrations --check` no drift (no model changes).

**Decisions:** R27 in §4 below (engineering call, recorded so the owner can veto).

### M2 increment 2 — create-or-match + import backfill (executed 2026-09-08, commits `26756be`, `dd65b01`)

Second increment of M2. `reeltalk.core.catalog` is where the TMDB client meets the database — turning a TMDB film into a local `Film` row and keeping imported films current (R27 keeps the pure HTTP client separate). No model changes, no new runtime deps.

**What landed:**
- **`create_or_match_film(tmdb_id, *, title=None, year=None)`** (D7): (1) a row already carrying this `tmdb_id` is returned as-is with **no API call**; (2) otherwise the detail payload is fetched and a normalized title+year match against an existing manual film **backfills** that row's empty metadata + the `tmdb_id` instead of creating a duplicate; (3) with no match, a new `Film` is created from the details + poster. `title`/`year` are optional matching hints that only matter when the detail payload lacks one of them.
- **`backfill_films(film_ids)`** (D11): the import backfill loop — skips films without a `tmdb_id` or already complete (poster + description), fetches details + poster, fills empty fields, paces at `BACKFILL_REQUEST_INTERVAL = 0.25 s`, logs + skips per-film `TmdbError`s so the batch always runs to the end, and is a no-op without a key. Returns per-outcome counts. Plain + directly testable — the Django-Q2 task wrapper joins with the worker service (increment 3).
- **Housekeeping (`dd65b01`):** `.gitignore` now ignores runtime media under `images/` (MEDIA_ROOT) except the tracked `.gitkeep` — bind-mount test runs were dropping poster files into the source tree. Matches the existing `/static/`, `exports/`, `backups/` ignores.

**Gotchas hit & fixed:**
- **`responses` needs `@responses.activate` on every test that makes a call.** A `responses.add(...)` registration alone does not intercept — without the decorator the client made *real* TMDB calls (fake key → 401). Added it to all catalog tests. Also, `assert responses.calls == []` is unreliable (a `CallList` vs a plain list); use `len(responses.calls) == 0`.
- **Poster attachment in tests needs a real image.** `ImageField.save()` runs Pillow validation, so the mock poster bytes must be a valid JPEG — tests generate a tiny one with Pillow.

**Test baseline:** before = 258 passed + 5 skipped. After = **269 passed, 5 skipped** (9 new catalog tests — create-or-match existing/backfill/keeps-metadata/create-new, backfill no-key/no-tmdb_id/already-complete/fetches-fills/per-film-failure — plus 2 clean-room guard cases for the two new files). ruff check + format green; no model changes.

**Decisions:** none new (implements D7/D11 as specified; catalog placement is consistent with R27).

### M2 increment 3 — worker service (executed 2026-09-09, commit `c5c611f`)

Third increment of M2. The task queue lands per R4: **Django-Q2 on a Postgres cluster** replaces the deferred celery + django-celery-beat + redis broker in one package — no Redis service at any milestone. No model changes; the first (and only) real task is the D11 backfill wrapper.

**What landed:**
- **Dependency:** `django-q2==1.11.1` in `[project.dependencies]` (license-audited in PLAN.md §4.4 before the stack audit; its transitive dep `django-picklefield 3.4.0` is MIT — audited now, PLAN.md §4.5). `uv.lock` updated via a one-off container running uv 0.12.10 (same version as the Docker build stage; uv is not installed on the host).
- **Settings:** `"django_q"` in INSTALLED_APPS (its ORM cluster tables) + `Q_CLUSTER`: name `reeltalk`, workers 2, timeout 3600 (explicit — a full import backfill runs ~15 min under D11 pacing), retry 7200, orm `default`, backend `postgresql` (R28).
- **Task wrapper** (`reeltalk/core/tasks.py`): plain function `backfill_film_ids(film_ids)` delegating to `catalog.backfill_films` (logic stays in the directly-testable module), plus `enqueue_backfill()` which centralizes the by-name path + a `task_name="backfill"` display name. **django-q2 1.11 has no `@task` decorator** (the increment plan assumed one) — a plain importable function enqueued via `async_task("module.func", ...)` is the current pattern.
- **Compose:** a `worker` service — same image, `command: python manage.py qcluster`, `depends_on: web: condition: service_healthy`. The entrypoint only migrates/collectstatics when it starts uvicorn, so waiting for web-healthy guarantees the `django_q_*` tables exist before the worker's first poll.

**Gotchas hit & fixed (all 1.11 version specifics, verified against installed source):**
- **The warning condition is `timeout > retry`, and there is no `retry_timeout` key in 1.11.** `Q_CLUSTER["retry"]` (default 60) is the acknowledgement wait for delivery-guaranteed brokers; with timeout 3600 django-q warns "Retry and timeout are misconfigured" at every startup until `retry >= timeout`. Set `retry: 7200`; an earlier attempt with a non-existent `retry_timeout` key did not silence it.
- **Model/queue shape differs from older docs:** queued work is a signed package (`SignedPackage`, Django signing + pickle) in `django_q_ormq` (`OrmQ`); a `Task` row (success/stopped/result fields — no `status` column, no `return_value`) only appears when the task executes. Tests assert on the decoded `OrmQ` payload for enqueue and the `Task` row for execution.
- **`docker compose run web ... pytest` injects `.env` via `env_file`** — so the real TMDB key is present in the test environment (the image itself stays clean; `.dockerignore` excludes it). Any test that needs the unconfigured path must force it with `override_settings(TMDB_API_KEY="")`.

**Live check (dev stack, 2026-09-09):** `docker compose up -d` recreated web on the new image — entrypoint applied the django_q migrations (`django_q_ormq`, `django_q_schedule`, `django_q_task` present) and only then did worker start; logs show the cluster running (2 workers + monitor + guardian). Enqueued a no-op batch from the web shell (`enqueue_backfill([])`): the worker picked it up within seconds — `django_q_task` row `success=t`, `attempt_count=1`, result = all-zero summary. The stack is left running with all three services.

**Test baseline:** before = 269 passed + 5 skipped. After = **274 passed, 5 skipped** (3 new: wrapper delegation, enqueue → signed OrmQ package shape, sync-run name resolution + result recording — plus 2 clean-room guard cases for the two new files). ruff check + format green; `makemigrations --check` no drift.

**Decisions:** R28 in §4 below (engineering call, recorded so the owner can veto).

### M2 increment 4 — search surface + exit bar (executed 2026-09-09, commit `c2a76d7`)

Fourth and final increment of M2. The D6 search surface: global search as the primary add-film flow, click-through create-or-match, one-click Watchlist, and the header suggest dropdown (R29 for the shape, R30 for the local-search implementation). No model changes, no new runtime deps; frontend is Django templates + ~150 lines of vanilla JS (R6).

**What landed:**
- **`GET /search/`** (`core/search.html`, **login-gated since R80**): TMDB-backed when a key is configured — each row shows poster + title + year, links to the click-through route, and carries a one-click "Add to watchlist" button. A TMDB failure (bad key / 429 / network) degrades to local-library results with the user-facing error message instead of an error page; without a key it is local-only. Anonymous users used to see results without actions (§3.4); that is withdrawn — the route now redirects to login. Locally blocked films are excluded by `tmdb_id` (TMDB rows) or film id (local rows). TMDB pagination via `?page=`.
- **`GET /search/film/<tmdb_id>/`** (login-gated): runs D7 `create_or_match_film` and redirects to the film page; a success message only when a new row was created; TMDB errors surface as a message + redirect back to search.
- **`POST /search/watchlist/<tmdb_id>/`** (login-gated, JSON): materializes the hit via D7 then `shelve_to_watchlist`; returns `{"status": added|already}` or 409 `{"status": "watched"}` (D1 refusal), 502 + error payload on TMDB failure. The results-page button updates in place ("On watchlist" / "Already watched") via fetch with the CSRF token from a `<meta name="csrf-token">` tag added to `base.html`.
- **`GET /search/suggest/?q=`** (JSON, min 2 chars, **login-gated since R80**): feeds the header dropdown — TMDB when configured (falling back to local on failure/empty), local only without a key; capped at 8 rows. Anonymous callers get `401 {"error": "login required"}` rather than a redirect, because the dropdown parses JSON and a 302 would deliver it the login page's HTML; the guard sits ahead of the min-length check. (R29's anonymous rows pointing at `/search/?q=<title>` are withdrawn — see R80.) Dropdown JS (`core/static/js/search.js`) debounces at 200 ms and hides on Escape/outside-click; `initSuggest()` returns early when the header form is absent, which is how the anonymous case stays silent.
- **Local search** (`catalog.search_local`, R30): `ts_rank` over the trigger-maintained `search_vector` via a parameterized `plainto_tsquery('simple', %s)` annotation — no new fields, no migrations.
- **Manual create demoted (owner expectation):** "New film" is out of the top nav; the search page's empty state carries a low-key "Can't find it? Add a film manually." link for logged-in users. The existing `/film/create/` route and its form-page heading are unchanged (the one test asserting "New film" targets that page's h1, verified before removing the nav item).

**Exit bar (verified live over HTTP against the real TMDB API, 2026-09-09):** throwaway account via `/signup/` → `GET /search/?q=Blade+Runner` rendered 15 live TMDB rows with click-through links + watchlist buttons → click-through `/search/film/78/` fetched details + poster, created the film, redirected to its page → one-click `POST /search/watchlist/78/` returned `{"status": "added"}` and the Watchlist tab listed it → `POST /film/<id>/watched/` with a 4.5 rating + review text redirected back; the film page showed `Reviews (1)` with the text, the film moved to the Watched tab and off the Watchlist (D1) → the suggest endpoint served live TMDB JSON with click-through URLs. Verification data was removed afterwards — the instance is left with only the owner's `minnix` account and zero films, ready for the owner's own run.

**Test baseline:** before = 274 passed + 5 skipped. After = **303 passed, 5 skipped** (26 new: local search 4, results page 6 incl. anonymous/blocked/degradation/no-key, click-through 4, one-click watchlist 6, suggest 6 — plus 3 clean-room guard cases for the three new files). ruff check + format green; `makemigrations --check` no drift (no model changes).

**Decisions:** R29–R30 in §4 below (engineering calls, recorded so the owner can veto).

### Owner verification + post-M2 backlog (2026-09-09)

The owner exercised the exit bar in a browser the same day and confirmed it works. They then requested four improvements, recorded as **R31–R34** in §4: suggest-dropdown posters (R31), dropdown keyboard navigation (R32), feed entries for watchlist additions + watched-with-review (R33 — supersedes D3's "no automatic feed notes" for these events), and posters on feed rows (R34). **Owner decision: this backlog is done next, before M3.** The owner's test films from the browser run were disposable and have been deleted — the instance holds only the `minnix` account with zero films/shelf rows/statuses.

### Push to origin + contributor attribution fix (2026-09-09)

At the owner's request, all work up to this checkpoint was pushed to `origin/main`, and a wrong contributor attribution in four earlier M1 commit messages was fixed. Previous pushes had added a `Co-authored-by: Qwen-Coder <qwen-coder@alibabacloud.com>` trailer that GitHub renders as an unrelated random account (`github.com/qwencoder`); the owner chose to **remove the trailer entirely** (no replacement line) so those commits show only the repo identity.

How it was done, with safety nets: confirmed clean tree + single remote; backup tag `backup/pre-attribution-fix` created at the pre-rewrite tip (`bcc022c`); verified all four trailer-bearing M1 commits were already pushed (ancestors of old `origin/main`); `git filter-branch -f --msg-filter 'sed "/^Co-authored-by: Qwen-Coder/d"' c496e83^..HEAD` rewrote the nine commits in that range (only the four carried trailers; the rest changed hash by ancestry alone); verified zero remaining trailers on `main`, an empty `git diff --stat backup/pre-attribution-fix HEAD` (messages only — no content change), and equal commit counts (36 = 36); force-pushed with lease (`--force-with-lease`). `origin/main` now equals local `main` at `248156a`. The backup tag is kept until the owner confirms satisfaction, then can be deleted. **All commit hashes cited in this file were updated to their post-rewrite values** (M1 increments 1–6 are unchanged — they predate the rewritten range).

### Owner backlog R31–R34 (executed 2026-09-09, commits `5075b03`, `77bbb1a`, `339d7e5`, `68bce8c`)

The four owner-requested improvements from the post-M2 browser run, completed before M3 per the owner's 2026-09-09 decision. Built as four green increments; **no migrations, no new runtime deps** — R35 records the feed design call.

**What landed:**
- **R31 — suggest-dropdown posters.** `/search/suggest/` rows now carry `poster_url` (TMDB rows: the search result's CDN poster via `SearchResult.poster_url`; local rows: `film.poster.url`) and the dropdown JS renders a small thumbnail beside title/year with a placeholder span when missing. CSP needed no change — `img-src` already allows image.tmdb.org + self.
- **R32 — dropdown keyboard navigation.** Standard combobox in vanilla JS (R6): ArrowDown/ArrowUp move the active row (wrapping), Enter follows it (with none active, Enter still submits the search form for the typed query), Escape closes as before; mouse click unchanged, and hover syncs the keyboard highlight.
- **R33 — feed shelf events.** Home-feed entries now distinguish "X added Y to their Watchlist" from "X watched Y", the latter carrying the D5 review text + star rating (supersedes D3 for these two events). Per R35: new `feed_entries(user)` in core/models.py derives the two events from members' to-read/read `ShelfFilm` rows and unions them with `Status.feed_for` statuses, newest first; each user's live D5 review folds into their watched entry (a comment on a watched film stays its own row; a review without a Watched row — not reachable locally in v0.1, possible from M4 mirrors — stands alone). The home view now passes `feed_entries`; the template renders per kind.
- **R34 — posters on feed rows.** Film-anchored feed entries show the same 2.5rem poster thumb (or placeholder) as the user-films page.

**Live verification (2026-09-09, real TMDB API):** throwaway account over HTTP → `GET /search/suggest/?q=blade runner` returned live rows with real `poster_url`s → **headless Chromium drove the served search.js** against a stubbed suggest response: 10/10 checks passed (dropdown render, poster + placeholder, ArrowDown/ArrowUp incl. wrap-around, Enter follows the active row without submitting the form, Escape closes) → click-through created Blade Runner (poster downloaded from TMDB), marked watched with 4.5 + review text, one-click watchlist added a second film → home feed showed both event types with poster thumbs and the 4.5/5 star fill (`title="4.5 / 5"`). Verification data removed afterwards (user, films, shelf rows, statuses, orphaned poster files) — the instance holds only `minnix` with zero films again.

**Gotchas hit & fixed:**
- **The review-fold swallowed comments.** The first fold keyed on (user, film) alone, so a *comment* on a watched film was absorbed into the watched entry; restricted to `status.is_review` — caught by the new test suite before commit.
- **Test runs litter the shared media volume.** `docker compose run --rm web pytest` shares `media_volume`, so the suite's tiny test JPEGs accumulate in `/app/images/posters/`; and queryset `.delete()` on Film does not remove poster files (only instance-level delete does), which orphaned the two live-verification posters. Cleared the directory after verification; housekeep it occasionally — the volume is regenerable.

**Test baseline:** before = 303 passed + 5 skipped. After = **319 passed, 5 skipped** (+16: R31 suggest posters 2; R33 `feed_entries` 9 + home shelf-event rendering 3; R34 feed poster rows 2). ruff check + format green; `makemigrations --check` no drift (no model changes beyond the new `FeedEntry` dataclass — not a model, no migration).

### M3 — increment plan (executed 2026-09-09; all 3 increments in one session)

M3 = "File import/export" (PLAN.md §5): TMDB-CSV import (D9) + export round-trip (D10), wired to the async backfill. Built as small verified increments; **each ended in a committed green checkpoint** with this file updated. Scope per increment:

1. ✅ **Import core** — `reeltalk.core.import_export` (R36): canonical ten-column header + up-front validation, field parsers (release year; TMDB 1–10 → nearest half-star), ID-stub find-or-match in D7 order with **no API calls**, per-row outcomes (unrated → Watchlist; rated → Watched + rating-only entry; existing reviews never touched), single-transaction import with per-row results + summary, D11 backfill queued on commit via `enqueue_backfill` (R28). *(done 2026-09-09, `89e3b6c`.)*
2. ✅ **Export core** — `export_film_csv`: the §3.5/D10 relationship set (`User.all_films`) as canonical ten columns in deterministic order; Your Rating = most recent rated review ×2, Date Rated in UTC; review text drops out by design. *(done 2026-09-09, `680b96e`.)*
3. ✅ **Surface** — `/preferences/import/` (upload → per-row results table + summary) and `/preferences/export/` (page → CSV download), nav links, minimal results-table CSS (R11). *(done 2026-09-09, `5983749`.)*

### M3 increment 1 — import core (executed 2026-09-09, commit `89e3b6c`)

First increment of M3. `reeltalk.core.import_export` is the directly-testable home for the D9 import (views join in increment 3); the TMDB client and backfill stay where they are (R27/R28). No model changes, no new runtime deps, no migrations.

**What landed:**
- **Header validation up front (D9):** `TMDB_CSV_HEADER` — the canonical ten columns (TMDB's own export shape); `parse_tmdb_csv` rejects a file missing any column with a message naming them (column order is not significant) and enforces the 20,000-row cap. `TmdbCsvError` is the user-facing rejection type.
- **Field parsers:** `parse_release_year` (a TMDB date down to its year); `parse_tmdb_rating` (1–10 "Your Rating" → nearest half-star — round to the whole point, then halve, in Decimal, so 7 → 3.5; empty / non-numeric / out-of-range read as unrated).
- **ID-stub find-or-match (D9: no API calls during import):** `find_or_create_film_stub` runs D7's order through the existing `Film.find_match` (tmdb_id → imdb_id → normalized title+year); a miss creates an ID stub (title/year/ids only — no metadata invented) for the D11 backfill; a match backfills empty tmdb_id/imdb_id/year. A non-numeric TMDb ID is treated as absent rather than aborting the transaction (R36).
- **Per-row outcomes:** `import_row` — non-movie rows and missing names skipped with a note; unrated → `shelve_to_watchlist` (D1's refusal surfaces as "already in your Watched list"); rated → existing-review guard first (an existing review is never touched), then Watched shelf + off-Watchlist (D1) + rating-only `Status`.
- **Transactional import:** `import_film_csv` — one transaction, per-row results (line numbers start at 2 — the header is line 1) + summary counts; the D11 backfill for every imported film id (created *and* matched, so a re-import heals stale stubs) is queued on commit via `enqueue_backfill` when a key is configured.

**Gotchas hit & fixed:**
- **Django 6.1 moved `captureOnCommitCallbacks`.** It is no longer in `django.test.utils` — it's a `TestCase` classmethod (`TestCase.captureOnCommitCallbacks(execute=True)`). Verified against the installed source before relying on it.
- **CSV column names carry spaces.** A test helper that mapped underscore kwargs straight into row dicts silently wrote `"TMDb_ID"` keys instead of `"TMDb ID"` — several tests passed *spuriously* via the title+year fallback until the mapping was made explicit (`_COLUMN_KEYS` in the test module).

**Test baseline:** before = 319 passed + 5 skipped. After = **358 passed, 5 skipped** (37 new: header validation 3, year parser 6, rating parser 11, stub find-or-match 5, per-row outcomes 9, transaction/summary/backfill-queue 3 — plus 2 clean-room guard cases for the two new files). ruff check + format green; no model changes.

### M3 increment 2 — export core (executed 2026-09-09, commit `680b96e`)

Second increment of M3. `export_film_csv` in the same module — the D10 side of the round-trip. No model changes, no new deps.

**What landed:**
- **`export_film_csv(user)`** (§3.5/D10): one row per film with any relationship (`User.all_films` — shelved ∪ reviewed ∪ commented), canonical ten columns: TMDb + IMDb IDs from the film, `Type` = "movie", Release Date = stored year as `YYYY-01-01T00:00:00Z`, Your Rating = most recent rated review ×2 (D5 allows one live review per user per film — a single query keeps it N+1-free), Date Rated from that review's published date in UTC; season/episode/community-rating columns empty. Review text drops out by design — the format carries ratings, not reviews.
- **Deterministic row order** (sort title, year, id) so repeated exports are byte-identical (R36 — legacy iterated a set).

**Test baseline:** before = 358 passed + 5 skipped. After = **365 passed, 5 skipped** (7 new: byte-exact unrated row, relationship-set scoping, rated row ×2 + UTC date, review-without-rating exports unrated, soft-deleted review exclusion, deterministic order, and the round-trip no-op on a mixed v0.1-reachable state). ruff check + format green; no model changes.

### M3 increment 3 — surface (executed 2026-09-09, commit `5983749`)

Third and final increment of M3. The §3.5 routes and templates; views stay thin (upload decode + validation only — all import/export logic lives in `import_export`). No model changes, no new deps.

**What landed:**
- **`GET/POST /preferences/import/`** (`core/import_films.html`, login-gated, local users only): upload form → utf-8-sig decode (TMDB exports carry a BOM) → header/row-cap validation → `import_film_csv`; the results page renders the per-row table (line/name/status/note) + summary counts and keeps the form for repeat imports (the re-import no-op is an owner-facing check). Rejections (no file / not UTF-8 / bad header / over cap) come back as messages on the upload page.
- **`GET/POST /preferences/export/`** (`core/export_films.html`): page with a download button; POST streams `export_film_csv` as an attached `reeltalk-export.csv`.
- **Nav:** "Import" + "Export" links beside "My films" for logged-in users; minimal `table.results` styles appended to reeltalk.css (R11).

**Test baseline:** before = 365 passed + 5 skipped. After = **377 passed, 5 skipped** (10 new: login gate, upload form, no-file error, bad header, non-UTF-8, row cap, BOM strip, results page + backfill queue over the test client, export page + CSV download, non-local-user redirect — plus 2 clean-room guard cases for the two templates). ruff check + format green; no model changes.

The instance now holds the owner's 1,378-film watchlist as ID stubs (plus their two pre-existing films); the D11 backfill (≈6 min at 0.25 s pacing) runs on the worker to fill metadata + posters — a second, mostly-already-complete batch from the re-import follows it in the queue.

### Known bugs — owner-reported 2026-09-10 (all fixed 2026-09-10)

Three bugs from the first real use of M3 (the owner imported a 1,378-movie watchlist). **All three fixed** — bug 1's poster fix + data recovery landed as `89542cb` with its cleanup in `7cb5f2b`; bug 2 in `9a16351`; bug 3 in `4a863af` (design recorded as R37). Execution record + live verification below.

**Bug 1 — all watchlist posters showed broken icons. (Fixed 2026-09-10.)**
Root cause: the `worker` service had **no `media_volume` mount** — the D11 backfill downloaded posters into the worker's *ephemeral* container layer (`/app/images/posters/`), so web (the only thing serving `/images/...`) could never see them. Only the two films added via search worked (web saved those itself). Aggravating factor: `Q_CLUSTER workers=2` ran the import backfill and the re-import backfill **in parallel, in lockstep** — ~1,276 posters were double-downloaded and Django's `get_available_name` gave them suffixed names (`tmdb-<id>_XXXXX.jpg`); some DB rows reference those suffixed names.
Recovery (done): tar-piped the worker-local `posters/` dir into the shared volume *before* recreating the worker; verified all 1,374 distinct DB-referenced poster paths exist on disk and serve 200. Fix: `media_volume:/app/images` mounted on the worker (compose change, `89542cb`).
**Remaining cleanup — done 2026-09-10 (`7cb5f2b`):** (a) pytest now runs with a private throwaway `MEDIA_ROOT` (`/tmp/reeltalk-test-media` via the pyproject pytest-env list) — verified the shared volume gains zero files across a full suite run; (b) deleted **1,332** unreferenced files from the volume — an exact diff of on-disk vs DB-referenced paths (zero referenced files missing): 1,314 double-download losers (`tmdb-*`, incl. suffixed `get_available_name` names) + 18 test JPEGs (`dune*`, `p*`); the earlier "~40" count had missed the double-download losers; backup at `/home/minnix/backups/reeltalk/unreferenced-media-20260910.tar.gz`; the volume now holds exactly the 1,374 DB-referenced posters + `.gitkeep`; (c) decision: **`workers: 1`** (R38) — serialized batches make a re-import's second full ID list nearly free (`backfill_films` skips already-complete films without an API call) instead of double-downloading every poster in lockstep.

**Bug 2 — the watchlist loads all ~1,380 films at once and crashes the browser. (Fixed 2026-09-10, `9a16351`.)**
`user_films` (`reeltalk/social/views.py`) rendered the whole tab queryset and `social/user_films.html` looped it all — no pagination anywhere. Fixed as planned: Django `Paginator` in the view (50/page; non-integer `?page=` → page 1, out-of-range → 404), template prev/next controls that preserve `?tab=`, all three tabs; the films querysets gained a deterministic `order_by(sort_title, year, id)` so pages are stable.

**Bug 3 — a file import pollutes the feed with one entry per film (1,378 "added to Watchlist" events). (Fixed 2026-09-10, `4a863af`, design R37.)**
R35's read-side derivation made one feed entry per `ShelfFilm` row, so a bulk import produced thousands of near-identical entries seconds apart. Fixed as the planned direction: read-side grouping in `feed_entries` (`reeltalk/core/models.py`) — consecutive same-user/same-shelf events within `FEED_BULK_WINDOW` (5 min, chosen from live data: the real 1,378-row import spanned 6.5 s) collapse into one entry showing the newest film + "and N other films". Edge cases decided in R37: watched events aggregate the same way; rating-only statuses of aggregated films are absorbed (no duplicate rows); a group containing a live written review stays individual so the review text is not lost. Rejected (as planned): writing a single "bulk" status at import time — contradicts R35's no-write model and D5's index.

### M3 bug fixes — owner-reported 2026-09-10 (executed 2026-09-10)

Three bugs from the owner's first real use of M3 (the 1,378-movie import). Built as three green increments; **each ended in a committed checkpoint** with the full gate (`ruff check` + `ruff format --check` + pytest in-container) green. No migrations (query/view/template/config only).

**Bug 1 cleanup — private test MEDIA_ROOT + volume junk + worker count (`7cb5f2b`).** The poster fix itself landed as `89542cb`; this finished its remaining cleanup:
- pyproject's pytest-env list gains `MEDIA_ROOT = /tmp/reeltalk-test-media` — the suite's fake posters no longer touch the shared volume (verified: a full run leaves the volume file count unchanged at 1,375).
- Deleted **1,332** unreferenced files from `media_volume` — an exact diff of on-disk files vs DB-referenced `core_film.poster` / `social_user.avatar` (zero referenced files missing): 1,314 double-download losers from the parallel-backfill race (`tmdb-*`, incl. suffixed `get_available_name` names) + 18 test JPEGs (`dune*`, `p*`). The earlier "~40 test JPEGs" count had missed the double-download losers. Backup: `/home/minnix/backups/reeltalk/unreferenced-media-20260910.tar.gz` (106M). Volume now holds exactly the 1,374 referenced posters + `.gitkeep`.
- `Q_CLUSTER workers: 2 → 1` (**R38**); worker recreated on the new image — cluster log shows one work process.

**Bug 2 — user films pagination (`9a16351`).** `user_films` now paginates with Django's `Paginator` (50/page; non-integer `?page=` → page 1, out-of-range → 404) and the template carries prev/next controls that preserve `?tab=` (the "all" tab has no query param). The films querysets gained a deterministic `order_by(sort_title, year, id)` so pages are stable. `.search-pager` renamed `.pager` (shared by search + films pages).

**Bug 3 — bulk-shelf feed aggregation (**R37**, `4a863af`).** `feed_entries` groups consecutive same-user/same-shelf `ShelfFilm` events within `FEED_BULK_WINDOW` (5 min) into one entry — "added Y and N other films to their watchlist", showing the newest film, dated at the newest row. Window chosen from live data: the owner's 1,378-row import spanned **6.5 s** (p99 row gap ~6 ms), while deliberate manual adds are minutes-to-hours apart; chaining means each row only needs to be within the window of the previous one, so a slow import still aggregates whole. Edge cases decided in R37: watched events aggregate the same way; rating-only statuses of aggregated films are absorbed (no duplicate feed rows — ratings stay visible on film/user-films pages); a group containing a live **written** review stays individual so the review text is not lost.

**Live verification against the real data (:3030, 2026-09-10):**
- **Posters:** 25/25 random DB-referenced poster paths serve 200; the volume holds exactly the 1,374 referenced posters + `.gitkeep`.
- **Pagination:** `/user/minnix/films/?tab=watchlist` → "Page 1 of 28" (1,379 films) with next `?tab=watchlist&page=2`; page 2 has prev+next; last page (28) is prev-only; the watched tab (1 film) shows no pager; the all tab paginates with paramless links.
- **Feed:** `feed_entries(minnix)` returns **3 entries** (was 1,380): the import as one aggregated entry + Blade Runner watched (review folded in) + one earlier manual add kept separate. Over HTTP via a throwaway follower account: "added Bluegrass Country Soul (1972) **and 1377 other films** to their Watchlist" rendered once; throwaway user removed afterwards, `minnix` is the only account again.

**Test baseline:** before = 377 passed + 5 skipped. After = **388 passed, 5 skipped** (+5 pagination, +6 R37 aggregation). ruff check + format green.

### M4 — increment plan (forward-looking; ~7 increments across sessions)

M4 = "federation (ActivityPub from spec)" (PLAN.md §5). Built against the W3C ActivityPub REC + RFC 9421 with **no AP library** (R7); new app `reeltalk.activitypub` per R9. Federation targets: ReelTalk instances + current Mastodon (owner decision 2026-09-10 — see R39). Scope per increment:

1. ✅ **Keys + HTTP signatures** — `reeltalk.activitypub`: Ed25519 keypair generation/parsing (`crypto.py`) and RFC 9421 sign + dual-format verify (`signatures.py`); PEM key fields on `User` (local users get a pair at creation; remote mirrors public-only) + migration; `cryptography` dependency (PLAN.md §4.6). Wire-format decision recorded as R39. *(done 2026-09-10, `d77efef`.)*
2. ✅ **Person wire type + webfinger + nodeinfo** — Person document serializer for local users (publicKey id = actor URL + `#main-key`), `/.well-known/webfinger`, `/.well-known/nodeinfo`; the actor-URL convention everything else hangs off. *(done 2026-09-10, `7bf5b22`; R40.)*
3. ✅ **Outbox/collections + Film wire type** — outbox/followers/following OrderedCollections with AP pagination, per-user + shared inbox routes (POST stub), Film (D15) + Note object serializers using the day-one `origin_id`/`remote_id` identity fields, content-negotiated `/film/<id>/`, and the `origin_id` backfill. *(done 2026-09-11, `b5047ba`; R41.)*
4. ✅ **Inbox core + remote fetch + mirrors** — signed delivery of activities into the inbox; remote-user mirror creation on first contact (public key fetched from their Person doc); graceful ignore of unknown activity/object types. *(done 2026-09-11, `178cb00`; R42.)*
5. ✅ **Follow/unfollow** — Follow/Undo(Follow) handling both directions, follower state feeding the existing feed query. *(done 2026-09-11, `5260894`; R43.)*
6. ✅ **Status broadcast + shelf events** — Create/Update/Delete of reviews and shelve events delivered to followers' inboxes; idempotent processing keyed by origin ids. *(done 2026-09-12, `55bb386`; R44.)*
