7. ✅ **Exit-bar verification** — two local ReelTalk instances follow each other and exchange reviews + film objects; a Mastodon-style third party can follow a ReelTalk Person without breaking (PLAN.md §5). *(done 2026-09-12, `a612174`; topology recorded as R45.)*

### M4 increment 1 (executed 2026-09-10, commit `d77efef`)

**What landed:**
- New app `reeltalk.activitypub` (R9) — no models of its own yet; `crypto.py` (Ed25519 keypair generation + PEM loading, thin wrapper over the audited `cryptography` package, PLAN.md §4.6) and `signatures.py` (RFC 9421 sign/verify + old-draft-format verify).
- `User.private_key` / `User.public_key` PEM TextFields (migration `0003`); local users get a key pair generated at creation in `save()`; remote mirrors will carry the public key only.
- **Outgoing** = RFC 9421 + Ed25519 covering `@method` + `@target-uri` (+ `content-digest` for bodies). **Incoming** accepts both live formats (R39): a `Signature-Input` header → RFC 9421 (Ed25519 keys); a bare `Signature` with `keyId/algorithm/headers/signature` → old draft format (RSA, rsa-sha256/hs2019 only) — what current Mastodon still sends. Verification reconstructs the sender's base from the actual request; any malformation or mismatch returns False ("reject this activity"), never raises.
- Tests: 39 in `reeltalk/tests/test_activitypub.py` (parser, sign shape, both verify paths incl. tamper cases) + 3 user-keypair tests in `test_user.py`.

**Gotchas hit:**
- The W3C ActivityPub REC specifies **no** signature scheme; the field has two live formats. The first implementation followed draft-cavage details (colon-without-space, trailing LF) that match neither real-world format — rewrote after checking what current Mastodon actually sends and verifies (Linzer/RFC 9421 for Ed25519-capable verification; old format RSA-only).
- RFC 9421 grammar: the parentheses wrap **only** the component list — signature parameters follow the closing paren — and `created` is a bare integer, so the parameter parser needs a quoted-string-or-token alternation.
- Django's `HttpRequest.headers` **caches on first access** — tamper tests must read/overwrite through `META` before any `.headers` touch, or they silently verify against the original headers (bit five tests; commented in the test file).
- The clean-room guard flagged "BookWyrm" in a docstring — it scans every project file and only the attribution docs are whitelisted; reworded to "no other legacy server's RSA format".

**Test baseline:** before = 388 passed + 5 skipped. After = **430 passed, 5 skipped** (+39 AP, +3 user keypair). ruff check + format green; `makemigrations --check` clean (migration 0003 committed).

### M4 increment 2 — Person wire type + webfinger + nodeinfo (executed 2026-09-10, commit `7bf5b22`)

**What landed:**
- **Actor-URL convention (R40)** — a local user's actor is `/user/<localname>/`: content negotiation serves the Person JSON-LD document (`application/activity+json`) to AP clients and 302s browsers to the films page. Collection paths are fixed now even though their routes land in increments 3–4: `/user/<localname>/inbox|outbox|followers|following/` + shared instance inbox `/inbox/`. The actor and films routes share one localname regex (R12 charset) — the old `<str>` converter rejected dots, which are legal localnames.
- **Person wire type** (`reeltalk/activitypub/identity.py`, built fresh against the spec per R7): `@context` (ActivityStreams + security/v1), id/type/preferredUsername/name/url, inbox/outbox/followers/following, `endpoints.sharedInbox`, and `publicKey` with **id = actor URL + `#main-key`** — the same keyid outgoing signatures carry (R39). Empty summary/image are omitted. Document URLs are built from the request: host as sent, scheme follows `X-Forwarded-Proto` when present (D14 — the same rule as R39's signature verification).
- **Discovery endpoints** (`reeltalk/activitypub/views.py` + `urls.py`, all GET/unauthenticated): `/.well-known/webfinger` (RFC 6454 JRD — subject/aliases/lrdd/profile-page/self; case-insensitive localname match, domain must be this instance's, stored spelling carried back in the subject), `/.well-known/nodeinfo` → `/nodeinfo/2.0` (software `reeltalk` + version, protocol `activitypub`, `openRegistration` mirrors the signup policy).
- **Key backfill** — `User.ensure_keypair()` (idempotent; local users only, remote mirrors untouched) + data migration `social/0004`: accounts created before the key fields had none and could not sign federation requests. The entrypoint migrates on every web start, so it ran automatically with no manual step — verified live: the `minnix` account got a working pair (owner note 2026-09-10: that account is test-only/disposable, safe to reuse for later two-instance testing).
- Tests: 26 in `reeltalk/tests/test_activitypub_identity.py` — Person document shape/omissions/image, content negotiation incl. Mastodon's real Accept header and bare-`*/*` rejection, forwarded-proto scheme, case-insensitivity, remote-mirror 404, webfinger resolve + malformed matrix, nodeinfo both documents + policy follow-through, ensure_keypair paths.

**Gotchas hit:**
- The test client and RequestFactory set no `Host` header in META — building document URLs from `request.headers["Host"]` produced `http:///...` in tests (10 failures on the first run). Use `request.get_host()` instead (reads the Host header, falls back to the server name; production behavior unchanged).
- Ruff's isort orders relative imports alphabetically within their own section (`absolute_uri` sorts before `accepts_activitypub`).

**Live verification (:3030, 2026-09-10):** webfinger resolves `acct:minnix@localhost` → actor with self/profile-page/lrdd links; nodeinfo index + 2.0 document correct (`openRegistration: true`); `GET /user/minnix/` with AP accept → 200 `application/activity+json` Person whose `publicKeyPem` matches the DB key and whose id carries `#main-key`; browser accept → 302 to the films page (serves 200). Migration 0004 applied at web start; `minnix` now has both keys in the live DB.

**Test baseline:** before = 430 passed + 5 skipped. After = **461 passed, 5 skipped** (+26 identity tests, +5 clean-room guard cases for the new files). ruff check + format green (verified on the built image); `makemigrations --check` clean (migration 0004 committed).

### M4 increment 3 — outbox/collections + Film wire type (executed 2026-09-11, commit `b5047ba`)

Third increment of M4. The read-side federation surface: the collection endpoints R40 reserved (outbox/followers/following as paginated OrderedCollections, per-user + shared inbox routes), the **Film** (D15) and **Note** object wire types, content-negotiated serving of a film object at its id URL, and the day-one `origin_id` identity backfill. No new runtime deps; one data migration (`core/0006`).

**What landed:**
- **Collection infrastructure** (`reeltalk/activitypub/collections.py`, R41): `OrderedCollection` + `OrderedCollectionPage` builders with ActivityPub pagination — a collection URL served without `?page` returns the collection doc (`totalItems` + `first`/`last` page URLs); with `?page=N` it returns one page (`partOf` + 1-based `startIndex` + that slice's `items`). `PAGE_SIZE = 20`. Reused by outbox, followers, and following.
- **Object wire types** (`reeltalk/activitypub/objects.py`, R41; Film per D15): `film_document` (the custom **Film** type — `name`/`subtitle`/`summary`/`year`/`runtime`/`genres`/`directors`/`cast`/`image` + `tmdbId`/`imdbId` for D7 dedup; empty fields omitted) and `note_document` (statuses — `content`/`publishedTime`/`editedTime`/`attributedTo`/`rating`/`film` ref/`inReplyTo`; one `Note` type on the wire, mapped back by shape). `create_activity` wraps a Note in a `Create` for the outbox (stable id keyed on the status origin identity; object rides inline). Object ids use the day-one origin fields: a local object's wire id is its `origin_id` (else pk) — `/film/<id>/`, `/status/<id>/`.
- **Endpoints** (`reeltalk/activitypub/views.py` + `urls.py`): `GET /user/<localname>/outbox/` (the user's non-deleted local statuses as Create(Note), newest first, paginated), `GET .../followers/` and `.../following/` (OrderedCollections of Person docs from the follow M2Ms — local users only; empty until follows land in increment 5), and the inboxes: `GET /user/<localname>/inbox/` + `GET /inbox/` → 405, `POST` on both → **202 Accepted stub** (the route exists so the Person doc's inbox URLs never 404 per R40; real handling — signature check, dedup, remote mirrors — is increment 4). Inbox handlers are `@csrf_exempt` (AP deliveries carry no CSRF token).
- **Content-negotiated Film serving** (`reeltalk/core/views.py`): `film_detail` now serves the **Film** JSON-LD document to ActivityPub clients (same Accept split as the actor endpoint, R40) and keeps the HTML page for browsers — so a remote instance can fetch a referenced film object by its id URL.
- **Day-one `origin_id` identity** (`core/0006` + model `save()`): a locally created object's `origin_id` is its pk. Data migration backfills the pre-existing rows (all 1,380 live films + local statuses) via a single `F("id")` UPDATE each; `Film.save`/`Status.save` set it on create (queryset `.update`, so `auto_now` `updated_date` is untouched). Remote mirrors (increment 4) carry `remote_id` instead and are left alone.

**Gotchas hit:**
- **Typed statuses must be film-anchored (R17)** — a test helper that created "review" statuses without a film raised the model's validation; standalone notes use `status_type=None`.
- **Django's `HttpResponse` has no `allow=` kwarg** — a 405's `Allow` header is set via `response["Allow"] = "POST"`.
- **`django.utils.timezone` shadows the stdlib `timezone`** (no `.utc`) — use `from datetime import UTC` for RFC 3339 conversion.

**Live verification (:3030, 2026-09-11):** web recreated on the new image; entrypoint applied `core/0006` — all 1,380 live films backfilled (`origin_id` = pk). Over HTTP: `/user/minnix/outbox/` → OrderedCollection (`totalItems: 1`, correct `first`/`last`); `?page=1` → a `Create` whose Note has `id: /status/5/`, `film: /film/8/` (Blade Runner), `rating: 5.0`; `/film/8/` with AP Accept → Film doc (`id: /film/8/`, `type: Film`, year 1982) and with a browser Accept → 200 HTML; `/user/minnix/followers/` → empty OrderedCollection; `/inbox/` GET → 405, POST → 202.

**Test baseline:** before = 461 passed + 5 skipped. After = **506 passed, 5 skipped** (+41 collection/wire-type tests, +4 clean-room guard cases for the new files). ruff check + format green (verified on the built image); `makemigrations --check` clean (migration 0006 committed).

### M4 increment 4 — inbox core + remote fetch + mirrors (executed 2026-09-11, commit `178cb00`)

Fourth increment of M4. The inbound delivery pipeline: the inbox POST routes are no longer stubs — they resolve the sender from the signature's keyid, verify the signature in either live wire format (R39), dedup by activity wire id, and dispatch by type. Remote-user mirrors are created on first contact by fetching the sender's Person document. No new runtime deps; two migrations (`social/0005`, `activitypub/0001` — the app's first model).

**What landed:**
- **Inbox pipeline** (`reeltalk/activitypub/views.py`): `_handle_inbox_post` runs extract keyid → resolve sender (a first-contact remote user is mirrored from their Person document as part of this) → verify signature against the resolved user's public key → parse JSON → process. Status codes: **401** for a missing, unresolvable, or invalid signature (including an unreachable sender — verified before anything is parsed or recorded), **400** for a signed but unparseable body, **202 Accepted** for everything else (handled, gracefully ignored, or duplicate — the outcome is not exposed to the sender). GET stays 405 + `Allow: POST`; the per-user inbox still 404s for unknown local users. Both inboxes process identically — dedup makes it harmless when a remote posts to both.
- **Remote fetch + mirrors** (`reeltalk/activitypub/mirrors.py`): `fetch_person_document` (public GET with AP Accept + `reeltalk/<version>` User-Agent; scheme restricted to http/https; typed `RemoteFetchError` on 404 / non-2xx / non-JSON / wrong type / no `publicKeyPem`), `mirror_user_from_person` (create-only, keyed on the document's id URL), and `resolve_sender` (keyid → strip `#main-key` fragment → the local user when the netloc matches the request's Host header and the path is R40's `/user/<localname>/` — no self-fetch; otherwise mirror lookup by `actor_url`, else fetch + create on first contact).
- **Mirror identity** (`social/0005`, R42): new `User.actor_url` (the home Person URL; partial unique index for non-local users) and `localname` widened 30→255 — mirrors are stored as `<preferredUsername>@<netloc>`, which can exceed 30 chars and can never collide with a local account (`@` is not in R12's charset, so the namespaces are disjoint). The `username` property now returns the @-qualified handle for remotes. Mirrors carry display_name + public key only (no summary/avatar in v0.1 — M5 profile work can refresh them); no document re-fetch on later deliveries and no key rotation (R42).
- **Dedup + dispatch** (`reeltalk/activitypub/inbox.py` + `activitypub/0001`): `DeliveredActivity` (unique activity wire id) is the app's first model; `process_inbound_activity` records the id and dispatches in one transaction — a handler failure rolls the record back too, so the sender's retry is not blocked by its own failed delivery. Increment 4 registers **no** handlers (Follow/Undo land in increment 5; Create/Update/Delete in increment 6) — every well-formed activity is accepted, recorded, and otherwise ignored: spec-correct for unsupported types (§3.6), and it keeps redelivery idempotent. `inbox.HANDLERS` is where increments 5/6 register.
- **`extract_key_id`** (`signatures.py`): the keyid from either wire format (RFC 9421 `keyid` / old-draft `keyId`); None → reject.

**Gotchas hit:**
- **Django 6.1's `RequestFactory.post` defaults to a multipart content type** — raw bytes bodies get handed to `encode_multipart` and raise `AttributeError: 'bytes' object has no attribute 'items'`; tests must name the content type explicitly.
- **`responses` raises whatever exception instance you pass as `body=`** — it must be a `requests` exception subclass or the client's `except requests.RequestException` won't catch it (a builtin `ConnectionError` escaped and failed the test).

**Live verification (:3030, 2026-09-11):** web recreated on the new image; entrypoint applied `social/0005` + `activitypub/0001`. A throwaway "remote instance" (a Person document with its own Ed25519 keypair served on 127.0.0.1:8099 inside the web container) delivered over real HTTP to `/inbox/`: first delivery → **202** and the mirror `carol@localhost:8099` created from the fetched document (public key only, no shelves, empty private key); redelivery of the same activity id → 202 with still one dedup row; an unknown type (`Announce` carrying a `Book` object) → 202, recorded, no content created. Unsigned POST → 401; GET → 405 + `Allow: POST`. Verification data removed afterwards (the instance holds only `minnix` again).

**Test baseline:** before = 506 passed + 5 skipped. After = **548 passed, 5 skipped** (+35 inbox/mirror tests, +7 clean-room guard cases for the new files). ruff check + format green (verified on the built image); `makemigrations --check` clean (migrations `social/0005` + `activitypub/0001` committed).

**Decisions:** R42 in §4 below (engineering call, recorded so the owner can veto).

### M4 increment 5 — follow/unfollow both directions (executed 2026-09-11, commit `5260894`)

Fifth increment of M4. The Follow/Undo(Follow) relationship in both directions: inbound handlers record a remote user's follow/unfollow of one of ours from the verified sender; outbound functions record a local user's follow/unfollow of a remote user and deliver a signed activity to their inbox. The follower state feeds the existing feed query (no change to it — it already reads `user.follows`), and the followers/following collections now carry remote mirrors. No new runtime deps; one migration (`social/0006`).

**What landed:**
- **Inbound handlers** (`reeltalk/activitypub/follow.py`, registered in `inbox.HANDLERS`): `handle_follow` records `sender follows <object>` and `handle_undo` removes it when the Undo's object is a Follow (an Undo of any other type, or an unresolvable object, is ignored gracefully — §3.6). Both attribute the relationship to the **verified sender** resolved from the signature keyid, never the activity's self-declared `actor` (which an attacker could forge; the signature is the authority — a forged-actor test pins this). The "followed" actor is resolved among users this instance already knows (`mirrors.resolve_known_actor`: local by R40 path on this host, remote by existing mirror) — no first-contact fetch as a side effect of processing a follow.
- **Pipeline threading** (`reeltalk/activitypub/inbox.py`): `process_inbound_activity(activity, sender, request)` now passes the verified sender + request to handlers (increment 4's single-arg form is gone); `HANDLERS` registers `Follow` + `Undo`. Dedup-by-wire-id and the one-transaction rollback are unchanged.
- **Outbound delivery** (`reeltalk/activitypub/delivery.py`): `deliver_activity(inbox_url, activity, private_pem, key_id)` signs per RFC 9421/Ed25519 (R39, via `signatures.sign_request`) and POSTs — a single synchronous send with no retry queue in v0.1. Reused by increment 6 for status broadcasts.
- **Outbound follow/unfollow** (`reeltalk/activitypub/follow.py`): `follow_user(request, local_follower, followed_actor_url)` records the M2M and delivers a signed Follow; `unfollow_user(...)` does the Undo(Follow) — a no-op (returns None) when not already following, so it never fetches a mirror or sends a spurious Undo. The follower must be local (it signs with its own key; a mirror has none). Activity ids are unique per event (uuid fragment) so a follow → unfollow → re-follow does not collide and get deduped as a redelivery on the receiving side.
- **Mirror inbox** (`social/0006`, R43): new `User.inbox_url` stores the home inbox advertised by the Person document, populated at mirror creation in `mirrors.mirror_user_from_person` (R42 create-only — no refresh). Delivery falls back to the `<actor_url>/inbox` path convention (holds for ReelTalk + Mastodon) when a document advertised none.
- **Collections** (`reeltalk/activitypub/views.py`): `_person_collection` now serves `related.all()` (not just `local=True`) — local users as full Person documents, remote mirrors as a minimal Person document whose id is the mirror's home actor URL (R42). So a local user's followers/following reflect both local and remote relationships.

**Gotchas hit:**
- **Attribute follows to the verified sender, not `actor`.** The first instinct was to read `activity["actor"]`; that is forgeable. The pipeline already resolves + verifies the sender from the keyid, so the handler takes the sender as the actor of record — a delivery signed by carol with `"actor": mallory` still records carol's follow (pinned by test).
- **Re-follow id collision.** A deterministic Follow id keyed on (follower, followed) would make a re-follow dedup as a redelivery of the first on the receiving instance. Fixed with a uuid fragment per event (test asserts 100 unique ids over 50 follow/undo pairs).
- **`RequestFactory` scheme for the signature round-trip test.** Verifying an outbound delivery against our own inbox check requires reconstructing the request's target-uri exactly as signed; setting `X-Forwarded-Proto` + `Host` in META (rather than assigning `request.scheme`) reproduces it.

**Live verification (:3030, 2026-09-11):** web recreated on the new image; entrypoint applied `social/0006` (`inbox_url` column present). Over HTTP: `/` 200; `/user/minnix/` with AP Accept → Person doc (id + type correct); `/user/minnix/followers/` → empty OrderedCollection. (Full two-instance follow interop is increment 7's exit bar.)

**Test baseline:** before = 548 passed + 5 skipped. After = **567 passed, 5 skipped** (+15 follow tests, +4 clean-room guard cases for the new files). ruff check + format green (verified on the built image); `makemigrations --check` clean (migration `social/0006` committed).

**Decisions:** R43 in §4 below (engineering call, recorded so the owner can veto).

### M4 increment 6 — status broadcast + shelf events (executed 2026-09-12, commit `55bb386`)

Sixth increment of M4. The social loop now closes across instances: a local user's review create/edit/delete and Watchlist add/remove are delivered as signed Create/Update/Delete activities to each remote follower's home inbox, and the receiving side processes them idempotently — mirroring the remote Note/Film objects (and their authors) keyed by origin ids so redelivery never double-applies. One migration (`core/0007`).

**What landed:**
- **Remote object identity** (`core/0007`, R44): `Film.remote_url` + `Status.remote_url` — the home wire URL as received (R42's deferred note: an integer `remote_id` alone can't reconstruct a host, so mirrors persist the full URL) with partial unique indexes on both models (`remote_url != ""`) making mirror creation idempotent. A film mirror is simply a row whose `remote_url` is set (Film has no `local` flag); the origin backfill in `Film.save` now skips mirrors — a mirror claims no local origin.
- **Wire types** (`reeltalk/activitypub/objects.py`): `film_url()` is mirror-aware (a mirror's home URL, else the R41 origin-id URL). New builders: `update_activity` (uuid activity id per edit, stable object id), `delete_activity` (stable id — a status is deleted at most once in v0.1; the tombstone keeps its identity so the Note rides inline), and the custom **ShelfEvent** type: object id stable per (user, film, shelf) while the Create/Delete activity ids are uuid-per-event, so an unshelve-then-re-shelve is not deduped away on the receiving side.
- **Inbound handlers** (`reeltalk/activitypub/statuses.py`, registered in `inbox.HANDLERS` as `Create`/`Update`/`Delete`): everything attributes to the **verified sender** (never `attributedTo` — a forged-attribution test pins this). Note mirroring maps the single Note type back by shape (rating ⇒ Review/ReviewRating, content-without-rating ⇒ Comment, no film ref ⇒ standalone) and mirrors the author's Watched shelf row for reviews (the R35 feed renders "watched" with stars). Film references resolve local-by-path on this host, mirror-by-`remote_url`, else fetch-and-mirror; before creating a film mirror, D7 `find_match` runs so the same movie never gets two rows. Shelf events apply to the sender's shelf (get_or_create / delete) for known identifiers only. Delete soft-deletes the Note by `remote_url`; Film deletions are ignored gracefully in v0.1 (rows are PROTECTed). An unresolvable film reference raises `RemoteObjectError` → the whole transaction rolls back **including the dedup row**, so a retry can succeed — never silently dropped.
- **Outbound broadcast** (`reeltalk/activitypub/broadcast.py`): `broadcast_status_create/update/delete` + `broadcast_shelf_event` deliver to each *remote* follower's home inbox (local followers read local rows; no delivery). Signed with the author's key via `delivery.deliver_activity`; a per-follower `requests.RequestException` is dropped — v0.1 has no retry queue and a dead follower must not fail the user's request. The local write commits before the broadcast runs, so a failed send can only lag the remote copy.
- **View wiring** (`reeltalk/core/views.py`): mark-watched broadcasts Create (or Update on re-finish) plus a ShelfEvent Delete when the film was on the Watchlist (D1); shelve/unshelve and the one-click search-watchlist broadcast ShelfEvent Create/Delete. File import deliberately broadcasts nothing (§3.5 — importing your own data is not a social act).
- **The `/status/<id>/` route** (R41's deferred item): serves the Note document to AP clients only (content-negotiated; 404 for browsers, tombstones, and mirrors — a mirror's canonical id is its home instance's URL). `delivery.inbox_for()` promoted from follow.py's private helper (the advertised `inbox_url`, else `<actor_url>/inbox` with no trailing slash per R43); `follow.py` now imports it.

**Gotchas hit:**
- **`responses` 0.26.3 clears its call list on context-manager exit.** `RequestsMock.__exit__` runs `stop(allow_assert=True)` and then `reset()`, so any `rsps.calls` assertion after the `with` block inspects an empty list — ten outbound tests failed with "Not all requests have been executed" / 0-calls assertions even though delivery worked (proven by printing calls *inside* the block). The project convention is the `@responses.activate` decorator, which keeps the mock active for the whole test body; converted all affected tests to it.
- **The inbox fallback has no trailing slash.** `inbox_for()`'s `<actor_url>/inbox` fallback (used when a Person doc advertised no inbox) produces `.../user/carol/inbox`; test constants with a trailing `/` never matched the registered mock URL. The R43 convention stands — real Person docs always advertise the exact inbox anyway.
- **Dedup is layered, and each layer keys differently** — activity wire id (`DeliveredActivity`), object `remote_url` (partial unique indexes), ShelfFilm row per (sender, film, shelf identifier). A second *activity* for the same object (an out-of-order Update after a Create) must still apply: object-level get_or_create handles it, and a test asserts one row.

**Live verification (:3030, 2026-09-12):** web recreated on the new image; entrypoint applied `core/0007`. Over HTTP: `/` 200; `/status/<id>/` with AP Accept → Note document (id = `http://localhost:3030/status/<id>/`, attributedTo the minnix actor, rating + film ref present); same URL without AP Accept → 404.

**Test baseline:** before = 567 passed + 5 skipped. After = **603 passed, 5 skipped** (+32 status/shelf tests, +4 clean-room guard cases for the new files). ruff check + format green (verified on the built image); `makemigrations --check` clean (migration `core/0007` committed).

**Decisions:** R44 in §4 below (engineering call, recorded so the owner can veto).

### M4 increment 7 — exit-bar verification (executed 2026-09-12, commit `a612174`)

Seventh and final increment of M4. No new features — the live end-to-end verification of the M4 exit bar (PLAN.md §5): **two local ReelTalk instances follow each other and exchange reviews + film objects; a Mastodon-style third party can follow a ReelTalk Person without breaking.** There is no follow UI yet (M5), so outbound follows were driven at the library level (`follow_user` with a request pinned to the canonical cross-instance host); everything else ran through the real views over HTTP.

**Two-instance topology (R45):** instance B = this repo as compose project `reeltalk-b` (`docker compose -p reeltalk-b --env-file .env.b up -d web db`; `.env.b` self-declares `ENV_FILE=.env.b`, and docker-compose.yml's `env_file:` is now `${ENV_FILE:-.env}` — the one repo change of this increment, plus gitignoring the secrets-bearing env file). B has its own named volumes (`reeltalk-b_*`; A's `pgdata` untouched), port 3031, DOMAIN=`reeltalk-b.test` (host `/etc/hosts` → 127.0.0.1; no worker on B, no TMDB key — verification films are manual). All cross-instance and live-verification traffic goes over the published ports via the host LAN IP (`http://192.168.1.138:3030` / `:3031`): each container's localhost is its own and compose projects have separate networks, so a routable address is required; using one canonical address per instance keeps every absolute actor/film/status URL consistent — mirror identity is keyed on the exact home URL, so mixed hosts would fragment identities. A stays DOMAIN=`localhost` (webfinger matches the acct-URI domain against `settings.DOMAIN`, not the request host, so IP-based discovery works).

**Verified live (all over real HTTP between the two running instances):**
1. **Discovery:** webfinger resolved `acct:fedb@reeltalk-b.test` on B and `acct:minnix@localhost` on A; Person docs served to AP Accept (publicKey id = actor URL + `#main-key`, home inbox advertised).
2. **Mutual follows:** minnix@A → fedb@B via `follow_user` (signed RFC 9421/Ed25519 Follow POST to B's per-user inbox): B resolved the sender from the keyid, first-contact mirrored minnix from A's Person doc (public key only + home inbox), verified the signature, and recorded the follow **from the verified sender**. Reverse direction identical — B reused the existing mirror (no re-fetch). Both sides' followers/following collections then served each other's Person docs keyed on the home actor URL.
3. **Review exchange A→B:** minnix shelved Arrival (2016) (ShelfEvent Create broadcast) and marked it watched with 4.5 + text (Note Create + ShelfEvent Delete): B matched the film reference to its own pre-existing manual Arrival row via D7 `find_match` (title+year — **no duplicate mirror; film count unchanged**), mirrored the Note attributed to the verified sender (`remote_url` = home status URL, rating/content carried), converged the shelves to Watched-only (the to-read add + removal), and fedb's feed query + home page rendered "minnix@192.168.1.138:3030 watched Arrival (2016)" with 4.5 stars.
4. **Review exchange B→A:** fedb marked Interstellar (2014) watched with 4 + text: A fetched the remote Film doc and created a film mirror (`remote_url` = home URL; year/runtime/directors/description carried), mirrored the Note onto fedb's mirror + Watched shelf row; minnix's feed rendered it.
5. **Out-of-order Update:** minnix re-finished the Blade Runner review (Update broadcast) — B had never seen that status and created the mirror from the Update alone (`edited_date` set), fetching + mirroring the referenced film (`tmdb_id` 78 carried for future D7 matches).
6. **Mastodon-style third party:** a throwaway stub inside A's web container (RSA keypair, Person doc on 127.0.0.1:8099, capture inbox) sent an old-draft-format signed Follow (`rsa-sha256`, `(request-target) host date digest` — what current Mastodon still sends): accepted with 202, mirror created from the fetched Person doc, follow recorded, followers collection updated. A subsequent broadcast from minnix was delivered to the stub's advertised inbox; the captured delivery's signature **verified independently** (raw `cryptography` Ed25519 over the RFC 9421 base — no reeltalk code), proving a third party can authenticate our outgoing deliveries. Stub artifacts removed afterwards.

**Gotchas hit:**
- **`docker compose --env-file` feeds interpolation only, not container env.** The hardcoded `env_file: .env` meant instance B booted with A's environment (DOMAIN=`localhost` → webfinger 404 for its own domain). Fixed by parameterizing to `${ENV_FILE:-.env}`; B's `pgdata` had to be reset because it was initialized with A's password before the fix.
- **The stub's first Follow got a 401:** it signed `(request-target)` as the full URL, but the legacy component is `method SP path` (`post /inbox/`) — what the verifier reconstructs from `request.path` (and what Mastodon signs). Stub bug, not server bug; the unit tests already pinned the correct shape.
- **RFC 9530 Content-Digest is colon-wrapped on both ends** (`sha-256=:…=:`) — the throwaway verifier's digest assertion initially dropped the trailing colon.

**Verification state kept (R45):** B stays up as a standing federation peer for M5 (the follow UI needs two instances); the mutual follows, mirrors, exchanged reviews, and fixture films remain on both. A holds `minnix` + the fedb mirror + Arrival/Interstellar rows; B holds `fedb` + the minnix mirror + Arrival/Blade Runner/Interstellar rows. The disposable `minnix` account's password was reset for this run (`Fedtest-2026-minnix`); B's admin is `fedb` / `Peertest-3031-fedb`.

**Test baseline:** no test changes — **603 passed, 5 skipped** unchanged (the clean-room guard's `SKIP_FILES` gained `.env.b`, keeping the count at baseline). ruff check + format green; `makemigrations --check` clean (no model changes).

**Decisions:** R45 in §4 below (engineering call, recorded so the owner can veto).

### M4 follow-up — film poster federation (executed 2026-09-13, commit `c519d87`)

Owner-reported bug from manual two-instance testing: "although I can see the review and post coming from the other instance, the movie poster is blank." Root cause: `_mirror_film` copied every metadata field of a remote Film document except `image` — the R44 limit ("skip poster download") was never revisited, so film mirrors arrived without posters while the Note mirrors rendered fine. (The owner's other three reported symptoms were diagnosed as non-code issues and closed: logouts = browser cookies shared across ports on one hostname; B search dead = `.env.b` carries no TMDB key by design; A search rows not clickable = anonymous rendering per R29, resolved once the cookie collision was worked around.)

**What landed (R46):**
- `_mirror_film` now calls a new best-effort `_attach_remote_poster(film, doc["image"])` **only for a freshly created mirror** — existing mirrors stay create-only (R42) and D7-matched local rows are never backfilled with a federated poster.
- The download is defensive: http/https only, 10 MiB cap (Content-Length pre-check + capped streamed read), Pillow `verify()` before storing, storage name = sanitized URL basename (generated fallback). **Any failure — scheme, network, non-2xx, oversized, non-image payload — logs a warning and skips the poster; the mirror row and the whole delivery are never failed by it** (contrast with the film-document fetch, which still raises `RemoteObjectError` so an unanchored review stays retryable).
- No model changes, no migrations.

**Verified live (both directions, over real HTTP between the R45 instances after rebuilding both images — note each compose project builds its own image: `reeltalk-web` vs `reeltalk-b-web`):**
1. **A→B:** minnix marked Hellraiser (2022) watched with 4.5 + text on A → B created the film mirror (`remote_url` = `http://192.168.1.138:3030/film/974/`, `tmdb_id` carried) **and downloaded the 57 KB poster from A's media volume**; the file serves at B's `/images/posters/…` (200, image/jpeg), the film page renders it, and fedb's feed row shows "watched Hellraiser (2022)" with the poster thumb — the owner's exact symptom, fixed.
2. **B→A:** fedb created a fixture film on B with an uploaded poster ("Poster Reverse Test" 2026) and reviewed it → A mirrored the film and downloaded the poster from B (original filename preserved); served + rendered on A.
3. **No backfill:** mirrors created before this fix (Interstellar on A, Blade Runner/Alien Nation on B) correctly remain poster-less — create-only posture holds.

**Verification state kept:** both fixture reviews/films remain on both instances (Hellraiser review A→B; Poster Reverse Test film + review B→A). The `Poster Reverse Test` film is a throwaway fixture with a generated placeholder image; delete it via the merge/absorb tool or leave it — it is inert.

**Test baseline:** before = 603 passed + 5 skipped. After = **610 passed, 5 skipped** (+7: poster downloaded + byte-identical + fetched once per redelivery; review-driven film fetch brings the poster; 404/non-image/oversized/unsupported-scheme all skip without losing the mirror; D7 match never requests the poster). ruff check + format green (verified on the built image); `makemigrations --check` clean.

**Decisions:** R46 in §4 below (engineering call, recorded so the owner can veto).

### M5 — increment plan (forward-looking; ~4 increments across sessions)

M5 scope for this phase (owner-set 2026-09-13): the UI layer on top of M4's federation library functions — follow/unfollow button, remote-user discovery (webfinger-based), human profile page, blocking + feed filters (R13), and the review-delete surface (`broadcast_status_delete` is library-only until there's a UI). The wider PLAN.md §5 M5 list (lists, groups, notifications, directory, RSS, moderation, 2FA) is out of scope for this phase. Built as small verified increments; **each ends in a committed green checkpoint** with this file updated, then the session stops.

1. ✅ **Human profile page + profile editing** — `/user/<localname>/` serves the profile (avatar, name/handle, bio, films link) to browsers while AP clients keep the Person document (R47); route pattern extended for mirror localnames; profile editing at `/preferences/profile/` with `User.raw_summary` (R48); lazy throttled mirror profile refresh (R49). *(done 2026-09-13, `3f6078b`.)*
2. ✅ **Follow/unfollow UI + remote-user discovery** — follow button on the profile page (local target → M2M only; remote → existing `follow_user`/`unfollow_user`); `/find/` page resolving `user@domain` via webfinger → redirect to their profile. *(done 2026-09-13, `a036dd9`, R50–R52 — incl. the owner-approved `DOMAIN` switch to the canonical IP:port; live two-instance verification passed both directions, throwaway state cleaned up.)*
3. ✅ **Blocking + feed filters (R13)** — block/unblock user (profile page) + film (film page); `feed_entries` honors blocks; film page hides blocked users' reviews. *(done 2026-09-14, `476a695`/`6df2a2a`/`3f3e760`, R53–R56.)*
4. ✅ **Review delete surface** — delete button on the user's own review → soft-delete + `broadcast_status_delete`. *(done 2026-09-14, `8247115`, R57; live-verified two-instance.)*

### M5 increment 1 — human profile page + profile editing (executed 2026-09-13, commit `3f6078b`)

First increment of M5. The actor URL gets a human side: browsers now get a real profile page instead of R40's redirect to the films page, AP clients keep the Person document by content negotiation, and remote mirrors become profileable (their `<preferredUsername>@<netloc>` localname is routable). Profile editing gives avatar/bio a write path — Letterboxd-parity `/edit/profile` shape (owner sign-off 2026-09-13), which also gives federation something visible to exchange across instances.

**What landed:**
- **Profile page** (`social/views.user_profile`, `social/profile.html`; R47): the route moves from `activitypub/urls.py` to the root URLconf (name `user-profile`) so it matches before the AP include and owns the bare `/user/<localname>/`. Content negotiation unchanged for locals (Person JSON-LD for explicit AP media types; a bare `*/*` gets HTML). **AP clients get 404 on a mirror profile** — a mirror's canonical document lives on its home instance (R42), so this instance never serves a second identity for it. The route pattern is a new `PROFILE_LOCALNAME_RE` (R40's charset plus `@` and `:` — the netloc carries the port when non-default); the **films route uses it too**, fixing mirror films pages that previously 404'd (the old pattern rejected `@`). Local lookup stays case-insensitive (R40), mirrors match verbatim (disjoint namespaces). Header nav: the username is now a link to the user's own profile.
- **Profile editing** (`/preferences/profile/`, `ProfileForm`; R48): login-gated, local users only (a mirror gets a message + redirect — its home instance owns it). Display name + markdown bio + avatar upload; the bio renders through the existing `render_markdown` (LinkDomain allowlist applies) at write time with the source kept in new `User.raw_summary` (R18's raw-source pattern — migration `social/0007`) so a later edit pre-fills markdown, not markup. No avatar removal in v0.1.
- **Lazy mirror profile refresh** (`mirrors.refresh_mirror_profile`; R49): viewing a remote profile whose mirror lacks an avatar or bio fetches the home Person document once per hour (LocMemCache throttle per actor URL — a passive page view cannot become an outbound-fetch loop) and fills whatever is missing: display name, summary (sanitized through a new `core.utils.sanitize_html` sharing `render_markdown`'s allowlist — remote summaries arrive as HTML), avatar (defensive download). **Identity fields are never touched** — no key rotation, no inbox re-derivation (R42); a rotated key in the document is ignored (pinned by test). Any failure is silent; the page renders with what we have.
- **Shared image download:** R46's poster download logic moved to `mirrors.fetch_image_bytes` (scheme check, Content-Length pre-check + capped streamed read, Pillow `verify`) with `image_storage_name` alongside; `_attach_remote_poster` now delegates to it (no behavior change — the M4 poster tests pass untouched). Avatars cap at 2 MiB.
- **Tests:** 17 in `reeltalk/tests/test_profile.py` (page identity/avatar/placeholder/case-insensitivity/edit-button-scoping/404s; mirror page incl. raw + percent-encoded localname forms and the films route; refresh fill/throttle/skip-when-complete/failure-silent/key-untouched; edit login-gate/prefill/save/no-avatar-replace). Two M4 identity tests updated: browser Accept now expects the profile page (200 HTML) instead of the 302.

**Gotchas hit & fixed:**
- **`login_required` lives in `django.contrib.auth.decorators`**, not `django.contrib.auth` — the first in-container run died at import.
- **Bleach strips the href, keeps the anchor text (R25).** A bio link to a disallowed domain leaves its URL as plain text — a test asserting the URL's absence failed; the correct assertion is "no live `<a href=` remains".
- **`get_available_name` suffixes colliding media names** across test runs sharing `/tmp/reeltalk-test-media` — assert on `avatar.url`, not a literal filename.

**Live check (:3030, 2026-09-13):** web recreated on the new image (entrypoint applied `social/0007`); anonymous `GET /user/minnix/` → 200 profile with `@minnix@localhost` + avatar placeholder; same URL with AP Accept → Person document (id/key shape unchanged).

**Test baseline:** before = 610 passed + 5 skipped. After = **630 passed, 5 skipped** (+17 profile tests, +3 clean-room guard cases for the new files). ruff check + format green; `makemigrations --check` clean (migration `social/0007` committed).

**Decisions:** R47–R49 in §4 below (engineering calls + owner sign-off on profile editing, recorded so the owner can veto).

### M5 increment 2 — follow/unfollow UI + remote-user discovery (executed 2026-09-13, commit `a036dd9`)

Second increment of M5: the follow relationship gets its human surface (the button on the profile page) and unknown remote users become discoverable (`/find/` over webfinger). Per R52 (owner-approved 2026-09-13) both instances' `DOMAIN` switched to the canonical IP:port so discovery works end-to-end in both directions over real HTTP.

**What landed:**
- **Follow/unfollow button** (`social/views._change_follow` + `user_follow`/`user_unfollow`; R50): right-aligned `.profile-actions` slot in the profile head — "Edit profile" for self, Follow/Following POST form for other authenticated viewers; hidden on one's own profile and for anonymous visitors. Local target → plain `User.follows` M2M add/remove (no delivery — same instance); remote target → the existing signed `follow_user`/`unfollow_user` delivery (R43). POST routes `/user/<localname>/follow|unfollow/` share `PROFILE_LOCALNAME_RE` so mirror handles match; GET → 405, login-gated, redirect back with a message. Only resolvable profiles can be followed — an unknown handle 404s (discovery creates an unknown remote's mirror first). A down home instance at delivery time records the local state and shows a warning instead of 500'ing (R44's broadcast posture; no retry queue in v0.1).
- **Remote-user discovery** (`social/views.find_user` + `social/find.html`; R51): login-gated single input `user@domain`. Same-domain handle (case-insensitive vs `settings.DOMAIN`) → iexact local resolution → profile redirect, no network. Other domains → webfinger client (`mirrors.webfinger_actor_url`): explicit non-default port → plain http at that port; otherwise https first with http fallback; a reachable 404 is definitive "no such user". The self link's actor URL → `ensure_mirror` (existing mirror by actor_url, else created from the Person doc on first contact — R42) → redirect to the mirror's profile. **Resolve-and-redirect only: submitting never auto-follows** — one POST changes at most one relationship. Failures re-render the form with a message and keep the submitted value. "Find people" added to the header nav.
- **DOMAIN switch** (R52, amends R45's topology note): `.env` → `DOMAIN=192.168.1.138:3030`, `.env.b` → `DOMAIN=192.168.1.138:3031`. No stored data changes (DOMAIN is never persisted in rows — username property, webfinger subject, about page compute it at request time; existing mirrors keep their R45 canonical actor URLs built from the Host header). Settings made port-tolerant: new `DOMAIN_HOST` feeds the ALLOWED_HOSTS default + DEFAULT_FROM_EMAIL; BASE_URL appends WEB_PORT only when DOMAIN has no explicit port.
- **Tests:** 15 in `reeltalk/tests/test_follow_ui.py` (button visibility matrix; local M2M without delivery; remote follow/unfollow deliver the right signed activity with no refetch of an existing mirror; unfollow-when-not-following is a no-op; unknown remote 404s; down instance records locally + warning; 405/login/404 route behavior) and 14 in `reeltalk/tests/test_find_user.py` (login gate, form, malformed inputs; same-domain resolution case-insensitive without network; remote creates mirror + redirects with no auto-follow; existing mirror not refetched; 404/unreachable/no-self-link errors; the scheme rule — explicit port plain http, no-port https-first + http fallback).

**Gotchas hit & fixed:**
- **Follow route for an unknown remote initially expected to 302** — design corrected: only resolvable profiles can be followed (the button renders only there; `/find/` creates unknown remotes' mirrors first), so an unresolvable handle is a 404. The real reachable failure is delivery to a *down* home instance (the M2M is recorded before delivery) → warning message instead of a 500, matching R44's broadcast posture.
- **`reverse()` does not percent-encode `@`/`:` in paths** (legal sub-delims) — test Location assertions use the unencoded form.
- **Django messages HTML-escape apostrophes** ("couldn&#x27;t") — the down-instance warning test asserts on a substring that avoids the escaped character.

**Live verification (both instances up, real HTTP via `curl -4`, 2026-09-13):**
- **Webfinger under the new DOMAIN:** both instances serve JRD with subject `acct:<user>@192.168.1.138:303x` and self links to the canonical actor URLs.
- **(a) Existing minnix↔fedb mutual follow:** profile button shows Following → unfollow POST → signed Undo delivered over real HTTP (B's followers collection 1→0, A's following 1→0, button flips to Follow) → re-follow → both sides restored (A following = 1; B's followers list minnix).
- **(b) Fresh discovery, both directions:** throwaway `throwb` created on B via `/signup/`; from A's `/find/` `throwb@192.168.1.138:3031` → webfinger over real HTTP → mirror with canonical `actor_url` `http://192.168.1.138:3031/user/throwb/` (verified in A's DB; **no auto-follow** — the follow M2M table held only the two minnix↔fedb rows) → follow from the profile (delivered; B's followers collection lists minnix) → throwb creates a film on B + posts a review → it appears in minnix's feed on A (film title, review text, author handle). Symmetric: `throwa` on A discovered from B's `/find/` (`throwa@192.168.1.138:3030`), fedb follows from the profile, throwa posts a review on A → appears in fedb's feed on B.
- **Cleanup:** all throwaway users + their films/reviews/mirrors/follow rows removed from both instances in one transaction each (dependency order: follow rows at *both* endpoints — an inbound Follow records an M2M row on the receiving instance too — then statuses, shelffilms, films, shelves, user; `Status.user` and `Shelf.user` are PROTECT). The minnix↔fedb mutual-follow state was kept on both sides; post-cleanup smoke check green (profile button + feeds on both instances).

**Test baseline:** before = 630 passed + 5 skipped. After = **662 passed, 5 skipped** (+29 tests, +3 clean-room guard cases for the new files). ruff check + format green; `makemigrations --check` clean (no migrations in this increment).

**Decisions:** R50–R52 in §4 below.

### M5 increment 3 — blocking + feed filters (executed 2026-09-14, commits `476a695`, `6df2a2a`, `3f3e760`)

Third increment of M5: the block relationship gets its human surface and the read-side filtering it implies. Per R13 the model already carried `User.blocks` (M2M self, `related_name="blocked_by"`) and `User.blocked_films` (M2M `core.Film`) from M1 — **no migration was needed** (verified: `makemigrations --check` clean). Built as three green sub-commits; **no migrations, no new runtime deps**.

**What landed:**
- **Feed membership honors blocks (R54, `476a695`).** New `User.feed_member_ids()` is the single source of the home-feed rule — self plus followed users, minus blocked users. Both `Status.feed_for` and `feed_entries` read it, so blocking a user removes their statuses **and** shelf events alike from the blocker's feed (previously each computed "self + followed" independently). Following a blocked user stays allowed (R50) but their content stays hidden while the block stands.
- **Block/unblock user on the profile page (R53, `6df2a2a`).** A Block / Blocked control joins the follow button in `.profile-actions` (hidden on one's own profile and for anonymous visitors). POST routes `/user/<localname>/block|unblock/` share the profile's extended localname pattern (R47) so mirror handles match; GET → 405, login-gated, redirect back with a message. **Blocking is read-side state only**: a plain `User.blocks` M2M change — there is no Block activity delivered over federation in v0.1 (accepted limit), so a remote target is handled exactly like a local one (nothing sent to the home instance). Only resolvable profiles can be blocked (an unknown handle 404s, mirroring follow).
- **Block/unblock film on the film page + review hiding (R55/R56, `3f3e760`).** A Block / Blocked control joins the film page's `.film-actions` row; POST routes `/film/<id>/block|unblock/` toggle the local-only `User.blocked_films` M2M. In v0.1 a blocked film's read-side effect is exclusion from search (already wired in M2 via `_blocked_tmdb_ids`/`_local_film_ids`) — no new feed/films-page hiding for films (that would be a separate feed-filter concern, out of scope here). The film page now **hides the reviews of users the viewer has blocked** (`film_detail` excludes `user_id__in=blocked` for authenticated viewers; anonymous visitors still see every review — blocking is per-logged-in-user state).

**Live verification (instance A over real HTTP as `minnix`, 2026-09-14):** throwaway `blocktest` created with a watched film + review, `minnix` following it. Over HTTP: profile page showed the Block button → block POST 302 → **the review disappeared from both the home feed and the film page** and the button flipped to "Blocked" → unblock POST 302 → the review reappeared on both surfaces and the button flipped back. Film block POST 302 → "Blocked" button + the `blocked_films` M2M row confirmed in the DB. CSRF note for future live runs: the `{% csrf_token %}` form field is a *masked* token that must pair with the exact `csrftoken` cookie — posting the **raw cookie value** as `csrfmiddlewaretoken` (double-submit) is the reliable curl path. Throwaway state removed afterwards in dependency order (follow rows → statuses → shelffilms → films → shelves → user); the minnix↔fedb mutual follow was kept (verified: `minnix.follows = [fedb@192.168.1.138:3031]`, blocks + blocked_films empty).

**Test baseline:** before = 662 passed + 5 skipped. After = **689 passed, 5 skipped** (+27: 5 feed-membership block tests in test_user_films.py, 13 user-block UI tests in the new test_block_ui.py, 8 film-block/review-hiding tests in test_film_views.py, +1 clean-room guard case for test_block_ui.py). ruff check + format green (verified on the built image); `makemigrations --check` clean (no migrations — the block relations predate this increment per R10/R13).

**Decisions:** R53–R56 in §4 below.

### M5 increment 4 — review-delete surface (executed 2026-09-14, commit `8247115`)

Fourth and final increment of the M5 phase: the user's own review gets a delete control on the film page, wired to the library-only pieces that landed in M1 (soft-delete) and M4 increment 6 (`broadcast_status_delete`). **No migrations, no new runtime deps** — `Status.delete()` (the §3.2 tombstone) and `broadcast_status_delete` already existed; this increment gives them a route + button.

**What landed:**
- **Delete button on the film page's review list (R57, `core/film/detail.html`).** A subtle "Delete" form sits right-aligned in each review's `.review-head` row, rendered **only** for an authenticated viewer who is the author of that review (`review.user_id == user.id`). It is hidden for other users' reviews and for anonymous visitors. (A reviewer's own status is always `local=True` — mirrors can't authenticate — so no separate local check is needed in the template; the route enforces it anyway.)
- **Route + view (`POST /status/<id>/delete/`, `core/views.delete_review`).** Login-gated, POST-only (GET → 405). The lookup is scoped to `user=request.user, local=True, deleted=False` — so another user's review, a remote mirror, or an already-deleted tombstone all **404** (author-only is the security boundary; no cross-user deletion, no deleting mirrors, no double-delete). On success it calls `Status.delete()` (soft-delete: `deleted=True`, content + raw_content cleared, `deleted_date` set, identity/`origin_id` intact) then `broadcast_status_delete(request, status)` to the author's remote followers, and redirects back to the film page with a message.
- **Federation behavior (confirmed live):** a local user deleting their own review broadcasts a signed Delete to each remote follower's home inbox; the receiver soft-deletes its mirror by the Note's home URL (the M4 increment 6 inbound `handle_delete`). A mirror's review is not deletable locally — only the author's own local reviews carry the button, and the route rejects non-local rows.
- **Feed side: no change needed.** Deleting a review leaves a bare "watched" feed entry — R35's documented consequence. `feed_entries` derives the watched event from the `ShelfFilm` row (untouched), and `Status.feed_for` / the films-page rating subquery already filter `deleted=False`, so the review text + rating simply drop off while the film stays on the Watched shelf (v0.1 has no unwatch — R19; the user can re-review via the finish modal, D5's partial index ignoring the tombstone).
- **Interaction with R56:** independent surfaces — R56 hides *blocked users'* reviews (read-side, viewer state); this is an author-only write action on one's own review. No conflict (a user cannot block themselves).

**Live verification (two-instance, real HTTP via `curl -4`, 2026-09-14):** A's web recreated on the new image; minnix↔fedb mutual follows + mirrors confirmed intact first. As `minnix@A`: created a throwaway film "M5 Delete Test" (2026) → marked watched with 4.5 + text → **B mirrored the Note** (`remote_url=http://192.168.1.138:3030/status/15/`, attributed to the minnix mirror; review visible on B's film page + fedb's feed). Button matrix on A's film page: author sees exactly one Delete form, anonymous sees none. `POST /status/15/delete/` → 302 back to the film page; **A's status 15 became a tombstone** (deleted=t, content cleared, origin_id intact) and — over real HTTP — **B's mirror (status 9) became a tombstone too**, confirming the real `broadcast_status_delete` path. After the delete: the review was gone from both instances' film pages ("No reviews yet") and from both feeds, leaving a bare "watched M5 Delete Test" entry on each (R35). Throwaway state removed in dependency order (statuses → shelffilms → films, one transaction per instance); minnix↔fedb mutual follows + mirrors kept (verified: 2 follow rows on each side, both sites 200).

**Test baseline:** before = 689 passed + 5 skipped. After = **697 passed, 5 skipped** (+8: 7 in test_film_views.py — login gate, POST-only, soft-delete + keeps-watched + film-page/feed effect, cross-user 404, already-deleted 404, button own-only matrix, anonymous-hidden; 1 in test_activitypub_status.py — the view broadcasts a Delete to the remote follower only). ruff check + format green (verified on the built image); `makemigrations --check` clean (no model changes — `Status.delete` + `broadcast_status_delete` predate this increment).

**Decisions:** R57 in §4 below.

### M6 — increment plan (forward-looking)

M6 = "Polish & public deploy" (PLAN.md §5). Unlike M1–M5 there is no formal exit bar in PLAN.md §5, so it is scoped here into small verified increments. Per the 2026-09-14 session directive, items needing owner input or external resources are **flagged as blockers rather than stalled on**; only the autonomous parts are built now.

1. ✅ **Daily `pg_dump` backup job** — `backup_database` management command (custom-format dump + count-based retention) + a dedicated `backup` compose service (daily sleep loop, independent of web/worker) + `backups_volume`; `postgresql-client` added to the image (§4.7). *(done 2026-09-14, R58; live-verified against the real DB — dump validated with `pg_restore`, retention pruning confirmed.)*
2. ✅ **Artwork with the owner (D17)** — rebrand the neutral M1 CSS into an original theatrical grindhouse / midnight-movie identity, co-created with the owner. **Pivoted 2026-09-16** from the initial R60 grindhouse palette to a mockup-informed design after the owner supplied `MOCKUP.png` + font studies + a ChatGPT brief in `/home/minnix/design-elements/` (reference-only — NOT to be copied; major decisions co-made with the owner). Decisions recorded as **R61**. Sub-increments, each committed + owner-reviewed live: **(A) theme foundation — ✅ done 2026-09-16** (commit `411dad8`): theatrical near-black-brown/cream/red palette + a self-hosted SIL OFL font system (Monoton neon logo / Bebas Neue titles+headings / Oswald nav+UI / Archivo body) with type roles, CSS-only; **(B) header — ✅ done 2026-09-16** (commit `6db3a1f`, R62): logo-left / centered-search / profile-right top row + a centered nav row (Home, My Films, Find People, Import, Export + Log out when authed; Home-only for anonymous), marquee band styling, no corner script and no tagline; **(C) home 2-pane + sidebar — ✅ done 2026-09-17** (commit `1a90c95`, R64): home runs in a 72rem container flush with the marquee band (`main_class` block; other pages keep the 40rem reading column) — left pane = the feed under a "Now Playing" heading (landing intro dropped for signed-in users, kept as the anonymous CTA), right rail = two owner-supplied ticket-stub graphics heading a plain **Trending Films** card (most live reviews in a strict last-30-days window, written + rating-only alike, top 8) and a **Popular Genres** pill card (genres of reviewed films only, no runtime TMDB), each pill opening the new public `/genre/<slug>/` review subfeed. No hero/featured-film band and no recommendations section (owner confirmed).
3. ✅ **Getting-started page (the "guided tour" decision)** — owner chose the Letterboxd-style **static welcome page** over an interactive first-run overlay or no tour (R59, 2026-09-15): a public `/welcome/` walking the core loop in four steps (find a film via TMDB search → add to Watchlist → mark watched with rating+review → follow people), reached right after signup and from a persistent "Getting started" footer link. A plain template over `base.html`, no JS (R6); sidesteps the Shepherd.js license re-obtain flagged in PLAN.md §4.2. *(done 2026-09-15, R59; live-verified `/welcome/` over HTTP on A.)*
4. ⬜ **Real-domain deploy + first public instance** — the same web+db(+worker)+backup containers behind the operator's TLS-terminating reverse proxy (D14 — no in-project proxy), with `ALLOWED_HOSTS`/`CSRF_TRUSTED_ORIGINS` set for the public origin. **BLOCKED on external resources**: the operator's real domain + TLS proxy. Flagged, not started.

**Proposed M6 exit bar:** a first public instance reachable over HTTPS at the operator's domain — the four containers behind their TLS proxy — with the daily backup job confirmed running and a restore-from-backup drill passed. (Depends on items 2–4; item 1 is already done.)

### M6 increment 1 — daily pg_dump backup job (executed 2026-09-14, commit `7e668cb`)

First increment of M6: the "daily `pg_dump` backup job" from PLAN.md §3.8/§5, which the plan requires before public deploy. **One system package added** (`postgresql-client`, license-audited in PLAN.md §4.7); no model changes, no migrations, no new Python deps.

**What landed:**
- **`backup_database` management command** (`reeltalk/core/management/commands/backup_database.py`) — the repo's first. Dumps the configured database with `pg_dump --format=custom` into `settings.BACKUP_DIR` (default `/app/backups`, the backups volume) and prunes older dumps down to `settings.BACKUP_RETENTION` (default 7). Connection params come from Django's live `connection.settings_dict`, so it targets whatever DB the app points at (the real one in prod, the test DB under pytest); the password goes through `PGPASSWORD`, never argv. Two pure helpers (`dump_filename`, `dumps_to_prune`) are directly testable without a DB or binary; filenames are zero-padded UTC timestamps so lexicographic order == chronological, which drives retention.
- **`backup` compose service** — its own long-running service (independent of web/worker health) running `backup-loop.sh`: a lean sleep loop (no cron dependency) that wakes once a day at `BACKUP_TIME` (default 03:17, container TIME_ZONE) and calls the command; a failed run logs a warning and retries next day rather than exiting. Mounts the new `backups_volume`.
- **Dockerfile** — `postgresql-client` added to the runtime image (provides `pg_dump`/`pg_restore`; §4.7); all three app-image services (web/worker/backup) share it. `/app/backups` is created appuser-owned in the image and declared a VOLUME, so the named volume initializes writable by the runtime user (the same artifact-volume ownership fix as `static_volume`/`media_volume`).
- **Settings + config** — `BACKUP_DIR` + `BACKUP_RETENTION` (env-overridable); `.env.example` documents the knobs (`BACKUP_TIME`/`BACKUP_RETENTION`/`BACKUP_DIR`) + a restore one-liner.

**Design notes (R58):** custom format (`-Fc`) over plain SQL — smaller, gzip-compressed, `pg_restore`-able, the Postgres-recommended default for automated backups. A dedicated service over a Django-Q2 scheduled task: backups must run regardless of app/queue health, and this avoids adding a qcluster `--beat` process, while the dump logic stays in one tested command (the service is just a thin daily wake). Retention is count-based (keep newest N), predictable at v0.1 scale.

**Live verification (:3030, 2026-09-14):** stack brought up with the new `backup` service — its loop logged the correct next-run time (~23 h to the next 03:17). A one-off `python manage.py backup_database` into the volume wrote a **691 KB custom-format dump** of the real `reeltalk` DB; `pg_restore --list` confirmed it valid (Format CUSTOM, gzip, 200 TOC entries incl. the real tables + the search-vector trigger function). Retention verified live: two further runs with `--keep 2` bounded the volume at exactly 2 dumps and pruned the oldest. **Gotcha hit & fixed:** the first live run failed with "Permission denied" — the fresh `backups_volume` initialized root-owned because `/app/backups` did not exist in the image; fixed by creating it appuser-owned + declaring it a VOLUME, then re-initializing the (empty) volume. Test dumps removed afterwards; the volume is left clean for production daily operation. Both instances still healthy (:3030/:3031 → 200); A's minnix↔fedb mutual follow intact (2 `social_user_follows` rows — the documented kept state).

**Test baseline:** before = 697 passed + 5 skipped. After = **709 passed, 5 skipped** (+7 backup tests — filename shape ×2, retention pruning ×3, end-to-end valid-custom-dump + prune; +5 clean-room guard cases for the five new files). ruff check + format green (verified on the built image); `makemigrations --check` clean (no model changes).

**Decisions:** R58 in §4 below.

### M6 increment 2 — static getting-started page (executed 2026-09-15, commit `f4e1ab3`)

Second increment of M6: the "guided tour" item from PLAN.md §3.7/§5 ("[polish, with owner]"). A **with-owner** decision (D17 — no solo UX calls), so the scope was confirmed with the owner before building: they chose the **Letterboxd-style static welcome page** over an interactive first-run overlay or no tour at all (R59). The recommendation was grounded on a verified look at how Letterboxd actually onboards — their `/welcome/` is a single static scrollable page, not coach marks — which also happens to be the cheapest option that fits R6 and sidesteps the Shepherd.js license re-obtain flagged in PLAN.md §4.2. **No migrations, no new deps, no JS** (a plain template over `base.html`).

**What landed:**
- **`GET /welcome/`** (`social/views.welcome`, `templates/welcome.html`; public — no login gate): the core loop in four numbered steps, in order — (1) **Find a film** (the search bar / full `/search/`, TMDB-backed; search is the primary add-film flow per the owner), (2) **Add it to your Watchlist** (film-page control or the one-click search-results button), (3) **Mark it watched** (star rating required + optional review), (4) **Follow film fans** (`/find/` → follow → their activity in your feed). Anonymous visitors get a "Sign up" CTA; signed-in users see their handle and a live Find-people link instead. Manual create/edit is deliberately absent from the tour (kept low-key, per the owner's search-is-primary expectation).
- **Signup redirect** — `signup` now lands new users on `/welcome/` (was `index`), so the page is reached right after account creation; the first-run `setup` wizard still redirects to `index` (the operator, not a new user).
- **Persistent link** — a "Getting started" footer link beside "About this instance" on every page (low-key, matching R29's demotion of secondary surfaces rather than crowding the header nav).
- **CSS** — a small neutral `.welcome-steps` / `.step` / `.step-num` block appended to `reeltalk.css` (R11: numbered-badge steps, consistent with the existing minimal styling; real visual design still deferred to the artwork item).

**Live verification (:3030, 2026-09-15):** A's web recreated on the new image (entrypoint re-collected static; no migrations). Over HTTP (`curl -4`): `GET /welcome/` → **200** with all four step headings in order, the `/search/` link in step 1, the anonymous "Sign up" CTA, and the footer `/welcome/` link present. The signed-in variant (handle + Find-people link, no signup CTA) and the signup→`/welcome/` redirect are pinned by the test client. Kept federation state confirmed intact after the web recreate: A still holds both `minnix -> fedb@192.168.1.138:3031` and the inbound `fedb@… -> minnix` follow rows; B (:3031) up at 200.

**Test baseline:** before = 709 passed + 5 skipped. After = **715 passed, 5 skipped** (+6: 4 in the new test_welcome.py — anonymous render + signup CTA, four steps in order + search link, signed-in handle/Find-people/no-signup-CTA, footer link on every page; +2 clean-room guard cases for the two new files `templates/welcome.html` + `test_welcome.py`). The existing `test_auth.py` signup test now also asserts the redirect target is `/welcome/`. ruff check + format green (verified on the built image); `makemigrations --check` clean (no model changes).

**Decisions:** R59 in §4 below.

### M6 increment 3 — artwork (D17), step 1: core palette + dark base theme (executed 2026-09-15, commit `2ab2dfb`)

First sub-step of the M6 **artwork** item (plan item 2): the with-owner (D17) rebrand of the neutral M1 CSS into an original grindhouse / midnight-movie identity. Per D17 this is a co-creation, not a solo design call — so before building, the four open design decisions were put to the owner and confirmed (recorded as R60): the 7-swatch palette (approved as proposed), self-hosted WOFF2 fonts (Anton + Kaushan Script, system-ui body), a neon marquee header + icon favicon, and a medium texture level. This step is **step 1 of 5**: the core palette + base dark theme only — the whole site flips from the light/neutral M1 look to the dark grindhouse palette in one reviewable change. No new files, no migrations, no JS; `reeltalk.css` alone changed (49+/29−).

**What landed (step 1):**
- **`:root` rewritten** — the seven approved IMAGE2 swatches as named vars (`--blood-red #a02e24`, `--amber #d1581f`, `--mustard #dda32b`, `--cream #ebe3d3`, `--teal #2f6b68`, `--brown #6e4a2c`, `--deep-black #171310`), plus derived shades for legibility on the dark base (`--neon-red #e2543c` — a brightened blood red for links/interactive accents, since the deep oxblood is too low-contrast for body-size link text on near-black; and `--muted`, `--border`, `--surface`, `--surface-2`, `--field`). The existing semantic roles (`--bg`/`--fg`/`--accent`/`--error`) are re-pointed at these, so the rest of the sheet needed only a color sweep, not a rewrite.
- **Hardcoded light-theme colors swept** — every literal that would break on dark: `#fff` (buttons, step badges) → cream; gold stars `#f5a623` → mustard; the five `#eef3f8` panel/hover surfaces → `--surface`/`--surface-2`; the error-message tint (`#fbeaea`/`#e3bcbc`) → a translucent blood-red fill + amber text; modal backdrop + dropdown shadow → black. Form fields (text/search/textarea) get an inset `--field` background + brown border so they read on the near-black page.
- **Deliberately untouched this step:** fonts (system-ui stays — step 2), the header/wordmark/favicon (still plain text — step 3), and all texture/grit (step 5). Layout, spacing, and structure are byte-for-byte unchanged, so this is a pure palette flip.

**Verification (:3030, 2026-09-15):** `docker compose build web` + `up -d web`; over HTTP the homepage returns 200 and the served stylesheet (`reeltalk.b688b76f0b55.css`) carries the new `--blood-red`/deep-black values. In-container gate on the built image: ruff check + format green, `makemigrations --check` clean (no model changes), **pytest 715 passed + 5 skipped** — identical to the pre-change baseline (CSS is not imported by Python, so no regression path). Awaiting the owner's live browser review of the dark theme before step 2.

**Decisions:** R60 in §4 below.

Sub-increment **B** of the M6 artwork item (plan item 2): `templates/base.html`'s header rebuilt into the R61 marquee layout — no backend changes, no new files, no JS touched (`base.html` + `reeltalk.css` only). Four open design points were confirmed with the owner before building (recorded as **R62**): **no tagline** under the wordmark; a **wide header band** (header container 72rem while page content stays in the 40rem column until sub-increment C); the nav row **centered**; anonymous visitors get a **Home-only** nav (Find People / Import / Export are login-gated views — no dead-end links offered).

The account/nav links no longer share this row.

The suggest dropdown (`#search-suggest` + `js/search.js`) anchors under the reflowed search box unchanged — form/input/ul markup and ids preserved verbatim; below 40rem the search drops to its own full-width row so the dropdown keeps its shape.

**Verification (:3030, 2026-09-16):** `docker compose build web` + `up -d web`. Anonymous homepage 200 over HTTP with the new markup (header-top grid, Log in / Sign up right, Home-only nav carrying `class="active"`); served stylesheet `reeltalk.e314c8a206dc.css` carries the new rules. Authenticated state verified via the in-container test client: all five nav items + Log out render; the active underline moves correctly across `/`, `/find/`, `/preferences/import/` and `/user/minnix/films/`; the handle links to the profile page; no "Reviews" in the header. Headless-Chromium screenshot confirms the rendered layout (logo left / centered search / actions right / centered nav with red-underlined Home). Gate: ruff check + format green, `makemigrations --check` clean, pytest **723 passed + 5 skipped** — identical to baseline (no Python changes, no new files → no new guard cases). Awaiting the owner's live browser review.

The nav-row **Log out lost its button box** (`.site-nav form.inline button`: no border/padding/box-shadow, muted Oswald at nav-link size, cream on hover) so it reads as nav text; inline buttons elsewhere keep their boxed style. - **Asset** — the owner-supplied tile moved to `reeltalk/social/static/images/grunge-background.png` (2.8 MB PNG, telltale-free; becomes a clean-room guard case → **new suite baseline 724 passed + 5 skipped**). Served content-hashed (`grunge-background.0903dd657c85.png`) via ManifestStaticFilesStorage.
- **Body texture** — `--grunge-stack` = `linear-gradient(rgba(9,8,7,.86))` scrim over the tile, `background-size: auto, 1000px`, repeat; texture reads at ~14% behind everything. `.feed-item` / `.review` panels get `rgba(14,12,10,.88)` so text stays readable while gaps show grain.
- **Header de-textured** — the increment-5 marquee background (red halftone + top-edge glow) removed; the header now carries the same site-wide surface as the rest of the page.
- **Dividers** — new `--divider: #c1c1be` token (owner pick): 3px rule under the header and above the footer (footer upgraded from 1px warm-brown). To sit them *under* the grunge, a 3px strip of the tile is blended over each line via `mix-blend-mode: soft-light` on `.site-header::after` / `.site-footer::before` — pixel-verified variance across the rule (min 146 / max 202) instead of a flat band. (First attempt painted scrim+tile over the lines — buried them at ~14%; blending is required because the tile is opaque.) The suggest dropdown's existing `z-index: 50` keeps it above the header strip.
- **Mobile fixes found during the width sweep** — below 40rem the wordmark steps down to 2.2rem and the wide logo/search gap collapses (3.6rem + 4rem gap overflowed a 390px viewport); `.review-head` wraps; long unbreakable remote handles (`overflow-wrap: anywhere`) break instead of scrolling sideways.

**Verification (:3030, 2026-09-17):** full gate — ruff check + format green, `makemigrations --check` clean, pytest **724 passed + 5 skipped** (+1 guard case for the new PNG). Headless-Chromium screenshots of the authenticated feed and a film page at 1400px and 390px (rendered through a throwaway cookie-injecting proxy against a test-client-minted session — proxy stopped, session revoked afterwards): texture visible without fighting text at .86, no horizontal overflow on mobile after the wrap fixes; welcome-page shots confirm both blended dividers. Owner reviewed live and approved ("Looks good") before commit.

**Decisions:** R63 in §4 below.

### M6 increment 7 — artwork: home 2-pane + ticket-stub rail + genre subfeed (executed 2026-09-17, commit `1a90c95`)

Sub-increment **C** of the M6 artwork item (plan item 2) — the last artwork piece. Eight open points were put to the owner before building and every one landed on the recommended option; recorded as **R64**. First pass over the live data shaped two of the answers: instance A holds **1384 films / 1376 with genres but only 6 reviews**, and the imported archive puts **1383 of 1384 films on a shelf** — so a shelf-inclusive genre tally would just echo the TMDB import, and a strict 30-day trending window shows a short list rather than a full stub.

**What landed:**
- **2-pane home** — `base.html` gained a `{% block main_class %}` hook; `home.html` opts into `.container.wide-main` (**72rem**, flush with the marquee band so the feed's left edge sits under the wordmark and the rail's right edge under the profile corner). Every other page keeps the 40rem reading column. Below 64rem the grid stacks and the two rail panels sit side by side; the rail is 22rem.
- **Left pane** — `h1.pane-title` "**Now Playing**" (Bebas, with a red hairline running out toward the rail via `::after`); the feed markup itself is unchanged (R34/R35/R37 rows). The landing intro (`<h1>{{ site.name }}</h1>`, tagline, "Signed in as …") is **dropped for signed-in users** — the header wordmark already carries the brand; anonymous visitors get that copy inside `<section class="landing">` where the feed would be, so `test_home_anonymous_has_no_feed`'s "`class=\"feed\"` absent" rule still holds.
- **Trending Films** (`trending_films()` in `core/models.py`) — live D5 reviews (`live_reviews()`: `status_type ∈ REVIEW_TYPES`, not deleted, **remote mirrors included** — the rail shows what the instance holds) grouped by film inside a **strict 30-day window** with no top-up, ranked by count then newest review then film id for a stable order, capped at 8. Written reviews and rating-only entries count alike (R61's owner-confirmed definition).
- **Popular Genres** (`ranked_genres()` / `popular_genres()`) — genres of **reviewed films only**, counted as live reviews per genre (matching the rows the subfeed lists), ties alphabetical. A shelf-inclusive or catalog-wide tally was rejected precisely because it would light up pills that open empty pages.
- **`/genre/<slug>/`** (`core/views.genre`, public, `GENRE_PAGE_SIZE = 20`) — that genre's live reviews newest-first with poster thumb + film link per row, paginated like My Films, R55/R56 read-side filters for a signed-in viewer (blocked films and blocked authors stay out). Slugs resolve through `genre_from_slug()`, which knows only genres carrying reviews → an unknown or stale slug **404s**, so no pill can dead-end.
- **Rail artwork** — two owner-supplied ticket graphics (`reeltalk/social/static/images/movie-ticket.png`, `popular-genres.png`; each supplied at 2172×724 RGBA ≈ 2.2 MB, resized to 1100×367 and FASTOCTREE-quantized to **≈114 KB** with the torn-edge alpha kept, per owner request) rendered as `<img class="rail-banner">` with a −0.8° tilt and `drop-shadow` so the shadow traces the torn silhouette instead of a rectangle. Because each graphic prints its own panel title, the cards below carry **no visible heading** — an `.visually-hidden` `<h2>` keeps each panel named for assistive tech and the page outline. An intermediate round drew the stub in pure CSS (masked scallops + punched tear line + micro-print on `--paper`/`--ink` tokens); the owner replaced that with their own artwork in review, so the CSS paper treatment and both tokens were removed.

**Verification (:3030, 2026-09-17):** full gate on the tree as committed — ruff check + format green, `makemigrations --check` clean (no model changes: helpers only), pytest **750 passed + 5 skipped** (20 new tests across `test_home_rail.py` + `test_genre_page.py`; each new file adds a clean-room guard case). Headless-Chromium shots of the anonymous and signed-in home (the latter through a test-client-minted session, revoked after) plus `/genre/thriller/`. Owner reviewed live over three rounds — CSS stub → their PNG banner + plain card, "Most reviewed in the last 30 days" note dropped, root source PNGs deleted with owner OK, asset optimized — and approved ("Looks great") before commit.

**Decisions:** R64 in §4 below.

### M6 artwork polish — worn chrome header/footer rails (executed 2026-09-18, commit `f067942`)

The M6 artwork item closed with increment 7; the owner then opened a **fresh, open-ended polish pass** on 2026-09-18 with no pre-assigned scope — they surface the items, we co-decide each (D17 stays in force).
