First item: the light-gray rules dividing the header and footer from the body "are hard to tell have the grunge texture overlaid", and should be made **dramatically grungy chrome**, with `MOCKUP.png` cited for the look (reference-only, as ever — studied, not copied; its own `MOCKUP-HTML.html` turns out to use plain 1px borders, so the grime there is painted into the mockup image). Recorded as **R65**.

**Why the R63 rule read as flat:** it painted a solid `--divider` `#c1c1be` border and overlaid a strip of the grunge tile with `mix-blend-mode: soft-light`. Soft-light barely moves luminance against a near-black backdrop, so the texture on the 3px line was effectively invisible.

**What landed:**
- **CSS blend modes could not fix it** — worth remembering before retrying one: the grunge tile is a *dark* texture, so every blend that removes metal (`hard-light` / `overlay` / `multiply`) crushed the whole rail to black instead of pitting it, and hand-placed `repeating-linear-gradient` "corrosion" read as a **dashed hairline**, not worn metal. Corrosion needs a mask/albedo, not a blend against a dark backdrop.
- **Original generated strip** — `reeltalk/social/static/images/chrome-rail.png` (seamless **2400×17**, 49 KB RGBA): bevelled bright metal lit from above, narrow corrosion pits eating ~8% of the run, short re-polished specular streaks, blurred warm rust stains bleeding past the edges, dense per-pixel grain so nothing is airbrushed, and a dark bottom edge that sells the extrusion. Longitudinal wear is a sum of **integer-frequency sinusoids over the strip width**, so it tiles with no visible seam; blobs are drawn wrapped at `x-W`/`x`/`x+W` for the same reason. Generated by this project, so REWRITE.md rule 4 is satisfied without an external asset.
- **Both rules paint it** `repeat-x` via `.site-header::after` / `.site-footer::before` (17px tall, offset `-10px`, `drop-shadow` following the alpha rather than a rectangle). The 3px borders underneath go **near-black (`--black-2`)** so the pits read as *dark gaps* — the light steel now lives in the art, so the **`--divider` token is deleted** (its only two uses were these rules). The footer starts mid-strip (`background-position: -1150px 0`) so it isn't a mirror copy of the header's pattern.

**Verification (:3030, 2026-09-18):** full gate on the tree as committed — ruff check + `format --check` (80 files) green, `makemigrations --check` clean (no Python change), pytest **750 passed + 5 skipped in 328s** in a fresh `docker compose run --rm` container. The new PNG is inside the clean-room guard's scope (`TOP_LEVEL_SKIP_DIRS` only skips top-level `images/`/`static/`, so `reeltalk/social/static/images/…` stays in scope): it collects its own case and the telltale parametrization now covers **144 files**. Headless-Chromium crops of both rails (magnified, and composited against the mockup's own rail at matched scale) plus a true-1× check that pitting doesn't break the bar at real size. Owner reviewed live at :3030 and approved ("Looks great") before commit.

**Tuning harness (not committed):** `.qwen/tmp/rail-gen.py` generates four drama levels (`a-clean`, `b-worn`, `c-rusty`, `d-max` — `d-max` is the one shipped) and `.qwen/tmp/rail-preview.py` composites candidates over real backdrop pixels from a live screenshot, so re-tuning drama/rust/thickness is a regeneration + file swap, no CSS change. Dials offered to the owner: drama level, rust warmth, thicker 5–6px core, faint warm bloom around the rail.

**Decisions:** R65 in §4 below.

### M6 artwork polish — weathered background plate (executed 2026-09-18, commit `4a79d80`)

Polish item 2: the distressed background was "a little too subtle and can be hard to notice at all" next to `MOCKUP.png`; goal stated as a more **worn and weathered** look. Recorded as **R66**.

**Measured before designing.** Sampling background-only pixels: our page background sat at **luminance 9.1 / stddev 1.9** (effectively flat) while the mockup's backgrounds sit at **lum 12–22 / stddev 15–32**. The owner-supplied tile was never the problem — it measures lum 15.2 / stddev 14.5; the `rgba(9,8,7,.86)` scrim was erasing ~86% of its grain.

**The trap worth recording:** thinning the scrim is the wrong lever on its own. Simulating the CSS stack (scrim over tile over `--black`) showed that hitting the mockup's stddev with the raw tile needs alpha ≈ .58, which drags **mean luminance to 43–56** — a muddy grey page, nothing like the reference. The mockup is **bimodal**: near-black areas plus sparse bright wear (dust, scuffs, peeled paint). Its margins carry high local contrast at a *low* mean, which only comes from bright marks on a dark base, not from lifting the whole plate.

**What landed:**
- **`reeltalk/social/static/images/grunge-weathered.png`** — derived from the owner's tile so the grain identity carries over (REWRITE.md rule 4: our own derived asset). Pipeline: autocontrast → **gamma-crush the midtones** (`x^2.7`) so the plate averages near-black → multiply a periodic low-frequency field to carve deep large-scale worn blotches → screen on **dense dust flecks, short scuffs and long streaky scratches** (the real source of the prominence) plus a handful of near-invisible pale veils. Every layer is built **periodic** (integer-frequency sinusoids over the tile width) or drawn wrapped at `x-W`/`x`/`x+W`, so it still tiles seamlessly.
- **Scrim `.86 → .62`**, now a pure prominence dial rather than a brightness one: with the plate's mean pinned low, page-background stddev rises **1.9 → 8.0** while mean luminance *drops* **9.1 → 7.5** — more visible wear, darker page, text contrast untouched. Content-column region measures stddev 29.5, inside the reference band.
- **Asset weight**: quantized FASTOCTREE 256-color → **228 KB** (from a 2 MB full-colour render; mean channel error ≈ 2/255, invisible), in keeping with the owner's earlier asset-size preference.
- The original `grunge-background.png` (2.8 MB) is now referenced nowhere but **kept** — deletion was offered and not requested, so it stays as the source tile.

**Dead ends worth remembering:** (a) lifting the plate's overall brightness raises the mean without buying variation; (b) pale "worn patches" above roughly lum ~50 read as **soft clouds/bokeh dots**, not wear — single blurred ellipses especially, so patches are built as irregular clusters and kept dim; (c) clustered saturated warm speckle reads as colour noise, so dust is neutral-warm `(v, .95v, .88v)` and evenly spread.

**Verification (:3030, 2026-09-18):** ruff check + `format --check` (80 files) green, `makemigrations --check` clean (no Python change), pytest **751 passed + 5 skipped in 325s** — exactly one more than the previous run, the new asset's own clean-room guard case. Live headless-Chromium measurement of the served page confirms the numbers above. Owner reviewed live and approved ("That looks great") before commit.

**Tuning harness (not committed):** `.qwen/tmp/bg-gen2.py` (+ the inline final recipe) regenerates plates at any dust/scratch/veil density; `.qwen/tmp/bg-preview.py` composites a plate at several scrim alphas and prints lum + stddev against the mockup target, so re-tuning prominence is one alpha edit in `--grunge-stack`. Offered dials: `.72` subtler / `.62` shipped / `.52` heavier.

**Decisions:** R66 in §4 below.

### M6 artwork polish — neon nav indicator (executed 2026-09-18, commit `e574522`)

Polish item 3: the red underline that marks which page you are on "is just a red line", while `MOCKUP.png` gives it a glowing red **neon** effect; the owner asked for the same treatment on our nav indicator. Recorded as **R67**.

**Measured before designing (the R66 lesson applied to CSS).** Probing the mockup's indicator at its core (`MOCKUP.png`, tube at y≈99–104 under the "Home" label) shows it is not a coloured rule at all: a **white-hot core** (peak `255,226,225`) sits inside saturated red `(245,31,35)`, and the bloom decays over ~8px above / ~10px below before meeting the backdrop. A luminance/redness map of the label region also shows the active word's glyphs wrapped in a warm red halo — which our R61 header already approximates with `text-shadow: 0 0 10px rgba(255,65,56,.5)`, so that was left alone. Our live page, by contrast, painted a flat 2px `--red-bright` border with no bloom whatsoever — hence "just a red line".

**What landed (CSS only, one file):**
- The marker moves from `.site-nav a.active { border-bottom-color }` to a positioned **`.site-nav a.active::after`** bar — a border can carry neither a gradient cross-section nor a shadow bloom. 3px tall at `bottom: -3px`, `background: linear-gradient(to bottom, var(--red) 0 1px, #fff 1px 2px, var(--red-bright) 2px)` (upper glass edge → white-hot filament row → lit body), over four shadow stops `3 / 9 / 20 / 40px` from hot orange-red to a faint wide spill.
- **Nav metrics are untouched:** the anchor keeps its `border-bottom: 2px solid transparent`, and gains only `position: relative`. `.site-header` sets no `overflow`, so the 40px spill is not clipped; nav wrapping and the ≤40rem mobile header behave exactly as before.
- **Scope held to the header nav row by owner decision** — the same motif was explicitly *not* applied to the `.tabs a.active` underline on the films page, and no hover warm-up was added.

**How it was chosen (fast-iteration pattern for pure-CSS work).** No Docker rebuild per candidate: `.qwen/tmp/neon-lab.html` links the **real** stylesheet from :3030 and repeats the header band four times, each variant overriding only the underline recipe; headless Chromium renders it and `.qwen/tmp/neon-sheet.py` cuts an annotated sheet (mockup reference → current live line → A/B/C/D, each at 1× and 3× over the real rendered backdrop). The owner picked from that sheet, then one CSS edit + one build shipped it. Two gotchas worth keeping: locating our own underline by scanning for hot pixels keeps catching the wordmark's glow and the candy-red "Sign up" button (the sheet pins the known y-band instead), and `numpy` is not installed on this host — plain PIL pixel access is the way.

**Verification (:3030, 2026-09-18):** full gate on the tree as committed — ruff check + `format --check` (80 files) green, `makemigrations --check` clean (no Python change), pytest **751 passed + 5 skipped in 328s** from a fresh `docker compose run --rm` container — *unchanged* from the `c9dec27` baseline because no file was added, so no new clean-room guard case. Headless-Chromium measurement of the served page confirms the tube reproduces the reference shape: `(212,43,38)` glass edge → `(255,255,255)` core row → `(255,65,56)` body, bloom rising over 8px above and decaying to backdrop by ~9px below. Owner reviewed live at :3030 and approved ("Looks good") before commit.

**Decisions:** R67 in §4 below.

### M6 artwork polish — wordmark relit as red glass with a hot filament (executed 2026-09-18, commit `085c10b`)

Polish item 4, the direct follow-on to item 3: "the new nav bar selection glow looks really good, but now the main ReelTalk logo looks somewhat dim compared to it" — the owner asked for similar treatment on the wordmark. Recorded as **R68**.

**Measured before designing.** Sampling the lit pixels of the wordmark region on the live page: `.brand` was a flat `#f9a58f` fill with **max luminance 193 and zero pixels above 200** — a uniform pale salmon with no hot spots anywhere, which is why it read as paint rather than light. The mockup's wordmark in the same measurement is **bimodal**: max 250, p90 220, **22.8% of lit pixels ≥200**, 0.7% ≥240 — deep red glass carrying sparse white-hot filament highlights (R66's contrast-at-low-mean lesson again).

**What landed (CSS only, `.brand`):**
- **Fill is the filament, the stroke is the glass.** `color: #fff4ee` plus `-webkit-text-stroke: 0.0174em var(--red)` left in the default paint order, so the stroke paints *over* the fill and carves red back into each Monoton bar, leaving a narrow white-hot core — the only one of seven rendered recipes that reproduces the reference's per-stroke structure. Setting `paint-order: stroke fill` instead puts the glass under the filament and loses it (that was round 1's variant B).
- **Bloom rebuilt from four stops to six:** `0 0 2px #fff → 5/12px --red-bright → 26px --red → 48px rgba(255,40,25,.7) → 80px rgba(255,30,20,.35)`.
- **Rejected after being built and measured:** plain hot fill (max 243 but 25% of pixels near-white — reads white/pink, not red neon), gradient-clipped tube via `background-clip: text` (hot crown pooling to red, no per-stroke core), a heavier 1.6px stroke (swallows the filament: flat red, max 211), and a warm step-up of the existing salmon (brighter, still no true core).

**The mobile trap — worth keeping.** The first build used `-webkit-text-stroke: 1px`. Under 40rem the brand steps down to 2.2rem, where Monoton's bars are only ~2–3px wide, so a fixed 1px glass edge **ate the filament entirely**: at 390px the wordmark measured **max lum 171 with nothing above 200 — darker than the flat fill it replaced**, i.e. a silent regression visible only on small screens. Caught by screenshotting at 390 as well as 1440 rather than trusting the desktop render. Fixed by expressing the stroke in **em** so it scales with the type (`0.0174em` == the 1px that worked at 3.6rem, ~0.6px at 2.2rem), which needed no media-query special case; mobile now measures max 223 / 7.5% ≥200 with visible cores. Variants measured at both widths by `.qwen/tmp/stroke-lab.py` (renders one header per pass so band detection is unambiguous, `1440` and `390`).

**Verification (:3030, 2026-09-18):** full gate on the tree as committed — ruff check + `format --check` (80 files) green, `makemigrations --check` clean (no Python change), pytest **751 passed + 5 skipped in 334s** from a fresh `docker compose run --rm` container; unchanged from baseline because no file was added. Note the gate was re-run after the em fix — the earlier green run had measured the superseded `1px` tree. Live headless-Chromium measurement: desktop wordmark **max 245, p90 197, 9.8% ≥200** (reference 250 / 220 / 22.8%), mobile **max 223, 7.5% ≥200**. Owner reviewed live at :3030 and approved ("That looks great"), and asked for this increment to be **committed and pushed**.

**Decisions:** R68 in §4 below.

### M6 artwork polish — header text moves to the film-title face (executed 2026-09-19, commit `8b41c7b`)

Polish item 5. Owner's complaint, verbatim: *"I'm not happy with the font used for the nav bar items. I would like to change it to the same font that's being used for the film titles that's in the Trending Films sidebar."* Recorded as **R69**.

**Measured before designing.** The stylesheet explains the mismatch without any guessing: `.site-nav a` is explicitly `var(--ui)` (**Oswald 400**, condensed), while `.trending-film` declares *no* family at all and so inherits body's `var(--body)` (**Archivo**) at `font-weight: 600`. So the request is Oswald → Archivo on the nav. The catch is that Oswald is condensed and Archivo is not, so the swap buys width: measuring the six signed-in nav items at 1440 gives **247px today → 311px (+26%)** for a straight weight-600 match (500 barely helps: 307px; .88rem recovers most of it: 285px). Container is 1152px, so desktop has room either way — the question that actually mattered was the phone.

**Getting a real 390px viewport.** Headless Chromium clamps `--window-size` to ~500px minimum and `--force-device-scale-factor` does *not* shrink the layout viewport under `headless=new`, so every "390" render up to this point was silently a 500px render. A true phone width needs a **same-origin iframe of that width** — media queries follow the iframe's viewport, and `--allow-file-access-from-files` lets the wrapper page read geometry back out of it. (Two self-inflicted variants of the same bug produced a black phone capture and an empty probe: the wrapper must reference a *separate* file, never overwrite the page it embeds.)

**What the measurement changed.** The nav row **already wraps to two lines at 390 today**, so this was never "does it wrap" but "**where does it break**": recipes A/B push `Import` onto line 2, recipe C keeps today's split. Four recipes were rendered against the **live signed-in page** (a saved copy with only the nav rule overridden, so the chrome rail, neon tube and every media query stay authentic — not a hand-built mock band) and put in front of the owner as an annotated sheet; `.qwen/tmp/nav-compare.py` + `nav-verify.py`.

**What landed (CSS only), owner's pick = C:**
- `.site-nav a` and its borderless Log out twin: `font-size .95rem → .88rem`, `letter-spacing .03em → .02em`.
- `.header-user a` (the `minnix` handle; anon Log in / Sign up): `.95rem → .88rem` — it matched the nav's size before, so it matches it after.
- Typography roles block (last in `reeltalk.css`, where the type system is documented): `.site-nav a` and `.header-user a` **removed from the `--ui` list**, new rule gives `.site-nav a`, `.site-nav form.inline button`, `.header-user a` → `font-family: var(--body); font-weight: 600`. Log out must be named explicitly or the `button` role keeps it Oswald.
- The two stale comments that claimed nav was Oswald (the role map and the `--ui`/`--body` token annotations) were corrected in the same pass.

**Verification (:3030, 2026-09-19):** gate on the tree as committed — ruff check + `format --check` (**80 files**) green, `makemigrations --check` clean (no Python change), pytest **751 passed + 5 skipped in 331.91s** from a fresh `docker compose run --rm` container; unchanged from baseline because no file was added. Live headless measurement at **1440 and a true 390**: nav link, active nav link, Log out and profile link all compute to **Archivo 600 14.08px**, matching `.trending-film`'s face+weight; `.trending-count` and `.genre-pills a` still **Oswald 400**, so the scope held; nav row 41.4px (was 43.1), one line at desktop, phone split unchanged (`Home · My Films · Find People · Import` / `Export · Log out`). Two process notes: the first "verified" claim was premature — the container had not been rebuilt yet, and the verifier caught the live page still serving Oswald; only after `docker compose build web && up -d web` did the numbers move. Owner reviewed live at :3030 and approved ("Looks good"), asking for the README to be refreshed first and then everything **committed and pushed**. Docs in the same push: README status line, roadmap (M5 checked, M6 broken into done/remaining) and a new **"The look"** section describing the identity, type roles and assets.

**Decisions:** R69 in §4 below.

### M6 artwork polish — avatar discs in the marquee corner and at the head of every review row (executed 2026-09-19, commit `acbcb50`)

Polish item 6. Owner brief: *"In the MOCKUP.png … the user is able to have an avatar next to their name at the top right of the page. 1. I would like to have this as well for ReelTalk. 2. I would also like for a user's avatar to also show in the feed next to their name."* Recorded as **R70**.

**Measured before designing.** Probing `MOCKUP.png` (`.qwen/tmp/av-probe3.py`, `av-probe-feed.py`) gave the disc geometry rather than a guess: header disc **~39px** against a 10px x-height handle (= ~2.7× a 14px font), feed disc **~46px**, ring **~2px warm cream `(217,195,179)`**, a saturated red bloom hugging the portrait *inside* the metal (`(224,120,103)` → `(173,23,21)`), ~12px disc-to-name gap, essentially no outer halo. That is R67/R68's hot-core-in-coloured-glass motif on a disc — which is why recipe A was always going to beat a plain border.

**Four recipes rendered, not guessed** (`.qwen/tmp/av-compare.py` injects the candidate CSS *and* the avatar markup into a saved copy of the live signed-in page, so the chrome rail, neon tube, grunge plate and real media URLs stay authentic; `av-montage.py` / `av-check2.py` cut the strips): **A** cream ring + red inner bloom · **B** neon tube (red glass, white-hot inner line) · **C** worn chrome bezel · **D** plain hairline. Owner chose **A**, at the mockup-ratio sizes, for **all three** surfaces that share `.review-head`, with a hand-drawn CSS silhouette as the no-avatar placeholder.

**What landed (5 files, no new file):**
- **The ring lives on the base `.avatar`** — `border: 2px solid var(--avatar-ring)` + `inset 0 0 0 2px rgba(196,30,24,.85)` + a faint 7px spill — so the profile pages, the marquee corner and every row head carry one treatment. The old tan `border: 1px solid var(--border)` is **deleted, not overridden**; new token `--avatar-ring: #d9c3b3`. `flex: none` joined the same rule so a long remote handle can never squeeze a disc in a tight flex row.
- **One size for people, one for rows:** `.avatar-row` (2.05rem) is the only size modifier; the corner inherits the base 3rem because the owner asked it to match the profile pages exactly — so `.avatar-header` was removed from CSS *and* template rather than left restating the default.
- **Header markup keeps one tab stop:** the disc rides *inside* the existing `.user-name` profile link (now `inline-flex`, `gap: .7rem` ≈ the reference's 12px) instead of becoming a second link to the same page.
- **Feed rows:** avatar before `.review-author` in `home.html`, `film/detail.html` and `genre.html`, following `profile.html`'s existing `{% if …avatar %}` pattern rather than inventing a partial for three call sites. Discs are decorative (`alt=""` / `aria-hidden="true"`) — the handle beside them is still plain text, as before.
- **Placeholder is CSS, not an asset:** `.avatar-placeholder` became a hand-drawn silhouette plate (head + shoulders over a red→black gradient), so an empty disc reads lit rather than broken. No file added ⇒ suite total unchanged.

**Two owner follow-ups on the same item** (same day, both accepted): enlarge the corner disc to the profile size — which turned out to mean *deleting* its size rule, not raising a number — and give the profile/My Films discs the same ring, which is what moving the recipe onto base `.avatar` accomplishes for all surfaces at once.

**Verification (:3030, 2026-09-19):** gate on the tree as committed — ruff check + `format --check` green, `makemigrations --check` clean (no Python change), pytest **751 passed + 5 skipped in 326.63s** from a fresh `docker compose run --rm web` container; unchanged because no file was added. Read back off the *deployed* container (`.qwen/tmp/av-verify.py`, `av-verify2.py`): corner / `/user/minnix/` / `/user/minnix/films/` discs all compute **48×48px with an identical ring and bloom**; feed discs **32.8px** with the same ring on home, `/film/12/` and `/genre/thriller/`; handle still **Archivo 600 14.08px** (R69 held). At a **true 390** (iframe wrapper): header row **127.7px — unchanged from before the disc existed**, brand→corner gap holds 16px, `scrollWidth == clientWidth` (no overflow), nav line break untouched; R65 rails (header *and* footer), R66 plate and R67 tube all still painting. Owner reviewed live at :3030 and approved ("Looks good"), asking for the commit **and push**.

**Worth keeping:** a bigger corner avatar is *free* — `.header-top` height is set by the wordmark row (110px desktop / 127.7px phone), so discs up to 48px cost nothing in header height at either width; and measure `scrollWidth == clientWidth`, not just "it looks fine", before claiming a phone header is safe. One probe bug worth remembering: a one-argument `el => getComputedStyle(el)` helper silently drops the `::after` argument, so pseudo-element guards read as `none`/absent — that produced two false regression alarms here that a corrected probe cleared in one run.

**Open dials:** disc sizes and the .7rem gap; whether feed avatar+handle should link to the profile (they don't today); the mockup's account **chevron**, which implies a dropdown menu we do not have; and a pre-existing asymmetry left alone — `user_films.html` has no `{% else %}` branch, so My Films renders *no* disc for a user with no avatar while `/user/<name>/` shows the placeholder.

**Decisions:** R70 in §4 below.

### M6 artwork polish — lit red controls, a three-zone footer and a neon sign-off (executed 2026-09-19, commit `75e00b1`)

Polish items 7–9, raised by the owner as one brief with three asks: the striped red buttons "I don't like", *match* the mockup's **Read Reviews** button (mottled dark/light red + neon glow outline) and give the Getting Started step circles the same treatment; rebuild the footer closer to the mockup (all-caps Bebas wordmark + tagline bottom-left, small Archivo links in the middle); and emulate the mockup's right-hand **"Keep Watching."** red neon cursive with its film reel. Recorded as **R71**.

**Measured before designing.** Scanning a line through the reference button gives a ~2px rim peaking at `(248,134,128)` over an interior of `(128,19,22)`–`(161,46,44)`: sampling that interior (avoiding the label) returns **luminance mean 50.7, p10 44, p90 53** — mottled crimson with broad dark and light blotching, lit from the edge. That is R66's contrast-at-a-low-mean lesson again, and the same hot-core-in-coloured-glass family as the nav tube (R67) and wordmark (R68). Our old control was a flat `--blood-red` fill under a `repeating-linear-gradient` stripe overlay.

**What landed (2 files changed, 3 added):**
- **The fill is an original generated tile**, not a gradient: `reeltalk/social/static/images/button-mottle.png` (seamless 128×128 RGBA, **5.1 KB**) painted over `linear-gradient(180deg, #9c1d17, #861713, #6b100e)`. Like the chrome rail (R65) it is periodic or drawn wrapped across the tile edges, and its marks go **both ways** — bright warm flecks lift the base, near-black maroon pits eat it — because a single translucent overlay cannot mottle in one direction only. Authored here, so REWRITE.md rule 4 holds with no external asset. Rim: `2px solid var(--neon-rim)` (`#ff6a5b`) + inner red glow + four outer bloom stops; radius 7px. Measured back off the deployed page: **median 50 / p10 40** against the reference's 50.7 / 44.
- **`.step-num` takes the same recipe** at disc scale (radial base, smaller bloom), so Getting Started's four circles are lit discs rather than flat red chips.
- **Labels move to Bebas Neue caps.** The catch is that Bebas ships **one weight**: the inherited `font-weight: 600` would synthesise a faux-bold and fatten the lettering, so `400` is explicit, with `1.1rem` / `.05em` to hold presence on a condensed face. `button, .btn` was removed from the `--ui` role list and the three stale comments claiming Oswald owns buttons/nav were corrected in the same pass (the R69 nav change had already made one of them wrong).
- **Ghost controls are named explicitly.** `form.inline button` (Block / Follow) and `.search-row .watchlist-btn` share the `button` element but are not lit red, so they keep Oswald 600 sentence case — they need `font-family`, `font-weight`, `line-height`, `letter-spacing` **and** `text-transform` resets, plus `box-shadow: none` against the new bloom. Left alone they silently inherit the marquee label: first build rendered "Add to watchlist" uppercase with tracking (117px → 138.4px wide) and a shrunken box.
- **Footer = three zones on the existing chrome rail** (`base.html` + CSS): `.footer-brand` (Bebas caps wordmark + letter-spaced Archivo tagline *A Federated Home For Film Fans*), centred `.footer-links` (Getting Started / About This Site / Code → `github.com/minnixtx/reeltalk`, new tab), and `.footer-signoff`. The band widens to **72rem** so its edges line up with the marquee above, and stacks centred ≤40rem.
- **"Keep Watching." is a real script face, not a fake:** **Kaushan Script**, self-hosted SIL OFL (latin subset woff2 34 KB + its `OFL.txt` under `static/fonts/`, new `--script` token) — we had no cursive face at all. Four-stop bloom + −3° tilt like bent tubing, matching R67/R68's reading. The **film reel is inline SVG** authored in the template (ring, hub, six sprockets, tail swash) glowing through `drop-shadow`, so it added no binary asset and stays original art.

**Two traps worth keeping.** (1) A `line-height` on the shared `button, .btn` rule resizes the **ghost** controls too — the first cut (`1.05`) shrank lit buttons 44.8 → 38.5px and ghosts 32.3 → 29.4px; leading is now `1.4` on lit / `1.55` (= body) on ghost, which puts every box back within a pixel of its old height. A face swap should not move layout the owner did not ask about. (2) The **first `button[type=submit]` in the DOM is the header's Log out**, so probing that selector measures the nav control and reports the page buttons as unchanged — scope probes to `main button…`.

**Verification (:3030, 2026-09-19):** gate on the tree as committed — ruff check + `format --check` (80 files) green, `makemigrations --check` clean (no Python change), pytest **754 passed + 5 skipped in 323.64s** from a fresh `docker compose run --rm web` container: baseline 751 **+ one clean-room guard case per new file** (tile, woff2, OFL). Read back off the *deployed* container at 1440 and a **true 390**: Import / Find / Edit review / Edit compute `"Bebas Neue" 400 17.6px` uppercase with `document.fonts.check` true; Block / Delete / Add to watchlist stay `Oswald 600 14.4px` sentence case at their original 32.3px height; nav Log out still Archivo 600 14.08px (R69 held); footer wordmark Bebas 38.4px uppercase, links resolve to `/welcome/`, `/about/` and GitHub, Kaushan loaded, reel SVG present, grid `405 / 261 / 405` at 1440 and one centred column at 390 with `scrollWidth == clientWidth`. R65 rails (both), R66 plate, R67 tube, R68 stroke and R70 avatar rings all still painting. Throwaway session revoked and re-checked with `SessionStore().exists` → False. Owner reviewed live at :3030 and approved ("Looks good"), asking for the commit **and push**.

**Tuning harness (not committed):** `.qwen/tmp/f3/mottle-gen.py` regenerates the tile at any blotch/pit/fleck density (drama is a regeneration + asset swap, no CSS change); `.qwen/tmp/f3/ui-verify.py` and `btn-font.py` are the live read-back probes for this batch. Offered dials: fill mottle drama, rim thickness, bloom radius, label size/tracking, reel size, and whether the footer links keep their pipe separators.

**Decisions:** R71 in §4 below.

### M6 artwork polish — lit controls' bloom pulled in to a tight halo (executed 2026-09-20, commit `8a0c15a`)

Polish item 10. Rather than a new complaint, the owner picked a **dial** off the R71 open list — "Lit control dials" — and we measured all four before choosing. Recorded as **R72**.

**Measured before designing (all four dials, reference vs our deployed render).** Locating the mockup's **Read Reviews** button by scanning for saturated-red runs gives it at `MOCKUP.png` **x 355→558, y 500→543 (203×43px)**, label glyphs breaking the red at y 515–528. Our live control was captured with its own `getBoundingClientRect` so the identical probe ran on both.

| Dial | Reference | Ours before | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| Rim | ~2px, peak `(245,120,118)` lum 146 | ~2px, peak `(255,106,91)` lum 137 | already matched (ours a touch hotter) |
| **Bloom** | **2px up / 4px down** @backdrop+4 | **10px up / 19px down** | the real outlier — 3–5× wider |
| Mottle drama | mean 42.1, p10 36, p90 47, spread **10**, σ 5.3 | mean 38.8, p10 31, p90 49, spread **19**, σ 7.0 | ours ~2× the reference's spread |
| Label | mixed case, cap ~14px, 0.33 of box | UPPERCASE, cap ~15px, 0.33 of box | size matched; casing differs |

**Two findings worth keeping.** (1) The mockup's "neon glow" is a **tight halo, not a wide bloom** — its luminance sits at flat backdrop (4–6) from 14px out until ~3px, then lifts only at the rim. Our `4/13/28px` stops were a brighter sign than the reference makes. (2) The reference mottle is a **narrow, low-contrast maroon weave** (spread 10), not the broad blotching R71's note claimed — that note's `mean 50.7 / p10 44 / p90 53` was a thin label-free slice; sampling the side columns instead reads high (spread 34) purely from **label anti-aliasing**, so mottle samples must exclude the label band *and* its blended edges.

**Owner's calls:** tighten the bloom to the mockup; **keep** the mottle drama (busier fill reads better at our small control sizes); **keep** uppercase labels (caps is our own choice, not a mockup match — the mockup's label is mixed case).

**How the radius was chosen.** A bloom lab renders candidate outer-stop sets over the real stylesheet and backdrop and measures each with the same ladder used on the mockup. Two layouts failed before one worked: stacking candidates vertically and laying them in a horizontal row both **contaminated the decay ladders with the neighbouring control's own glow** (a 3/7/12 candidate read 18px because its neighbour's bloom lay in its measurement window). The working shape is **one candidate per page render** (`.qwen/tmp/f3/bloom-lab2.py`), isolated with no neighbour inside the window:

| Recipe | up | down | left | right |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A — current `4/13/28` | 10 | 9 | 10 | 9 |
| **B — `3/7/12`** | **5** | **4** | **5** | **4** |
| C — `2/5/9` | 3 | 2 | 4 | 2 |
| D — `3/8/14` | 6 | 5 | 7 | 5 |
| E — `2/6/10` | 4 | 3 | 5 | 3 |

**What landed (CSS only, one file, +19/−16):** `button, .btn` outer stops **`4/13/28 → 3/7/12`** (@ .8/.45/.18), hover **`6/18/40 → 4/9/15`**, `.step-num` **`4/12 → 3/8`** (@ .8/.42). Inset glows, the 2px `--neon-rim`, the 7px radius, the mottle tile and the Bebas caps labels are untouched. The R71 comment block was corrected to the measured reference (lum ~42, rim `(245,120,118)`, tight halo) rather than left restating the superseded numbers.

**Verification (:3030, 2026-09-20, off the rebuilt container).** Button glow ramp: before `299:14 300:28 … 306:63` reaching backdrop at ~364 (**~12px**); after `300:19 301:12 … 306:53` reaching backdrop at ~358 (**~6px**). Step disc before `386:18 387:27 388:36 389:52` → after `386:13 387:19 388:26 389:47` — lower at every step. Rim unchanged at ~2px `rgb(255,106,91)`, radius 7px. Regressions all held: lit controls 44.6px on welcome / import / find / film; **ghost controls unchanged at 32.3px with `box-shadow: none`** (Block / Follow / Add to watchlist / Delete — the R71 trap did not re-open); footer zones, Kaushan sign-off and reel SVG intact; true-390 `scrollWidth == clientWidth == 390`, no overflow; R65 chrome rail, R67 nav tube, R68 brand stroke `1.00224px` and R70 avatar ring `rgb(217,195,179)` + red inner bloom all still painting.

**A process bug worth more than the CSS change.** The first verification pass was minted with **only `SESSION_KEY`**. Django resolves `request.user` from `SESSION_KEY` **plus `HASH_SESSION_KEY`** (and needs `BACKEND_SESSION_KEY` for the backend), so a session missing the hash authenticates as **anonymous** — silently. Symptoms that gave it away: the avatar ring read `None`, the nav tube read `None` on `/welcome/`, and `/find/` and `/preferences/import/` returned **302** where the harness reported "page may lack the probed nodes". Re-minting with all three keys and re-running turned every one of those green. **Any future session: assert the minted session is actually authenticated before trusting a signed-in probe** — an anonymous render still shows lit buttons, so bloom/colour numbers survive the mistake while anything behind login silently does not get checked at all.

**Gate (fresh background run):** `ruff format --check` — *80 files already formatted*; `ruff check` — *All checks passed!*; `makemigrations --check` — *No changes detected*; pytest **754 passed + 5 skipped in 336.80s** — unchanged from the `eb22c04` baseline (CSS-only, no new asset ⇒ no new clean-room guard case). Both throwaway sessions revoked and re-checked with `SessionStore().exists` → `False`. Owner reviewed live at :3030 and approved ("Looks good"), asking for the commit **and push**.

**README:** "The look" still reads *"boxed by a hot rim with real bloom"* — no numeric claim, so it remains true after tightening; left unchanged (offering to say *tight halo* was declined by omission).

**Tuning harness (not committed):** `.qwen/tmp/f3/bloom-lab2.py` (isolated per-candidate bloom lab), `dial-measure.py` (the four-dial probe: mottle / rim / bloom / label, works on any located box), `dial-find.py` (locates a red button by scanning for red-dominant runs), `dial-live.py` (captures live control geometry + screenshots). Remaining dials from R71 that this pass deliberately did **not** touch: fill mottle drama, rim thickness, label size/tracking, footer reel size, footer pipe separators.

**Decisions:** R72 in §4 below.

### M6 artwork polish — the film page's metadata stops falling into the right-hand gutter on a phone (executed 2026-09-20, commit `baafb25`)

**Owner's item (2026-09-20)**, raised with a phone screenshot at `design-elements/REELTALK-MOBILE.jpg`: *"The type for the Runtime, Genres, Directors, and Cast is all squeezed to the right side when viewing a film page on mobile. I want to fix this so that it flows better and is not all squeezed together to the right."*

**Measured before designing.** The screenshot is DPR 3 (1080px wide → 360 CSS px); its pixel bands matched a true-390 probe of the deployed page to within a couple of px, so the reference and our render were the same numbers and one table covers both:

| band | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| `.film` | flex **row**, gap 20 | flex **column**, gap 18.4 |
| poster | 160 wide, x 16 | 160 wide, **centred** (x 115) |
| `.film-meta` | **178** wide (x 196) | **358** wide (x 16) |
| `dt` | 96 (`min-width: 6rem`) | full width, `min-width: 0` |
| value column `dd` | x 300, **74px** | x 16, **358px** |
| Runtime / Genres / Directors | 1 / 3 / 2 lines | 1 / 1 / 1 lines |
| Cast | **13 lines** (widest 72.3px) | **3 lines** (widest 344.5px) |
| `scrollWidth` vs `clientWidth` | 390 = 390 | 390 = 390 |

**The cause was structural, not a stray width.** `.film` is a `display: flex` row that never broke: the poster (160) + gap (20) left `.film-meta` 178px, and inside it `.meta-list dt { min-width: 6rem }` claimed 96 of those plus an 8px gap — leaving every value **74px**, narrower than a single cast name. Nothing on mobile was misconfigured; mobile simply had no rules for this block.

**What landed (CSS only, one file, +37).** A new `@media (max-width: 40rem)` block placed with the film-page rules, breaking at the same 40rem band the header (R62/R69) and footer (R71) already use:

- `.film` → `flex-direction: column`, with `.film-poster { align-self: center }` and a centred `h1`, so poster + title read as one **centred plate** instead of a small image stranded at the left edge of a 358px column.
- Each `.meta-list` row becomes `display: block`: the `dt` drops `min-width` and becomes a small uppercase tracked label (`0.72rem` / `.14em`, still `--muted`) — a slate field, in the Oswald metadata face it already carried — and the `dd` takes the whole column at `line-height: 1.55`, with `0.8rem` above each row so a long cast list breathes.
- **Scoped to `.film .meta-list`** on purpose: `templates/about.html` shares `.meta-list`, and its three short values read fine in two columns. Verified untouched at 390 (`display: flex`, `min-width: 96px`, `text-transform: none`).

**Deliberately left alone:** the poster stays 160×240, so there is dark space either side of it in the 358px column — flagged to the owner as its own dial rather than folded into this fix. Desktop untouched: at 1440 the block still computes `flex row`, `dt` 96px, cast 324px over 3 lines.

**Verification (:3030, 2026-09-20, off the rebuilt container).** `docker compose build web && up -d web`, then the film page probed at a **true 390** through a same-origin iframe wrapper (`.qwen/tmp/f3/meta-390.py`; `--window-size` clamps at ~500px, so a real phone width is only reachable this way). Every number in the table above came from that probe, not from the stylesheet. No horizontal overflow before or after. The session was minted with all three keys (`SESSION_KEY` + `HASH_SESSION_KEY` + `BACKEND_SESSION_KEY`, via `.qwen/tmp/f3/mint-session.py` — needs `DJANGO_SETTINGS_MODULE=reeltalk.settings`, `get_user_model()` for the swapped `social.User`, and `get_session_auth_hash()`), and the signed-in state was asserted on the page itself (`Log out` + `Remove from Watchlist` present) before any measurement, per R72's lesson. **Gate (fresh background run):** `ruff format --check` — *80 files already formatted*; `ruff check` — *All checks passed!*; `makemigrations --check` — *No changes detected*; pytest **758 passed + 5 skipped in 328.32s** — unchanged from the `8b14a0e` baseline (CSS-only, no new asset ⇒ no new clean-room guard case). Owner reviewed live at :3030 and approved ("Looks good"), asking for the commit **and push**.

**README:** "The look" gains a **Film page** bullet — the centred plate and the stacked label-over-value fields — since this is a visible surface with no prior entry there.

**Dial left open:** mobile poster size (still 160×240 inside a 358px column).

**Decisions:** R73 in §4 below.

### Deploy-readiness increment 1 — trust + cookie hardening (executed 2026-09-20, commits `abbec07`, `62a4d5d`)

Opened the real-domain deploy phase (owner brief 2026-09-20: `https://reeltalk.minnix.dev`, Cloudflare DNS-only, TLS at nginx proxy manager `192.168.1.141`, invite-only, fresh DB). Full detail in **R74**; summary of what landed and what was measured:

- **Proxy scheme gate** — `reeltalk/proxy_trust.py` (`TrustedProxySchemeMiddleware`), first in `MIDDLEWARE`. Confirmed on the deployed image that Django 6.1.1 has **no** `SECURE_TRUSTED_PROXIES` and that `SECURE_PROXY_SSL_HEADER` trusts `X-Forwarded-Proto` from anyone, so the gate is ours: strip the header unless `REMOTE_ADDR` ∈ `TRUSTED_PROXIES`; set `SECURE_PROXY_SSL_HEADER` only when that list is non-empty. Reproduced the old leak live before fixing it (spoofed header → `https://192.168.1.138:3030/…` actor id).
- **Cookies** — `SECURE_COOKIES` (default true) drives `SESSION_COOKIE_SECURE` + `CSRF_COOKIE_SECURE`; `SESSION_COOKIE_SAMESITE = "Lax"` pinned. Unconditional `Secure` killed LAN-HTTP login (verified: `csrftoken` never returned over `http://`, POST 403s), so `reeltalk/cookie_policy.py` (`SchemeAwareCookieMiddleware`, immediately after the gate) sets `Secure` per request scheme; `COOKIES_FOLLOW_SCHEME=false` restores strict.
- **Deleted** dead `BASE_URL` (zero references outside `PROGRESS.md` prose).
- **Measured for the cutover:** Docker preserves the real peer IP (`/proc/net/tcp` showed `remote=192.168.1.138`, not the `172.18.0.1` gateway), so `TRUSTED_PROXIES=192.168.1.141/32` will match NPM; and `_get_raw_host()` appends the port only in the no-`Host`-header branch, so the cutover must confirm no `:3030` leaks into published actor IDs.
- **Pre-flight cutover check:** explicit CNAME → public `97.98.21.119` (not a wildcard), Cloudflare DNS-only, NPM cert `CN=*.minnix.dev` valid to 2026-11-01, port 80 → 301 https, 3030 closed from public, NPM passes the original `Host` (proven via the `DisallowedHost` 400 carrying Django's own headers). `X-Forwarded-Proto` forwarding still **unverified** — nothing observable until `ALLOWED_HOSTS` accepts the domain; the AP actor ID over real HTTPS is the check.
- **Owner relaxed the binding constraint:** `0.0.0.0` is acceptable, so increment 3 will not bind to loopback.
- **Gate:** `790 passed + 5 skipped in 334.10s` (ruff / `format --check` / `makemigrations --check` clean) from a fresh `docker compose run --rm web`; settings and cookie behaviour read back off the **deployed** container, plus a live LAN login on `:3030` with a throwaway account (302 → `/`, `sessionid HttpOnly; SameSite=Lax`, signed-in handle rendered), then deleted.

### Deploy-readiness increment 2 — one trusted scheme (executed 2026-09-20)

Removed the last two places in the app that read `X-Forwarded-Proto` for themselves, so after R74's gate there is exactly one source for the scheme. Reasoning in **R75**; what changed and what was measured:

- **Two sites, one shape** — `reeltalk/activitypub/identity.py`'s `absolute_uri()` (behind every published actor, inbox, outbox, followers/following, film, note and poster URL) and `reeltalk/activitypub/signatures.py`'s `_target_uri()` (the `@target-uri` we rebuild for inbound RFC 9421 verification) each carried `forwarded.split(",")[0].strip() or request.scheme`. Both are now a single f-string over `request.scheme`.
- **The signature site was the load-bearing one** — reading the header there made the URI we compare against what the sender signed movable by whoever sent it, independent of the gate. Now the gate is the only way the scheme moves, so verification and publication cannot disagree.
- **Sole source, checked rather than assumed** — a grep over non-test app code for `build_absolute_uri` / `is_secure()` returns only `reeltalk/cookie_policy.py:34`, which is deliberate and sits one slot after the gate (pinned by `test_the_cookie_policy_runs_right_after_the_gate`). No app code calls `build_absolute_uri`.
- **Live proof over real TCP** (throwaway containers, so the deployed stack's own `TRUSTED_PROXIES=[]` was never loosened): with `TRUSTED_PROXIES=192.168.1.138/32` and a request from the app box carrying `X-Forwarded-Proto: https` + `Host: reeltalk.minnix.dev`, the actor document came back `https://reeltalk.minnix.dev/user/minnix/`, inbox `https://reeltalk.minnix.dev/user/minnix/inbox/`, sharedInbox `https://reeltalk.minnix.dev/inbox/` — **no `:3030`**, i.e. the R74 port-append trap stays shut because a real `Host` header is present. Same container with no forwarded header → `http://reeltalk.minnix.dev/…` (real transport). A second container trusting only `192.168.1.141/32`, hit with the same spoofed header from `.138`, stayed `http://reeltalk.minnix.dev/…`.
- **Deployed readback** (`:3030`, new image): no `headers.get("X-Forwarded-Proto"` anywhere under `reeltalk/activitypub/`; `TRUSTED_PROXIES=[]`, `SECURE_PROXY_SSL_HEADER=None`; a spoofed `X-Forwarded-Proto: https` from the LAN still yields `http://192.168.1.138:3030/user/minnix/` for id/inbox/outbox/followers/following/sharedInbox.
- **Tests** — `test_activitypub.py`'s `_signed_request` now mirrors the URL's own transport (`secure=`), so an https-signed target arrives over https; the old `test_verify_rfc9421_follows_forwarded_proto` became three cases: https-signed over https verifies with no header present at all, https-signed over plain http with the header claiming https does **not** verify, and http-signed over plain http still verifies with a stray header riding along (the header is inert, not merely distrusted). `test_proxy_trust.py` gained a `verify_signed_inbox_post(remote_addr, forwarded)` section — one fixed signature, varying peer — proving the gate/`@target-uri` composition end to end. `test_activitypub_follow.py`'s self-verification round trip sets `secure=True` instead of the header.
- **Worker left on the old image on purpose** — federation delivery is request-synchronous from `reeltalk/core/views.py` (`broadcast.py` takes the caller's `request`); `reeltalk/core/tasks.py` only enqueues the TMDB backfill, so nothing scheme-dependent runs in the worker. Recreate it with everything else at the cutover.
- **Gate:** `ruff check` + `ruff format --check` (84 files) clean, `makemigrations --check` clean (no model change), pytest **794 passed + 5 skipped in 344.45s** from a fresh `docker compose run --rm web` on the rebuilt image — baseline 790 + 4 new cases.

### Deploy-readiness increments 3 & 4 — port exposure documented, signup CTAs follow the policy (executed 2026-09-20)

Reasoning in **R76**. Increment 3 turned out to be documentation-only; increment 4 is the code change.

**Increment 3 — plain-HTTP exposure (docs only).** `docker-compose.yml` publishes `${WEB_PORT:-3030}:3030` with no bind address, i.e. `0.0.0.0`, and the owner has accepted that, so nothing here changes the binding. `.env.example` now states the firewall expectation under the `WEB_PORT` block, including the trap that R74's gate does **not** cover: a publicly reachable `WEB_PORT` lets anyone open a cleartext session that skips the terminator's certificate entirely, and the forwarded-scheme gate cannot help because it decides whom to *believe* about the scheme, never whom to *let in*. No code path changed; R74's pre-flight already measured 3030 closed/filtered from the public IP.

**Increment 4 — the four policy-blind signup CTAs.** Four anonymous surfaces hard-linked to `/signup/` regardless of policy, so an invite-only instance still advertised signup everywhere and the click landed on `signup.html`'s "not accepting new signups" notice. `site` reached templates only where a view happened to pass it (`social/views.py` index/about), so there was nothing for the templates to check.

- **One new context processor** — `reeltalk/social/context_processors.py` exposes a single boolean `signup_open`, derived exactly as `signup()` gates it and as nodeinfo already derives `openRegistration`, so the three cannot drift. Deliberately **not** the `SiteSettings` row: the CTAs need one bit, and handing a template the settings object invites it to render what the view never approved. Registered last in `TEMPLATES[0]["OPTIONS"]["context_processors"]`.
- **All four surfaces adapted** — `base.html` header keeps `Log in` and drops `Sign up`; `home.html` landing becomes `Log in if you have an account.`; `welcome.html` and `login.html` drop the line entirely. Closed means **hidden**, not redirected.
- **First-run unaffected** — a fresh instance defaults to `open`, so the CTA still shows before any admin exists and still routes the operator to `/setup/`. No special case needed.
- **Live verification on the deployed container** (`:3030`, rebuilt image), not the tree. Signup links per surface, `open` → `invite` → restored `open`:

  | Surface | open | invite | restored |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | `/` | 2 | 0 | 2 |
  | `/welcome/` | 2 | 0 | 2 |
  | `/login/` | 2 | 0 | 2 |
  | `/about/` (header only) | 1 | 0 | 1 |

  Login links were unchanged across all three states (`2/1/1/1`) — hiding signup does not hide the door in. `/signup/` itself still rendered *"not accepting new signups right now."* while closed and a `POST` returned **403** with no account created. Policy was flipped back to `open`, its original value, and the CTAs came back.
- **Gate:** `ruff check` / `ruff format --check` / `makemigrations --check` clean, pytest **805 passed + 5 skipped in 341.67s** from a fresh `docker compose run --rm web` on the rebuilt image — baseline 794 **+11** (10 new cases + 1 clean-room case for the new module, which reports **100%** coverage).

**Measured on the way: the public path already reaches the app.** The owner asked whether `https://reeltalk.minnix.dev` should work yet. It should not — but probing it pinned down the whole chain ahead of the cutover:

- DNS resolves (`reeltalk.minnix.dev` CNAME → `minnix.dev` → `97.98.21.119`); TLS terminates cleanly on `CN=*.minnix.dev` (Let's Encrypt `YE2`, valid to **2026-11-01**); `server: openresty` answers, so NPM is in the path.
- The request **arrives at our container**: `docker logs` shows `192.168.1.141:35580 - "GET / HTTP/1.1" 400 Bad Request`. That is a second, independent confirmation — over the real public path rather than `/proc/net/tcp` — that **NPM presents as `192.168.1.141`**, exactly the address `TRUSTED_PROXIES=192.168.1.141/32` must match.
- The 400 is Django's own `DisallowedHost` (Django's security headers ride the response), because the deployed `ALLOWED_HOSTS` is `['localhost', '127.0.0.1', '192.168.1.138']`, derived from `DOMAIN='192.168.1.138:3030'`. Setting `DOMAIN=reeltalk.minnix.dev` is what admits it.
- **`X-Forwarded-Proto` forwarding is still unverified** — unchanged from R74. The 400 happens before anything scheme-dependent renders, so nothing observable. The https actor ID over real HTTPS remains the check, at the cutover.

### Deploy-readiness increment 5 — the restore path, built then drilled (executed 2026-09-20)

Reasoning in **R77**. The brief's headline finding was confirmed before anything was written: `backup_database` was the only management command in `reeltalk/core/management/commands/`, and `pg_restore` appeared nowhere in non-test code (only in docs prose and the Dockerfile's license note). So this increment could not "run a restore and check it" — the restore had to be **built** before it could be drilled.

**The decision was put to the owner before building** (command vs hand-driven `pg_restore` vs a command that cannot touch the live DB; throwaway DB in the existing cluster vs a separate container). Chosen: **a `restore_database` command, drilled against a throwaway database in the same cluster.**

- **Asymmetry is the whole design** — dumping the live database is harmless; restoring over it destroys the owner's imported watchlist. So the target is never defaulted: `--into <db>` is **required**, naming the live database additionally requires `--force`, and the target must already exist — the command never creates one, so a typo is a loud connection failure rather than a fresh empty database nobody is looking at.
- **pg_restore flags** — `--exit-on-error` (fail loudly, not pg_restore's default of carrying on and exiting 0), `--single-transaction` (all-or-nothing: a mid-way failure leaves the target empty, not half-loaded), `--no-owner` (drops the dump's `ALTER OWNER` so a restore under a different role than the dumper still works). Connection params come from Django's live settings like `backup_database`; the password travels in `PGPASSWORD`, never argv.
- **The drill, on the real stack, never touching live:**

  | Step | Result |
  |---|---|
  | Fresh dump via `backup` service | `reeltalk-20260921T134126Z.dump`, 693,255 bytes |
  | Restore into `reeltalk_drill` | Clean |
  | Per-table row counts vs live | **24 public tables identical** — 1385 films / 1385 shelf films / 9 statuses / 2 users / 2 follows |
  | App booted against the restored DB (`-e POSTGRES_DB=reeltalk_drill`, `:3132`) | entrypoint reported `No migrations to apply` (the dump carries a current schema), `GET /` → 200, `GET /film/7/` → `<title>Take the Trash (2008)</title>` |
  | Teardown | container stopped, `DROP DATABASE` done, cluster back to `postgres`/`reeltalk`/`template0`/`template1`, nothing left on `:3132` |
  | **Live after teardown** | **row counts diffed identical to the pre-drill baseline** |

- **Guards verified live, not only in tests** — `--into reeltalk` refused with a message naming `--force`; `--into ""` refused; missing `--into` refused; a nonexistent dump path refused before any database was touched. The echoed command line shows no password.
- **Gotcha found and documented: each service has its own image tag.** `docker compose build web` does not update `backup`, so the first restore attempt died with `Unknown command: 'restore_database'` — the same no-bind-mount trap as everything else here, now on a service people will actually reach for in an emergency. `DEPLOYING.md` §7 covers it.
- **`DEPLOYING.md` written** — topology, prerequisites (incl. the LE wildcard cert expiring 2026-11-01 and why Cloudflare must stay grey-cloud), a full env table, NPM's must-forward list, the port-binding/firewall expectation, first-deploy steps, the build gotcha, backup procedure, the restore procedure with the drill recipe, invite-only setup, a post-deploy verification checklist, and the known limits named rather than hidden.
- **`.env.example` had a footgun** — its backup section told the operator to restore with `pg_restore -d reeltalk <dump file>`, i.e. straight into the live database. Replaced with a pointer to the command and DEPLOYING.md §9.
- **A live defect on the invite-only path, found while checking that "an admin creates the account" was documentable.** `User` was registered with a bare `admin.ModelAdmin` with no password handling. Verified on the deployed image before the fix: the add form exposed the model's `password` column as a plain **visible** `AdminTextInputWidget` (not even masked), required, with no `clean_password`/`save_model` hashing — so `COLUMN VALUE = 'hunter2'`, `HASHED = False`, `LOGIN WOULD WORK = False`. Creating a user from `/admin/` wrote plaintext to the column **and** produced an account that could not log in. Owner pulled the fix into this increment.
  - **Fix** — `reeltalk/social/admin.py` now uses Django's auth forms: `InviteUserCreationForm` (hashes via `set_password`, restates signup's 30-char cap and `LOCALNAME_RE`, rejects case-insensitive duplicates) for add, `AdminUserChangeForm` for change (its `password` field is a read-only hash field whose `clean_password` returns the stored value, so plaintext cannot be written from the admin). `private_key` is on **no** form at all; `localname` is read-only after creation (renaming a federated identity orphans every published URL and invalidates other instances' mirrors); `raw_summary`/`summary` are read-only because `summary` is rendered by the profile-edit view, not by `save()`, so editing the raw source in admin would desync them permanently.
  - **Verified on the deployed container** — the add form now exposes only `display_name`, `email`, `localname`, `password1`, `password2`, both password fields `PasswordInput`, and `'password' in add.fields` is **False** (the exact reversal of the finding).
- **Tests** — `reeltalk/tests/test_restore.py` (18 cases: argv shape, the live-target guard including exact case-sensitivity, password-in-env-not-argv via a stubbed subprocess, refusal-before-invoking-anything, plus a real round trip that dumps the pytest DB, creates a scratch DB, restores, and compares the whole per-table row-count map, and proves the scratch DB does not survive the suite) and `reeltalk/tests/test_admin_users.py` (14 cases through the real admin HTTP endpoints: hashed creation, no plaintext left on the row, the created account authenticates, no privileges granted, D1 shelves still created, unusable/duplicate localname rejected, confirmation mismatch rejected, an edit cannot swap the hash for plaintext, an edit cannot rename, `private_key` on no form, federation fields not submittable).
- **Three of my own restore tests failed the first gate** (an off-by-one on `argv[-2:]` vs `argv[-3:]`, `argv[argv.index("-p")]` instead of `+ 1`, and expecting `SystemExit` where Django's `OptionParser` raises `CommandError`). All were test bugs, not command bugs — fixed and re-gated rather than loosened.
- **Gate:** `ruff check` / `ruff format --check` / `makemigrations --check` clean, pytest **841 passed + 5 skipped** from a fresh `docker compose run --rm web` on the rebuilt image — baseline 805 **+36** (18 restore + 14 admin + 4 clean-room cases for the two new files).

### Contributor attribution re-clean (executed 2026-09-20, force-push `83d0f91 → 196eb25`)

The owner reported that GitHub account **`qwencoder`** ("Qwen-Coder") was showing as a contributor on `minnixtx/reeltalk`, and that blocking them from the profile made them vanish briefly and then **reappear**.

**Root cause.** Three commits on `main` from 2026-09-16 still carried the trailer `Co-authored-by: Qwen-Coder <qwen-coder@alibabacloud.com>`, and that address maps to the unrelated GitHub account `qwencoder` (id 224605497). GitHub's contributors graph **parses `Co-authored-by:` trailers** and credits whatever account the email resolves to, so the account gained a contributor entry without ever committing. **This was not a regression of the 2026-09-09 fix** — that rewrite did clean the then-current four M1 commits, which now survive only in the local, never-pushed `backup/pre-attribution-fix` tag and the `refs/notes/ai-attribution` notes. The three M6 artwork commits were made a week *later* and re-introduced the trailer, so the entry came back. **Why blocking did nothing:** blocking/unblocking a user does not strip attribution from a public repo's history — the contributors graph is recomputed periodically and on each push, and each rebuild simply re-parsed the trailers. Only removing the trailers fixes it.

**The re-clean.** `git filter-branch --msg-filter` (a small python filter at `.qwen/tmp/strip-trailer.py` that drops the exact trailer line and the blank lines left behind) over the range `2ab2dfb~1..main`.

**Safety verification (all passed before pushing):** trailer count on `main` **0**; the email appears **0** times anywhere in `main`'s messages; `git diff --stat backup/pre-attribution-fix-2 main` **empty** — trees byte-identical, so no code changed and the running container needed no rebuild; commit count unchanged at **129**; the first **96** commits are byte-identical (`main~96` == `backup/pre-attribution-fix-2~96` == `ecde8e2`). New SHAs for the three offenders: `2ab2dfb → 36af2cd`, `7fed7aa → fe2a36f`, `411dad8 → 6eaf197`; **35 of 129** SHAs changed in total (the 3 plus their 32 descendants). Pushed with `--force-with-lease` (`83d0f91...196eb25`, forced update).

**Post-push confirmation via the API:** scanning all 129 commits on GitHub's `main` finds **none** containing the trailer, and `GET /contributors?anon=1` returns only `minnixtx`. The Insights → Contributors graph can lag the REST list by up to ~a day; if the entry persists after that it is cached graph data and only GitHub Support can purge it. Old commit objects may also remain reachable by their old SHA until GitHub's garbage collection runs.

**Standing rule, restated because it has now bitten twice:** **no `Co-authored-by` trailer of any kind** on any commit in this repo (owner's final call, 2026-09-09 — see the attribution note in memory). The rule was known and the slip still happened on fresh commits, so treat "did this commit get a trailer?" as a check to verify **before every push**, not something to trust. Rollback refs kept locally and never pushed: `backup/pre-attribution-fix-2` (pre-rewrite tip) and filter-branch's own `refs/original/refs/heads/main`.

**Guard installed the same day (owner: "do what you can to make sure this will never happen again").** Three layers, all exercised before committing:

1. **`.githooks/commit-msg`** — rejects any commit whose message carries a co-author trailer. Tested: the exact qwen trailer, plus `CO-AUTHORED-BY:`, `co-authored-by :` (space before colon), indented `  Co-Authored-By:` and `coauthored-by:` — **all blocked**; a clean message commits normally.
2. **`.githooks/pre-push`** — scans exactly the refs being pushed (git's `<local_ref> <local_sha> <remote_ref> <remote_sha>` stdin format) and refuses the whole push if any commit in the range carries a trailer, so an amend / cherry-pick / merge that smuggles one in is caught at the boundary. Tested: pushing the old pre-rewrite history was **blocked with the three offenders named**; pushing current `main` passed.
3. **`.github/workflows/attribution-guard.yml`** — the layer `--no-verify` cannot skip. Scans the pushed range, falling back to the whole branch when `github.event.before` is absent/unknown (new branch, force-push, initial push). Logic verified locally against a range containing the offenders (fails, names them) and a clean range (passes).

**Matching on the whole trailer family, not one string:** all three layers use the pattern `^[[:space:]]*(co-?authored-by|coauthor-by)[[:space:]]*:` case-insensitively, so renaming the address or re-spacing the key does not slip past it. The rule is *no co-author trailers at all*, which is what makes it robust — a blocklist of specific emails would need updating every time.

**Enablement:** `core.hooksPath` is per-clone local config, so `.githooks/install.sh` sets it (idempotent, verifies executability) and **`setup.sh` now calls it** during bootstrap. README carries a "Commit hygiene: no co-author trailers" note under AI Disclosure explaining the mechanism and telling contributors to run the installer after cloning.

**One action left for the owner (cannot be scripted):** the CI layer is advisory until marked required — **Settings → Branches → branch protection for `main` → Require a status check to pass → `attribution-guard`**. Until then a `--no-verify` push can still land with a red check rather than being refused.

**Gate (fresh background run):** `ruff format --check` — *80 files already formatted*; `ruff check` — *All checks passed!*; `makemigrations --check` — clean; pytest **758 passed + 5 skipped in 330.30s** — baseline 754 **+4**, one clean-room guard case for each of the four new files (`.githooks/commit-msg`, `.githooks/pre-push`, `.githooks/install.sh`, `.github/workflows/attribution-guard.yml`), which is the expected movement and confirms the new files are inside the guard's scan.

### Clean-room guard (added 2026-09-07)

Owner asked for assurance that no BookWyrm code is in the repo. Verified clean: git provenance (16 commits since the AGPLv3 seed `0f3c520`, no BookWyrm/legacy ancestry, single remote `minnixtx/reeltalk`), zero BookWyrm markers in any `.py`/template/static file or dependency — the only mentions are the attribution prose REWRITE.md rule 5 requires. To keep that guarantee self-enforcing, `reeltalk/tests/test_clean_room.py` scans every project file for telltale strings (`bookwyrm`, `mouse reeve`, `anti-capitalist`, word-boundary `acrl`, case-insensitive). Discovery is a filesystem walk — the suite also runs inside the built image where there is no `.git`; top-level `static/`+`images/` (build artifacts/volumes), caches, `.venv`, and gitignored local files are skipped. Only the four attribution docs and the guard itself are whitelisted, deliberately by hand. Suite baseline: **145 passed + 5 skipped** (the whitelist).

### Deploy-readiness increment 6 — the cutover (executed 2026-09-21)

Reasoning in **R78** (the cutover) and **R79** (the three tests that turned out to be passing by accident). This is the increment the whole deploy-readiness phase was building toward: the instance moved from a LAN IP with no TLS to the real public origin.

**Four decisions were raised to the owner before anything destructive, not picked silently** — how to get the fresh DB, whether to take the instance down, what happens to instance B, and the ordering of the unverified `X-Forwarded-Proto` check relative to the destructive step. Chosen: **rename the old DB aside in place**, **a short planned outage**, **retire instance B**, and **probe the scheme before destroying anything**. The last one is the ordering that mattered most — the brief's own sequence verified the scheme *last*, after the database was already gone.

**Measured before touching anything:**

- **Cert re-checked live rather than trusted:** `CN=*.minnix.dev`, issuer Let's Encrypt `YE2`, valid 2026-08-03 → **2026-11-01** (41 days). DNS `reeltalk.minnix.dev` → `minnix.dev` → `97.98.21.119`.
- **What is actually IP-keyed, rather than what R52 was assumed to mean.** A full text-column scan of the live database found **12** occurrences of the LAN IP, and **every one of them was an instance-B mirror or test debris**: `social_user` id 8 (`fedb@192.168.1.138:3031`), `core_film` 1390/1391 (one literally named `Poster Reverse Test`), `core_status` 8/11, and 5 `activitypub_deliveredactivity` ids including a `throwb` leftover. The owner's own `minnix` account has **empty `actor_url` and `inbox_url`** — both derived from `DOMAIN` at runtime. So R52 holds for the owner's data and fresh + re-import is genuinely clean; the only thing tying the database to the old address was the federation test peer.
- **A brief discrepancy worth correcting:** the running container was believed to have `TRUSTED_PROXIES=192.168.1.141/32` and `SECURE_COOKIES=true`. Measured: `TRUSTED_PROXIES = []`, `SECURE_PROXY_SSL_HEADER = None`, `SECURE_COOKIES = True` (the *default*, not set). Neither key existed in `.env` at all. With an empty trust list the gate strips the forwarded header from everyone including the terminator, so the cutover had to **add** these, not confirm them.

**The scheme probe — run against the existing database, before any destructive step.** `DOMAIN`/`ALLOWED_HOSTS`/`CSRF_TRUSTED_ORIGINS`/`TRUSTED_PROXIES` were set on the live container with the old data still in place (fully reversible by an env flip), and the actor document was read over real HTTPS:

```
"id":           https://reeltalk.minnix.dev/user/minnix/
"inbox":        https://reeltalk.minnix.dev/user/minnix/inbox/
"sharedInbox":  https://reeltalk.minnix.dev/inbox/
```

https, **no `:3030`**, on every published URL including the keyid. **NPM does forward `X-Forwarded-Proto: https`** — the one link that R74, R75 and R76 each had to leave unverified because the `DisallowedHost` 400 fired before anything scheme-dependent could render. The negative case was taken in the same breath: the identical spoofed header sent from the LAN directly returns `http://192.168.1.138:3030/...`, so the gate is doing its job in both directions on the real stack. Had this come back `http`, the plan was to stop and fix NPM with the old database still intact.

**The sequence, and what each step produced:**

| Step | Result |
|---|---|
| Rebuild **all three** images | `web`, `worker`, `backup` — the R77 gotcha honoured rather than assumed |
| Drill the restore first | fresh dump `reeltalk-20260921T154527Z.dump` (693,255 bytes) → throwaway DB → **24 tables, row-for-row identical to live** → probe dropped |
| Scheme probe (old DB intact) | **passed** — see above |
| Retire instance B | `reeltalk-b` project removed; `reeltalk-b_pgdata` / `_media` / `_static` **kept**. Done *before* the DB swap so B could not deliver stale activity into the fresh database |
| Stop `web` + `worker` + `backup` | confirmed zero connections to `reeltalk` before renaming |
| `ALTER DATABASE reeltalk RENAME TO reeltalk_legacy_20260921` | old data intact in-cluster; **rollback is a rename back, no restore required** |
| `CREATE DATABASE reeltalk` | matching the original exactly: owner `reeltalk`, `UTF8`, `en_US.utf8` |
| Bring the stack up | all migrations applied clean, collectstatic ran, stack healthy |
| `signup_policy = invite` | set immediately — a fresh instance defaults to `open`, so the public door was open for minutes and closing it was the first thing done after migrations |
| First account | owner ran `/setup/` themselves so the password never passed through this session; `minnix`, `is_superuser=True`, password hashed |
| Import `watchlist.csv` | **1670 rows → 1378 created / 292 skipped (not a movie (tv))**, backfill queued — matches the M3 exit bar exactly |
| D11 backfill | **1367 / 1378 posters**, queue drained (11 titles have no poster upstream) |

**The import went through the service layer, not a browser.** `/preferences/import/` is a thin wrapper — utf-8-sig decode, `parse_tmdb_csv()`, `import_film_csv(user, rows)` — so the import ran as `manage.py shell` calls against those two functions with the checksum verified on both sides (`d40983e12932766ca398457b846b44b7`). Same code path, no session, no CSRF dance, and no password in a command line. The temp copy inside the container was removed afterwards.

**Post-deploy verification, all five §11 checks:**

1. **Actor ID https with no `:3030`** — ✅ (above).
2. **Spoofed header from an untrusted peer is inert** — ✅ `http://192.168.1.138:3030/...`.
3. **LAN plain-HTTP login still works** — ✅ over `http://` the `csrftoken` is set **without** `Secure` (`SameSite=Lax` preserved); over real `https://` the same cookie carries `Secure`. The permanent plain-HTTP LAN requirement survives the cutover intact.
4. **Signup CTAs gone under invite** — ✅ `0` signup links on `/`, `/welcome/`, `/login/`, `/setup/`; login links untouched. `/signup/` 302s to `/setup/`.
5. **`3030` closed from outside** — ✅ connection refused to `97.98.21.119:3030`.
6. **nodeinfo** — `openRegistration: false`.

**This increment changed no tracked files.** `.env` is gitignored (`.gitignore:2`), so the cutover itself is pure ops and produced no code diff — the only code that came out of the session is the test fix in R79.

**Rollback position:** `reeltalk_legacy_20260921` holds the entire pre-cutover database, readable directly, and `/tmp/env.pre-cutover.bak` holds the exact previous `.env`. A rollback is a rename back plus that env restored.

### Deploy-readiness increment 6b — three security tests were passing by accident (executed 2026-09-21)

Reasoning in **R79**. The first full gate after the cutover came back **3 failed, 838 passed, 5 skipped in 363.32s** against a tree that had not changed since R77's green run of 841 + 5. The gate was not flaky; setting `TRUSTED_PROXIES` for the real deploy had turned a latent test defect into a visible failure.

**Root cause, established by A/B rather than inferred.** Running the same three tests with `TRUSTED_PROXIES` cleared: **3 passed**. With the production `.env`: **3 failed**. Two separate confusions were stacked:

- **`RequestFactory` defaults `REMOTE_ADDR` to `127.0.0.1`** (measured). So `pass_through(None, "https", trusted=("0.0.0.0/0",))` never had a *missing* remote address at all — it had loopback, which **is** inside `0.0.0.0/0`, so the middleware correctly treated it as trusted and kept the header. `test_a_missing_remote_addr_is_not_trusted` was **vacuous**: it asserted a property of a case it never constructed.
- **`request.scheme` is a cached property read *after* the `override_settings` block exits.** `pass_through` runs the middleware inside the override and returns the request; the assertion then computes the scheme against the **ambient** `SECURE_PROXY_SSL_HEADER`, which the operator's `.env` controls. While no trust list existed that ambient value was `None`, the header was ignored, and the test passed for a reason unrelated to the control.

The two `test_activitypub` RFC 9421 cases had the same shape with higher stakes: they called `verify_request()` on a hand-built request that **never passes through the middleware**, so under a configuration where the header is genuinely believed, the raw function *does* bless the header. Their comments claimed the gate's protection without ever running the gate.

**Production was never vulnerable** — the real composition was verified live in this same session with the middleware actually in the chain: trusted terminator → `https`, same header from the LAN → stays `http`. The control worked; the tests did not model it. That distinction is why this was worth fixing rather than explaining away.

**The fix makes the tests stricter, not looser** (owner's explicit choice among three options):

- `pass_through` now **deletes** `REMOTE_ADDR` when asked for a missing address, and **reads the scheme inside the override** so the cached value is pinned to the settings under test instead of the deploy config.
- `test_activitypub.py` gained a `DEPLOYED` override and a `_through_gate()` helper, and the two RFC 9421 cases now run the request through `TrustedProxySchemeMiddleware` from an untrusted peer — so they assert about the control that actually protects us.

**Non-vacuity proven, not assumed.** A mutation check under the deployed posture: with an **untrusted** peer the header is stripped and `verify_request` returns `False`; with a **trusted** peer the header survives and the same delivery returns `True`. The header is genuinely believed in this configuration, so the `False` is caused by the gate and not by an inert header — which is exactly the property that was silently missing before.

**Hermetic both ways:** `test_proxy_trust.py` + `test_activitypub.py` = **54 passed** with the production `.env`, **54 passed** with `TRUSTED_PROXIES` cleared.

**Gate (fresh `docker compose run --rm web`, production env):** `ruff check` — *All checks passed!*; `ruff format --check` — *89 files already formatted*; `makemigrations --check` — *No changes detected*; pytest **841 passed + 5 skipped in 357.91s**. Back to the R77 baseline, with three of those tests now worth what they say.

**Durable lesson: a test that passes because of an ambient default is worse than no test** — it advertises coverage for a control while depending on the absence of the very setting that control exists to govern. The cutover was the first time this repo ran its suite against a production-shaped `.env`, and it found three. Any test whose assertion depends on a setting the operator can change should either pin that setting explicitly or exercise the code that reads it.

### Deploy-readiness increment 7 — the search surface goes members-only (executed 2026-09-21)

Reasoning in **R80**. `DEPLOYING.md` §12 had been carrying the line *"search_suggest has no login gate — strangers can drive it and burn the shared TMDB quota even with signup closed."* It was written while the instance was still a LAN IP; after the 2026-09-21 cutover it describes something on the public internet. Closing it took four files.

**What changed.** `film_search` gained `@login_required`. `search_suggest` deliberately did **not**: it answers an XHR that parses JSON, so a 302 would hand the dropdown the login page's HTML where it expects a payload — it now returns `JsonResponse({"error": "login required"}, status=401)` from an explicit guard placed **ahead of** the min-length check, so a short anonymous query is refused rather than answered with an empty-but-successful body. The two click-through and watchlist routes were already gated and are untouched. Once both readers are authenticated-only, the `authenticated` local inside `search_suggest` is a constant, so the blocked-ids ternary and the row-`url` ternary collapsed, deleting the anonymous `f"/search/?q={quote(hit.title)}"` branch — and with it the only use of `urllib.parse.quote` in the module, whose import ruff would otherwise flag. `templates/base.html` wraps the whole `<form class="global-search">` in `{% if user.is_authenticated %}`, so the entry point is gone for strangers rather than merely inert; `search.js` needed no change because `initSuggest()` already returns early when the form is absent.

**The header grid was the part where a careless fix looks fine and isn't.** `.header-top` is `grid-template-columns: 1fr minmax(0, 34rem) 1fr`; removing the middle child means auto-placement drops `.header-user` into that 34rem column, so `Log in` renders centered instead of at the right edge — `justify-self: end` only pushes within whatever column it landed in. Pinned with `grid-column: 3` in the base rule and `grid-column: 2` inside the `@media (max-width: 40rem)` rule, which has only two explicit columns. Explicit line numbers rather than `-2` because the last-column line index is breakpoint-dependent and a negative line reads the same at both while resolving differently; a base `3` left unoverridden would open an implicit third track on the phone and leave a gap on the right.

**Verified by measurement, not by assumption** (headless Chromium against the real stylesheet, both variants at both breakpoints): anonymous desktop resolves `grid-column-start: 3` with the handle's right edge at **1200px**, exactly the grid's content edge; anonymous 390px resolves `grid-column-start: 2` with the right edge at **374px**, again the content edge. The member variant lands on the same two numbers (**1200** / **374**), so the corner does not shift when the box appears, and the phone's search row still spans the full 358px. No overlap with the wordmark at either width.
