**Tests.** Two flipped, five re-fixtured, one added. `test_search_page_anonymous_sees_results_without_actions` → `test_search_page_anonymous_redirected_to_login`, which also asserts `len(responses.calls) == 0` with a key configured and `responses` active — the redirect alone doesn't prove the quota wasn't spent, the zero count does. `test_suggest_anonymous_rows_link_to_search_page` → `test_suggest_anonymous_gets_401_json_not_a_login_redirect`, asserting status, `Content-Type: application/json`, the exact body, the zero call count, the short-query-still-401 ordering, **and** a member request to the same route returning 200 + rows in the same test — per R79, the 401 has to be shown to be the gate rather than a broken view. Five existing anonymous tests (`search_page_empty_query_shows_hint`, `search_page_without_key_is_local_only`, `suggest_short_query_returns_empty`, `suggest_local_when_no_key`, `suggest_falls_back_to_local_on_tmdb_failure`) moved from the anonymous `client` fixture to `login` so they keep testing what they are named for. New: `test_header_search_box_hidden_for_anonymous_shown_for_members`, asserting both halves on separate `Client` instances against one URL. It probes `/user/alice/` rather than `/` because a fresh test database has no admin and `index` sends that state to `/setup/` — the first run of this test failed on exactly that, a 302 with an empty body, caught by the gate rather than shipped.

**Gate** (fresh `docker compose run --rm web` on the rebuilt image, all services rebuilt): `ruff check` — *All checks passed!*; `ruff format --check` — *89 files already formatted*; `makemigrations --check` — *No changes detected*; pytest **842 passed + 5 skipped in 365.39s** — baseline 841 + 1 (the new header case).

**Verified live over real HTTPS after deploy:**

```
GET /search/suggest/?q=blade        → 401, application/json, {"error": "login required"}
GET /search/?q=blade                → 302 https://reeltalk.minnix.dev/login/?next=/search/%3Fq%3Dblade
GET /search/                        → 302 https://reeltalk.minnix.dev/login/?next=/search/
GET / (anonymous header)            → 0 occurrences of "global-search"; brand + "Log in" only
```

And the standing LAN constraint re-checked on the same deploy rather than assumed: `http://192.168.1.138:3030/` still **200**, `/search/suggest/` **401**, `/search/` **302** — the gate is scheme-independent and plain-HTTP LAN access is unaffected.

**One loose end, raised and settled.** `templates/welcome.html` step 1 still links to `{% url 'film-search' %}`, so an anonymous visitor to `/welcome/` who clicks it now lands on the login page. **Owner asked 2026-09-21 and chose to leave it:** that page is a tour of a members-only loop and, under invite-only, offers an anonymous reader no signup CTA anyway, so a bounce to login is consistent with the door being closed.

### Deploy-readiness increment 8 — invite links, and the door was found open (executed 2026-09-21, commit `fabe050`)

Reasoning in **R82**. The instance was shut against the public internet with no way for a member to bring anyone in except by asking the owner to hand-type an account into the admin. This is the mechanism `SiteSettings`' own docstring had been pointing at as future work since before the cutover.

**What changed.** New `Invite` model plus `SiteSettings.invite_scope` (`admins` default / `all`), minted from a new block on the user's own profile and landed on a new public `/invite/<code>/` signup page. Eleven files, one migration (`0008`, additive: one column and one table, no data touched).

**The finding that outranks the feature.** While verifying the deploy the policy was *read* rather than trusted, and it came back `signup_policy = open` with `openRegistration = true` — the live instance was accepting account creation from anyone. The cutover record says `invite` was set immediately after the cutover migrations; the likely cause is increment 4's live test flipping the policy to `invite` and back to `open` to prove the CTA toggles both ways, and never being set back. **Owner confirmed 2026-09-21 it was deliberate at the time** — they had opened it to see what the page looked like to an anonymous visitor who wanted to sign up — **and had it set back to Invite-only.** The durable point is not who flipped it: a security-relevant setting that lives in a database row is invisible to a code review, so the deploy check has to *read the live value* rather than restate the recorded one. That is now how the live verification in this file is written.

**The lock is the feature, not the code.** `Invite.lock_live(code)` takes a `select_for_update` row lock and the signup view holds it inside the same `transaction.atomic()` that creates the account and calls `invite.redeem(user)`. Check-then-mark as two statements is true until two strangers open one link in the same second; on an invite-only instance that is a seat the owner never approved. Per **R79** the lock is *shown* rather than asserted: `test_lock_live_is_a_real_row_lock_not_a_plain_filter` runs under `transaction=True` and requires `TransactionManagementError` when called outside an atomic block — if `lock_live` were a bare `.filter().first()` it would return quietly and the single-use guarantee would be a comment instead of a property.

**Two shapes worth keeping in mind.** *(a)* The mint is `POST`-only because it is not idempotent — a `GET` that mints lets a page render or a browser prefetch spend someone's invite. *(b)* The mint redirects back to the inviter's own profile with `?invite=<code>`, and rendering that code is gated again in `_invite_context` on the code belonging to the requester. Without that second gate the profile becomes an oracle that confirms whether a handed-in code exists, so a guessed code could be validated by observation.

**Two environment constraints shaped the copy button.** `CSP_SCRIPT_SRC` is `'self'` with no `'unsafe-inline'`, so an inline handler would be silently blocked and the button would do nothing — the JS lives in `static/js/invite.js`. And `navigator.clipboard` only exists in a secure context, which the permanently-plain-HTTP LAN origin is not, so the select-and-`execCommand` fallback is the *required* path there rather than a courtesy for old browsers.

**Also fixed, because it is what prompted the look.** Every `<a class="btn">` rendered as underlined text while its `<button class="btn">` siblings did not: the sheet set the control's colour and fill but never overrode the browser's underline on anchors. Fixed on the shared `button, .btn` rule so the tag stops mattering, which also de-underlines the film page's `Edit` and the two `Sign up` CTAs. Measured rather than eyeballed, headless Chromium against the real stylesheet at 1280px and 390px: `a.btn` → `text-decoration-line: none`, `button.btn` → `none`, with a plain `<a>` in the same page still computing `underline` as the control that proves the probe can see an underline at all. The first probe run reported `underline` for everything and was wrong for a reason worth remembering: `page.setContent()` leaves the document at `about:blank`, which refuses to load `file://` subresources, so the stylesheet never applied and every number was the browser default wearing our clothes. Fixed by writing the probe page to disk and navigating to it, and by asserting `.invite-box`'s computed background so a silently-missing stylesheet cannot pass again.

**Gate** (fresh `docker compose run --rm web`, all services rebuilt): `ruff check` — *All checks passed!*; `ruff format --check` — *90 files already formatted*; `makemigrations --check` — *No changes detected*; pytest **882 passed + 5 skipped in 395.72s** — 35 of them new, no pre-existing test changed state.

**Verified live on `reeltalk.minnix.dev` over real HTTPS.**

```
200  /                                   0 invite boxes for anonymous
200  /invite/no-such-code-000/          "That invite link is not valid."
404  /invite/not!valid!code!/           charset refused at the route, no lookup
302  /search/                           R80 holds
401  /search/suggest/?q=blade           application/json, R80 holds
200  http://192.168.1.138:3030/        LAN plain-HTTP holds

minted as minnix → landing page "minnix invited you to join ReelTalk" + form
POST signup      → 302 /welcome/
created_by = minnix | used_by = r82probe | used_at = 2026-09-21 20:47:42Z | live = False
second stranger, same link → no form offered:
  "That invite link has already been used — each one opens exactly one account."
```

Two curl artifacts that are *not* product bugs, recorded so a future session doesn't chase them: the first POST 403'd because Django does strict `Referer` checking over HTTPS and `curl` sends none; and the 64-char form token against the 32-char cookie is Django's CSRF masking working as designed, not a mismatch. Probe account and invite deleted afterwards — `Invite.objects.count() = 0`, no stray accounts.

**After the policy was set to Invite-only:** `openRegistration = False`; `/signup/` renders the closed notice with no form; a `POST` to `/signup/` returns 200 with no account created; all four CTA surfaces carry `signup=0` with `login` intact (`/` 2, `/welcome/` 1, `/login/` 1, `/about/` 1); and a freshly minted link still renders the signup form, which is the policy-independence property holding under the real closed setting rather than only in a test. Cert re-checked rather than trusted: `CN=*.minnix.dev`, `notAfter=Nov 1 02:26:19 2026 GMT`.

**Docs.** `DEPLOYING.md` §10 rewritten — it had been asserting *"Invite-only in v0.1 means closed: an admin creates the account. There are no invite links or codes,"* which this change makes false, and its `POST /signup/` status was wrong too (documented **403**, actually **200** — the gate is the view declining, not the CSRF layer). Same interest-rate lesson recorded in R80: a limit written down before a deploy is a debt, and this one was two sentences from being trusted as an operating instruction.

### Deploy-readiness increment 8b — the R82 comment printed itself on every admin's profile (executed 2026-09-21)

Owner spotted it on the live page: the profile rendered the literal text `{# R82: the invite box lives only on one's own profile, and only for a user the instance lets mint links (SiteSettings.invite_scope). #}` above the invite box.

**Cause, confirmed against the deployed Django 6.1.1 rather than reasoned about:** `{# … #}` is a **single-line** comment. Written across a line break, the tokenizer does not match it, so it is not a comment at all — the markers and the prose between them are ordinary template text and get emitted verbatim. Demonstrated directly in the container: `Template('{# single #}VISIBLE')` → `'VISIBLE'`, `Template('{# multi line\n   spanning #}VISIBLE')` → the markers come back in the output. `{% comment %}` is the multi-line form.

**Why no test caught it.** Every existing invite test asserted the *presence* of the pitch and the box; none asserted the absence of anything else in that region. A test that only checks for what should be there cannot see what shouldn't be.

**Fix.** Split into two single-line comments, matching the convention already used everywhere else in this codebase — `base.html:16-17` and `37-38` are each two `{# … #}` lines, not one wrapped one. A repo-wide scan (`grep '{#' | grep -v '#}'`) showed the R82 block was the **only** unterminated comment in the project, so the convention was sound and only my line broke it.

**Guard added, per R79.** `test_smoke.py::test_no_template_has_an_unterminated_hash_comment` walks every `.html` under the repo and fails listing `file:line` for any line containing `{#` without `#}` on the same line. Non-vacuity was proven before trusting it: run against `git show HEAD:…profile.html` it reports line 52, run against the fixed file it reports nothing. It is not a `django_db` test and costs milliseconds, so it guards every template this project has or will have rather than only the one that regressed.

**A live-verification trap worth recording, because it nearly produced a false pass.** The first check logged in with Django's test `Client` and fetched `/user/minnix/`, printing a line for each marker — and reported `False` for the leaked text. It looked like the fix had landed. It had not been *seen*: the response was **400 DisallowedHost**, because the test client's default `testserver` host is not in this instance's `ALLOWED_HOSTS`, so the body was a 143-byte error page containing neither the comment nor the invite box. The tell was the box being absent too — `may_send_invites(minnix)` returned `True`, so a rendering that showed no box could not be a correct render. Re-run with `HTTP_HOST='reeltalk.minnix.dev'` gives **200**, 4134 bytes, `invite-box` present, pitch present, and `R82` / `{#` / `#}` all absent. **The general rule: a negative assertion against a response whose status was never checked is not evidence.** Printing `False` for "the bug is gone" is worthless if the page you searched was never the page in question.

**Verified live after deploy.** `openRegistration = False`; anonymous home 200 with **0** `invite-box` and **0** `R82`; `reeltalk.css` and `js/invite.js` both 200; plain-HTTP LAN `http://192.168.1.138:3030/` still 200. All four containers healthy on the rebuilt images.

**Gate** (all services rebuilt, fresh `docker compose run --rm web`): `ruff check` — *All checks passed!*; `ruff format --check` — *90 files already formatted*; `makemigrations --check` — *No changes detected*; pytest **883 passed + 5 skipped in 398.02s** — baseline 882 + the 1 new guard, no pre-existing test changed state.

## 2A. Forward plan — feed interactions: likes, comments, per-post pages

**Forward-looking, not a record.** Written 2026-09-21, before any of it was built, so that each session can be pointed at one increment instead of re-deriving the shape of the feature. Owner request: *"letting users like and comment on other users' reviews from their feed, as well as clicking on a post in the feed and having it open in its own page similar to how it works in Mastodon so that all comments and replies are visible."*

### The finding that sets the scope

**Remote posts are already in the local feed.** `Status.feed_for` (`core/models.py:498`) has **no `local=True` filter**, and `feed_member_ids` includes remote mirrors the user follows. So a federated review shows up on the home feed today. **This is therefore not a single-instance feature** — every interaction we add locally has a federation counterpart, or we ship a visible asymmetry (likeable local posts, dead remote ones). That single fact roughly doubles the work and is why federation gets its own two increments at the end rather than being folded in.

### What exists, precisely

| Thing | State |
| --- | --- |
| The post model | `Status` (`core/models.py:354`), `status_type` ∈ `comment` / `review` / `review_rating` (R17). **No `Review`, `Post` or `Comment` class exists.** |
| Threading column | **`Status.reply_parent` exists** (`:410`, `PROTECT`, `related_name="replies"`) and is serialised outbound as `inReplyTo` (`objects.py:134`). **Nothing in the codebase ever writes it.** A dead column with a live wire field. |
| Likes | **Nothing at all.** No model, field, view, URL, template, serializer or handler. Only trace is intent: `PLAN.md:91` lists *"Like (favorite) on statuses"*. |
| Per-post page | `/status/<int:status_id>/` exists but is the **ActivityPub wire URL only** — `HttpResponse(status=404)` for non-AP clients, and filtered `local=True` (`core/views.py:102`). Its own docstring: *"there is no human-facing status page in v0.1."* |
| Feed rows | `feed_entries(user)` (`core/models.py:547`) returns a **Python list of `FeedEntry` dataclasses, not a queryset** — and `FeedEntry` (`:525`) carries **no status id**. A like/reply button rendered on a feed row has nothing to point at. |
| Inbox | `HANDLERS` = Follow, Undo, Create, Update, Delete (`inbox.py:29`). **`handle_undo` returns unless the inner type is exactly `"Follow"`** (`follow.py:70`) — `Undo(Like)` is silently ignored. No `Accept`/`Reject` is ever emitted, despite `PLAN.md:91`. |
| Inbound threading | **Dropped.** `_mirror_status` (`activitypub/statuses.py`) never reads `inReplyTo`; remote replies land as flat mirrors. |

### Decisions the owner must make — do not decide these solo (D17)

A Mastodon-style per-post page appears **nowhere** in `PLAN.md`: neither planned nor deferred. `PLAN.md §7` rule 1 makes that a decision to make *with the owner*. Six are load-bearing:

1. **The R35 fold vs "like this review."** A review by a user of a film they watched is *folded into* the "watched" `FeedEntry` (`core/models.py:634–641`), which then wears the review's rating and prose. So "like this review from the feed" is ambiguous — the row is a shelf event. Either keep the fold and attach the like to the underlying `Status` anyway, un-fold reviews into their own rows, or make shelf events non-likeable and only interact with real statuses.
2. **Do replies appear as top-level feed entries?** Mastodon shows them but commonly hides them. Recommendation: **no** — replies live on the post page only. This keeps the feed from exploding and matches "click the post to read the thread."
3. **Permalink URL shape.** `/status/<id>/` is already the AP id URL (`objects.py:65 note_url`). Recommendation: **use the same URL and content-negotiate** — that is exactly what Mastodon does, and the negotiation branch is already wired; only its HTML arm is a stub 404. The clean split: the **AP branch keeps `local=True`** (we must never mint identity for another instance's object), the **HTML branch serves both local and mirrors** (which also fixes "remote posts have no resolvable page here at all"). Alternative is a separate `/p/<id>/`, which avoids the collision but is less canonical.
4. **Like semantics.** Single binary like vs multiple reaction types. Recommendation: binary.
5. **Interim behaviour on remote posts,** before the federation increments land: hide the like/reply controls on mirrors (recommended — an honest absence) vs show controls that no-op (a lie).
6. **Is the HTML post page public?** The AP branch must stay anonymous (same reasoning as `film_detail`, D15/R40, owner-confirmed 2026-09-21). Whether the human page is public like film pages (R56) or members-only like search (R80) is genuinely open.

### Increments

Each is sized for one session and ends at a committed, deployed, verified checkpoint.

1. **Give feed rows an identity.** Add `status`/`status_id` to `FeedEntry`; settle decision 1 (the fold); make shelf-only rows explicitly non-interactive. **No user-visible feature** — it is the unblocking prerequisite, and everything after assumes it.
2. **The per-post page.** Fill in the HTML arm of `status_detail`'s existing negotiation branch per decision 3. Block-aware rendering (see gotcha 4). Renders the status in full plus its reply list — empty at first, and that is fine. This is the destination every later increment attaches to.
3. **Likes, local only.** `Like` model with **day-one AP identity discipline (R41/R42)** even though nothing federates yet — `origin_id` on create, unique constraint on `(user, status)`, so increment 5 doesn't need to re-shape the table. `POST /status/<id>/like/` toggle. AJAX with no reload, following the one existing precedent (`base.html:8` csrf meta + `search.js:144` fetch → `JsonResponse`). Counts on feed rows and the post page.
4. **Comments, local only.** Finally gives `reply_parent` a writer. Reply form, thread rendering with an explicit depth policy, `film_id` inheritance (see gotcha 2), soft-delete orphan handling (gotcha 3), block filtering.
5. **Federation, outbound.** Emit `Like` and `Undo(Like)`; extend `handle_undo` past the Follow-only check; emit threaded `Create(Note)` carrying `inReplyTo`. **Ids need a uuid fragment** or a re-like dedups as a redelivery (see `objects.py:update_activity`).
6. **Federation, inbound.** `Like` handler **keyed on the verified sender, never `activity["actor"]`** (every existing handler obeys this); read `inReplyTo` on ingest so remote threads stop flattening; decide the policy for a like targeting a note we don't have (fetch vs ignore).

### Two constraints to design around, not discover late

- **No feed pagination exists.** `feed_entries` materialises everything in Python and `index` renders all of it. The owner already crashed a browser with a 1,378-row watchlist (`social/views.py:525`). Whether comments multiply feed rows depends on decision 2 — if replies do appear, pagination becomes part of increment 4, not a later cleanup.
- **Broadcast is synchronous** — one blocking POST per remote follower, in-request, failures silently dropped (`broadcast.py:_deliver_to_followers`). A like on a post with many remote followers makes the request slow. **Django-Q2 is already available and used** for the TMDB backfill, so queued delivery is a live option the current pattern deliberately avoided.

### Out of scope here

Notifications (`PLAN.md:58`, `§5`) — "you got a like / someone replied" needs the whole unbuilt `Notification` model, page and count badge. This feature will want it, but it is a separate piece of work, not a tail on these six.

## 3. Host facts (this box)

- Fedora 44, Docker via dnf; compose project **`reeltalk`**, port **3030** owned by this stack (legacy stack torn down 2026-09-05).
- Owner's browser reaches the instance at **http://192.168.1.138:3030** — `.env` (gitignored) carries `ALLOWED_HOSTS=localhost,127.0.0.1,192.168.1.138` + `CSRF_TRUSTED_ORIGINS=http://192.168.1.138:3030`; if the workstation's LAN IP changes, update both or browser requests 400 (DisallowedHost) / form POSTs 403 (CSRF).
- Legacy insurance: six named volumes `reeltalk-work_*` kept until the new instance is proven, then `docker volume rm`. Pre-freeze DB dump at `/home/minnix/backups/reeltalk/pre-freeze-20260905.sql.gz`.
- Binding host quirks (all in PLAN.md §6): `:z` on bind mounts; `curl -4` (IPv6 docker-proxy resets); artifact volumes (`static_volume`, `media_volume`) re-initialize from the image after a runtime-user change — never touch `pgdata`. (The nginx-restart-after-web-rebuild and rebuild-all-celery-images quirks are gone with those services.)
- Resource constraint: local LLM inference on a separate server — at most ONE background subagent at a time; serialize heavy docker/pytest runs.

## 4. Decision log (rewrite-era)

New owner decisions for the rewrite are recorded here, numbered R1, R2, … The legacy decision log (its §8 #1–34) remains binding and is distilled as D1–D17 in PLAN.md §2 — do not re-litigate those. Stack-audit decisions below were taken with full discretion per the audit brief; none are product-visible or hard to reverse, so they are recorded rather than blocking.

- **R1 — Python 3.13 + uv.** Runtime on 3.13 (3.14 is current stable but only ~11 months out); uv for lockfile hygiene + build speed (`uv.lock` committed). Replaces M0's 3.11/pip.
- **R2 — Django 6.1 over staying on 5.2 LTS.** Current stable (6.1.1, 2026-09-02); zero migration cost on a fresh codebase, while 5.2's security EOL (2028-04) would force the same move within a year. New `MAILERS` setting adopted from day one.
- **R3 — uvicorn/ASGI replaces gunicorn/WSGI.** Django's forward path (async views, future WebSockets); no async views needed yet — sync code runs fine under ASGI.
- **R4 — Task queue deferred to M2, then Django-Q2 on Postgres.** M1 has zero background tasks; when the TMDB backfill lands (D11), Django-Q2 (MIT, active) with a Postgres cluster replaces celery + django-celery-beat + the redis broker in one package, its built-in scheduler covering periodic jobs. Rejected: Celery (inertia — 3 packages + Redis), ARQ (needs Redis), Dramatiq (LGPLv3+, heavier model), plain async views (no retry/survival for a ~15-min job).
- **R5 — No proxy, no Redis in the stack.** whitenoise serves static in-process and web exposes :3030 directly (D14 operator TLS unchanged); LocMemCache + DB sessions. nginx + redis services removed; artifact volumes re-initialized for the non-root image.
- **R6 — Frontend: vanilla JS, no framework, no build step** for v0.1 (search-as-you-type + one-click watchlist are ~50 lines of trivial JS). HTMX/Alpine rejected at this surface size; revisit if M5+ grows it.
- **R7 — M4 federation written from spec.** No maintained Python ActivityPub library exists (verified 2026-09-05: PyPI's `activitypub` is 2018-era, Fedify is TypeScript); clean-room rules already mandate building against the W3C specs.
- **R8 — Toolchain refresh.** ruff 0.16.6 (config modernized: +isort/+pyupgrade, legacy-carried ignores dropped), mypy 1.7.1 → 2.3.1 + django-stubs[compatible-mypy] 6.1.0, pytest 9.1.1 stack, responses 0.26.3.
- **R9 — App structure: `reeltalk.core` + `reeltalk.social`.** The project package `reeltalk` is no longer itself an app; real code lives in sub-apps split by domain — `core` (Film/MergedFilm/Shelf/ShelfFilm) and `social` (User/Status, lands increment 2). M4 federation will add a third (`reeltalk.activitypub`). Shared cross-app templates live at the repo-root `templates/` dir (TEMPLATES DIRS), not inside the project package.
- **R10 — Custom user model now.** `reeltalk.social.User` will be set as `AUTH_USER_MODEL` in increment 2, before any social migrations exist — it is very hard to change after the first migration. Defining it up front (with localname/display_name/summary/avatar/local-vs-remote + follow/block relations) keeps M4 federation clean instead of bolting on a separate profile model.
- **R11 — Minimal original CSS for M1; visual design deferred to M6 with the owner (D17).** M1 pages use a small, deliberately neutral hand-written stylesheet (original AGPLv3 code) just enough to make forms/lists legible — not a design statement and no third-party framework. The Bulma re-vendoring option (license-cleared in PLAN.md §4.2) and all real styling/artwork happen at M6 *with* the owner per D17. Owner may veto; nothing here is hard to reverse.
- **R12 — First-run flow: setup wizard gates the instance until an admin exists.** While no superuser exists, `/` and `/signup/` redirect to `/setup/`; the first account created there becomes the instance admin (is_staff + is_superuser) and the wizard then disappears. Rationale: an instance without an admin isn't operational, and open signup on a fresh public instance would otherwise race the wizard for the admin slot. Consequences: on a truly fresh instance "signing up" means going through the wizard first (still satisfies the M1 exit bar — one account-creation step); after setup, signup is open (the §3.7 open/invite-gated policy lands with site settings in increment 7). Localname rules fixed here: 1–30 chars of `[a-zA-Z0-9._-]` starting with a letter/digit; uniqueness enforced case-insensitively at signup (DB stays case-sensitive); email optional and non-unique until password reset needs it. Owner may veto.
- **R13 — User field scope for M1.** Per R10 the model carries localname/display_name/summary/avatar/local flag plus follow + user/film block relations now. Deliberately deferred: server-level blocking (a federation concept — lands with M4), feed filters, 2FA and email verification (§3.7 marks them later; M5). Adding these later is a plain migration on an existing model — no AUTH_USER_MODEL risk remains. Owner may veto.
- **R14 — Shelf/ShelfFilm shape for M1.** Shelf: name / identifier (unique per user) / description / user FK (CASCADE) / films M2M through ShelfFilm / origin_id+remote_id (day-one federation identity, same pattern as Film). No privacy/editable fields in v0.1 — D16 removes the custom-shelf UI; M4's wire type can add what it needs then. ShelfFilm: shelf CASCADE, film **PROTECT** (a film cannot be deleted while shelved — merge re-points first, so merges are unaffected), user non-null actor defaulting to the shelf owner in save(), shelved_date, unique (film, shelf). Consequence: Django 6.1's M2M `.add()` bypasses the through model's save() (bulk insert), so shelving code creates ShelfFilm rows explicitly or passes `through_defaults` — documented on the model. Owner may veto.
- **R15 — Default shelves created in `User.save()` for new local users only** (D1: `to-read`=Watchlist, `read`=Watched). Remote mirrors (M4) receive their shelves from federation instead of auto-creation. Import direction is social→core only; core references `"social.User"` as a string FK, so no Python or migration cycle. Consequence: existing local users are not backfilled retroactively — the dev instance still had zero users when this landed (first-run wizard untouched), and no released version predates it, so no data migration is warranted. Owner may veto.
- **R16 — Merge/absorb semantics.** `Film.merge_into(canonical)`, all-or-nothing: (1) backfill only empty fields onto the canonical via an explicit field list — subtitle/description/year/runtime/poster/tmdb_id/imdb_id scalars plus order-preserving unions for genres/directors/cast; identity/provenance (title/sort_title/origin_id/remote_id) and dates are never touched; (2) re-point related rows — ShelfFilm (shelved_date + actor preserved, duplicate-on-same-shelf deduped), Status, and `User.blocked_films` — one block per model in `_repoint_related()`; (3) record the MergedFilm old→new mapping; (4) delete the absorbed row; the canonical is saved **last** because a backfilled unique `tmdb_id` would otherwise collide with the value the absorbed row still holds. Owner may veto.
- **R17 — Status model shape for M1.** §3.2's Comment/Review/ReviewRating subtypes are one concrete `Status` table with a `status_type` discriminator (comment/review/review_rating; null = standalone note), not per-subtype tables or multi-table inheritance. Rationale: D5's one-review-per-user-per-film must coexist with soft-delete — enforced as a Postgres partial unique index on (user, film) WHERE status_type IN (review, review_rating) AND NOT deleted, which needs both columns in one table; M1 also forbids new runtime deps, so there is no model_utils for polymorphic MTI queries. Consequences: a rating-only entry (`review_rating`) is a review that always carries its rating (save() raises without one), editable in place and can later gain text while keeping its type (legacy behavior); re-finishing updates the existing row (D5 rule 4). Statuses live in `reeltalk.core` per owner direction, amending R9's parenthetical ("User/Status" → social). Soft-delete is instance-level: `status.delete()` keeps the row as a tombstone (identity fields intact for M4 federation consistency) with content cleared; queryset bulk delete is not used in our code paths. `mark_watched(user, film, *, rating, ...)` encapsulates §3.3 rules 3–4: validates the star rating before any DB write (0.5–5, half steps — keeps D10's rating×2 export integral), shelves onto Watched + removes from Watchlist (D1 binary model; mirrors legacy's mutually-exclusive read-status shelves), creates or updates the review entry, posts no feed note (D3). Film merge re-points statuses via a `_repoint_statuses` block in `_repoint_related()` (R16). Owner may veto.
- **R18 — `Film.raw_description` field.** The model stores `description` as rendered HTML; a new `raw_description` keeps the markdown source so the edit form pre-fills markdown, not stored HTML markup. Mirrors the established `Status.content` / `raw_content` pattern. One small migration (`core/0005`). Owner may veto.
- **R19 — M1 shelve/unshelve semantics (D1 binary model).** "Add to Watchlist" (`shelve_to_watchlist`) refuses when the film is already on the user's **Watched** shelf — a watched film can't also be wanted, and **unwatching is out of v0.1 scope** (no path off the Watched shelf). `mark_watched` is the only way onto Watched and it removes the film from Watchlist, so the two shelves are mutually exclusive at the model level regardless of entry point. Both helpers are idempotent / race-safe (`get_or_create`). Consequence: a user cannot currently "unwatch" a film in v0.1; revisit if the owner wants it. Owner may veto.
- **R20 — Finish-flow UI shape (owner's pick + engineering).** The §3.3 finish flow is a **modal dialog** with a **clickable half-star widget** (owner chose modal over inline/separate-page, and clickable stars over a select dropdown, per D17 alignment with the Letterboxd template). Engineering shape: one form + one endpoint (`POST /film/<id>/watched/`) serves both "Mark as watched" and "Edit review"; the edit case pre-fills from the user's existing rating + raw markdown (D5) and re-finishing updates the row in place. The star widget is ~60 lines of vanilla JS (R6) — ten invisible half-star hit zones over the CSS star fill; submit stays disabled until a rating is chosen, but the server still validates (no-JS / tampered input can't write an invalid rating). Owner may veto.
- **R21 — Film page lists all users' reviews/ratings.** The detail page shows every user's review + rating-only entry for the film (author, stars, text, date), not just the current user's — makes it a real social film page now rather than deferring others' reviews to M5. Owner chose this over own-only. Comments are not yet composed/listed (the v0.1 composer is a later increment); only the D5 review types show. Owner may veto.
- **R22 — Manual create/edit gating + dedup.** Film create/edit are **login-gated** (any authenticated user), not staff-gated — matches the M1 exit bar where the owner signs up and creates films; a public-instance spam concern is deferred to antispam (M5). The D4 TMDB lock is enforced in `film_edit` by redirecting (GET and POST) back to the detail page when `tmdb_id` is set, so TMDB metadata is never user-editable. `film_create` runs `find_match(title, year)` first and redirects to an existing match instead of inserting a duplicate (D7). Owner may veto.
- **R23 — Increment 6 shape: films-page URL/tabs + minimal feed.** The user's films page is `GET /user/<localname>/films/` with a `?tab=all|watchlist|watched` query param (default and unknown values fall back to all) rather than three separate paths — one named route, tab state stays in the URL so tabs are linkable. Publicly readable: user pages are public on a social site; blocking users/films and feed filters land with M4/M5 (R13). "All films" is the §3.5/D10 relationship set — films on any of the user's shelves ∪ films carrying a non-deleted status — so it matches what the export will emit. Query logic lives on `User` (`films_on_shelf` / `all_films`, both annotated with the user's D5 rating via one subquery) and `Status.feed_for(user)` (own + followed users' non-deleted statuses, newest first); views only pick a queryset. The feed is the home page for logged-in users (anonymous keeps the landing), no pagination in v0.1, groups join the union at M5. Owner may veto.
- **R24 — Site settings singleton + v0.1 signup policy semantics.** `SiteSettings` is a single row (pk=1, created on first use) in `reeltalk.social`, admin-managed: instance name/description (plain text, auto-escaped at render) and the §3.7 signup policy (`open`/`invite`). In v0.1 `invite` means **closed** — accounts are created by an admin; the invite mechanism itself is a later deliberate step (§3 does not specify one, so none was invented unilaterally). The first-run wizard (R12) is unaffected: it precedes settings and always creates the admin. Owner may veto.
- **R25 — Link-domain allowlist semantics + write-time enforcement.** `LinkDomain` rows gate outbound hrefs in user-authored markdown: a host matches when equal to an allowed domain or a subdomain of it (case-insensitive; port/userinfo stripped before matching; deny-by-default, so an empty list strips every external link from reviews/descriptions — anchor text stays). Enforced at write time inside `render_markdown` via bleach's per-tag attribute filter, so stored HTML never carries a disallowed href and no read-time filtering is needed. Owner may veto.
- **R26 — Film merge/absorb tool shape.** The §3.7 v0.1 admin "film merge/absorb tool" is a staff-only custom view at `/admin/films/merge/` (the ModelAdmin framework has no multi-object action) rendered in the admin chrome: pick one canonical film + N absorbed rows; the batch runs `Film.merge_into` inside one outer transaction so a mid-batch failure rolls back every merge in it. The form rejects selecting the canonical as its own absorb; all merge semantics stay on the model (R16). Owner may veto.
- **R27 — TMDB client shape (M2).** The v3 client lives in `reeltalk.core.tmdb` (film domain, per R9) and is server-side only (D8 key shared by all users; no browser calls). Failures map to a `TmdbError` base with three subtypes the spec calls out — `TmdbAuthError` (401), `TmdbRateLimitError` (429), `TmdbNetworkError` (network / unexpected status) — so views show a user-facing message rather than a stack trace. The TMDB overview is plain text: it is stored verbatim in `raw_description` and rendered to sanitized HTML for `description` via the existing `render_markdown` (one description code path; cast capped at ten). The client makes no DB calls itself — create-or-match and backfill act on films and import from here. Owner may veto.
- **R28 — Django-Q2 cluster shape + task-wrapper placement (M2).** One `worker` compose service (same image, `qcluster`) joins web+db; it waits for web to be *healthy* because the entrypoint only migrates when it starts uvicorn — that ordering is what creates the `django_q_*` tables before the worker's first poll. `Q_CLUSTER`: name `reeltalk`, workers 2, timeout 3600 (a full D11 backfill runs ~15 min; kept explicit so a long job is never killed mid-run), retry 7200 (must be ≥ timeout or django-q warns at every startup — in 1.11 `retry` is the ack wait and there is no `retry_timeout` key), orm `default`, backend `postgresql`. Tasks are plain importable functions enqueued by name (`async_task("reeltalk.core.tasks.<name>", ...)`) — 1.11 removed the `@task` decorator; wrappers live in `reeltalk/core/tasks.py` (film domain, R9) and delegate to the directly-testable modules they queue (`catalog`). `enqueue_backfill()` is the single place that knows the task path, so M3's import calls it without repeating the string. Owner may veto.
- **R29 — Search surface shape (M2, D6).** Routes: `GET /search/` (results page, `?q=` + `?page=` for TMDB pagination), `GET /search/film/<tmdb_id>/` (login-gated click-through running D7 create-or-match → redirect to the film page), `POST /search/watchlist/<tmdb_id>/` (login-gated JSON: materialize via D7 then shelve; `added`/`already` 200, `watched` 409 per D1, TMDB failure 502 + error payload), `GET /search/suggest/?q=` (JSON, min 2 chars, capped at 8). Source selection: TMDB when a key is configured, local-only without one; a TMDB *failure* degrades to local results plus the user-facing message rather than an error page (the suggest endpoint falls back the same way). Anonymous users see results without actions — no click-through link, no watchlist button — and their suggest rows point at `/search/?q=<title>` instead of the login-gated route. Locally blocked films are excluded from both surfaces (§3.4): TMDB rows by the user's blocked films' `tmdb_id`s, local rows by film id. The one-click button is keyed on `tmdb_id` (it may materialize a new row), and its CSRF token comes from a `<meta name="csrf-token">` in `base.html` rather than relying on cookie timing. "New film" leaves the top nav — search is the primary add-film surface per the owner's stated expectation; manual creation stays reachable via a low-key link in the search page's empty state and the route itself is unchanged. Owner may veto (esp. the nav change). **[Partially SUPERSEDED by R80 (2026-09-21): the anonymous half is withdrawn — `/search/` and `/search/suggest/` are now members-only and the header box is not rendered for strangers, because every anonymous read spends the instance's shared paid TMDB quota and the instance went public on 2026-09-21. Everything above about the authenticated shape stands.]**
- **R30 — Local-library search implementation (M2, D6 fallback).** `catalog.search_local(query)` ranks films with a `ts_rank(search_vector, plainto_tsquery('simple', %s))` annotation over the trigger-maintained column from M1 (migration 0002) — parameterized, so no tsquery injection; `rank > 0` filter drops non-matches; ordering is rank then sort_title. No new model fields or migrations: the tsvector stays a database-owned column (the ORM never reads/writes it). Empty/whitespace queries return early without a query. Owner may veto.
- **R31 — Suggest dropdown shows posters (owner request, 2026-09-09).** Each dropdown row renders a small poster thumbnail beside title + year so a film can be recognized by its artwork when the title isn't. Needs `poster_url` in the `/search/suggest/` JSON (TMDB rows: the search result's poster path; local rows: `film.poster`) and an `<img>` (with placeholder fallback) in the dropdown JS. CSP `img-src` already allows `image.tmdb.org` + self. Owner backlog, post-M2.
- **R32 — Suggest dropdown keyboard navigation (owner request, 2026-09-09).** Arrow keys move the active suggestion, Enter follows it, Escape closes (already works); mouse click unchanged. Standard combobox behavior in vanilla JS (R6). Owner backlog, post-M2.
- **R33 — Feed shows shelf events: watchlist additions + watched-with-review (owner request, 2026-09-09).** Home-feed entries must distinguish "X added Y to their Watchlist" from "X watched Y", the latter carrying the D5 review text + star rating. **Supersedes D3's "no automatic feed notes" for these two events.** Today a watched film already surfaces as its review status; the gaps are watchlist additions (invisible in the feed) and an explicit "watched" framing with stars. Implementation to decide next session: derive events from `ShelfFilm` rows (user, `shelved_date`, shelf identifier) unioned with statuses under the existing feed membership rules (own + followed users), deduping a watched film's review status into its watched entry — this avoids any migration — or add an explicit status type written at shelve time. Owner backlog, post-M2.
- **R34 — Film posters on social-feed rows (owner request, 2026-09-09).** Feed entries for film-anchored events show the film's poster thumbnail (placeholder when missing), consistent with the user-films-page thumbs. Owner backlog, post-M2.
- **R35 — Feed shelf events are derived from ShelfFilm rows, not written as statuses (implements R33).** The home feed is a read-side union: `feed_entries(user)` derives the two events D3 used to suppress ("added Y to their Watchlist" / "watched Y") from members' to-read/read `ShelfFilm` rows (actor + `shelved_date` + shelf identifier) and merges them with `Status.feed_for` statuses, newest first; a user's live D5 review folds into their watched entry (rating + text on the event, no second row), while comments — and reviews without a Watched row, which M4 remote mirrors may bring — stand alone. Rejected: explicit status types written at shelve time — it would need a migration, new write paths in `shelve_to_watchlist` / `mark_watched` / `unshelve_from_watchlist`, exclusion from D5's partial unique index, and deletion bookkeeping on unshelve/re-watch, for no v0.1 benefit; M4 will broadcast shelf events as ActivityPub objects at shelve time regardless of the local read model. Consequences: unshelving removes the feed note (shelf state is the single source of truth), a soft-deleted review leaves a bare "watched" entry, and there is no pagination — fine at v0.1 scale. Owner may veto.
- **R36 — M3 import/export shape.** Import validates the **full canonical ten-column header** up front (spec §3.5; the legacy accepted files carrying only TMDb ID/Name/Release Date) — missing columns are rejected with a message naming them, column order is not significant; 20,000-row cap. A non-numeric TMDb ID in a row is treated as absent (the row still imports by title+year) rather than aborting the whole transaction. Rated import rows **remove the film from the Watchlist** (D1 binary model, consistent with `mark_watched`), and an unrated row for an already-watched film is refused via `shelve_to_watchlist` ("already in your Watched list") — the legacy shelved anyway. Export rows are **deterministically ordered** (sort title, year, id) so repeated exports are byte-identical (legacy iterated a set). Known consequence: a film whose only relationship is a comment exports as an unrated row, and re-importing it would shelve it to the Watchlist — unreachable in v0.1 (no comment composer; M4 remote mirrors may bring such states), noted for M4. Owner may veto.
- **R37 — Bulk shelves aggregate into one feed entry (owner bug, 2026-09-10).** A file import produced one "added to Watchlist" feed entry per film (1,378 for the owner's watchlist). Read-side grouping in `feed_entries` keeps R35's no-write model: consecutive same-user/same-shelf `ShelfFilm` events within `FEED_BULK_WINDOW` = **5 minutes** collapse into one entry — "added Y and N other films to their watchlist", showing the newest film, dated at the newest row. The window is chosen from live data (the real 1,378-row import spanned 6.5 s, p99 row gap ~6 ms; deliberate manual adds are minutes-to-hours apart) and **chains** — each row only needs to be within the window of the previous one, so a slow import still aggregates whole. Edge cases: watched events aggregate the same way; rating-only statuses of aggregated films are absorbed (they must not surface as separate rows — ratings stay visible on film/user-films pages); a group containing a live written review (non-empty content) stays individual so the review text is not lost from the feed. Rejected: writing a single "bulk" status at import time — it contradicts R35's read-side derivation and D5's partial index, and needs deletion bookkeeping on unshelve. Owner may veto (esp. the 5-minute window).
- **R38 — One Q_CLUSTER worker (owner bug, 2026-09-10).** `workers: 2 → 1`. A re-import enqueues a second full backfill ID list by design (it heals stale stubs, D11); with two workers the import and re-import batches ran in lockstep — every poster double-downloaded (`get_available_name` suffixed names) and TMDB traffic doubled (~8 req/s against the 50/10-min limit), the aggravating factor of the poster incident. Serialized, the second batch runs after the first and is nearly free: `backfill_films` skips already-complete films without an API call. Cost accepted: a long backfill delays any other queued task (M4+ periodic jobs) — revisit if that ever matters. Owner may veto.
- **R39 — Federation wire formats + key storage (owner scope decision, 2026-09-10).** Owner: "A legacy reeltalk instance does not exist, and i don't care if reeltalk can federate with bookwyrm, only with a new reeltalk instance or current mastodon instance." Consequences: **outgoing** requests are signed per RFC 9421 (HTTP Message Signatures) with Ed25519 — the only published standard in this space and the format current Mastodon verifies (its Linzer path); the W3C ActivityPub REC specifies no signature scheme, and the draft-cavage line never became an RFC. **Incoming** accepts both live formats: a `Signature-Input` header → RFC 9421 (Ed25519 keys), else the old draft format (`keyId/algorithm/headers/signature`, RSA, rsa-sha256/hs2019 only) — what current Mastodon still sends. BookWyrm interop explicitly out of scope. Keys stored as PEM TextFields on `User`: local users get a pair generated at creation; remote mirrors carry the public key only (fetched from their Person doc); keyid = actor URL + `#main-key`. v0.1 limits accepted: no replay window on `created`/`date` (activities are idempotent, keyed by origin ids), one signature label per request, legacy path covers `(request-target) host date digest` only, `@target-uri` scheme follows `X-Forwarded-Proto` when present (D14 proxy). Owner may veto.
- **R40 — Actor-URL convention + identity/discovery endpoints (M4 increment 2).** A local user's actor is `/user/<localname>/` with content negotiation: an Accept naming `application/activity+json` or `application/ld+json` gets the Person document; anything else — including a bare `*/*` wildcard, which deliberately does not count — 302s to the films page, so browsers never receive JSON and no new profile-page design is invented (the human profile page with follow button is M5). Collection URLs are fixed from day one even though their routes land in increments 3–4: `/user/<localname>/inbox|outbox|followers|following/` + shared instance inbox `/inbox/`, so the Person document's shape never changes; between now and increment 4 those routes return 404 (dev-only window — no remote can complete a follow until the inbox exists anyway). Webfinger: case-insensitive localname match (our identity model treats case variants as one user — signup rejects insensitive duplicates — and Mastodon lowercases usernames), domain must be this instance's `DOMAIN`, stored spelling carried back in the subject so remotes learn the canonical case. NodeInfo 2.0: software `reeltalk` + `__version__`, protocol `activitypub`, `openRegistration` mirrors the signup policy (`invite` → false). Document URLs use the host as sent and follow `X-Forwarded-Proto` (D14, same rule as R39's signature verification); M6 will still need proxy-header config for site-wide scheme correctness. Pre-existing local users without keys are backfilled by data migration `social/0004` (auto-runs on web start; `User.ensure_keypair()` is the idempotent primitive, remote mirrors untouched). Owner may veto.
- **R41 — Outbox/collections shape + object wire types + day-one origin identity (M4 increment 3).** The read-side federation surface lands before any delivery: **collections** are ActivityPub `OrderedCollection`s — a collection URL served without `?page` returns the collection doc (`totalItems` + `first`/`last` page URLs), with `?page=N` one `OrderedCollectionPage` (`partOf` + 1-based `startIndex` + that slice's `items`); `PAGE_SIZE = 20`, shared by outbox/followers/following (shelves reuse it later). **Outbox** = the user's non-deleted local statuses, each a `Create` activity wrapping a **Note** object (newest first; the object rides inline so a follower backfills from the outbox without extra fetches). **Followers/following** = OrderedCollections of Person docs from the follow M2Ms, local users only (a remote entry needs the followed actor's home URL, which the mirror does not store yet — lands with increment 4/5); empty until follows land. **Inboxes** (per-user + shared) exist now so the Person doc's URLs never 404 (R40): GET → 405, POST → 202 Accepted stub (`@csrf_exempt` — AP deliveries carry no CSRF token); the real handling (signature check, dedup by origin id, remote-mirror creation) is increment 4. **Object wire types** are built fresh from spec (R7): the custom **Film** type (D15) carries `tmdbId`/`imdbId` for D7 dedup; statuses serialize as one **Note** type mapped back by shape (a `rating` ⇒ review/rating-only, content without a rating ⇒ comment). **Object ids use the day-one origin fields**: a local object's wire id is its `origin_id` (backfilled to the pk — data migration `core/0006` heals existing rows, `Film.save`/`Status.save` set it on create via a queryset `.update` so `auto_now` `updated_date` is untouched), giving `/film/<id>/` and `/status/<id>/`; a local film's id resolves to the existing detail route, which now serves the Film JSON-LD doc to AP clients by content negotiation (browsers keep HTML). Reconstructing a *remote* object's home-instance URL from `remote_id`, and the fetch endpoint for `/status/<id>/`, both land with delivery (increments 4/6) — v0.1 serializes local objects only and remotes consume them inline. Owner may veto.
- **R42 — Inbox core shape + remote-user mirrors (M4 increment 4).** The inbox POST pipeline is: extract the keyid from either wire format → resolve the sender → verify the signature → parse JSON → dedup by activity wire id → dispatch on type. Status codes: **401** for a missing, unresolvable, or invalid signature (an unreachable sender included — verification happens before anything is parsed or recorded), **400** for a signed but unparseable body, **202 Accepted** for everything else (handled, gracefully ignored, or duplicate — the outcome is not exposed to the sender); GET stays 405. Increment 4 registers **no activity handlers** (Follow/Undo → increment 5; Create/Update/Delete → increment 6): every well-formed activity is accepted + deduped + otherwise ignored, which is spec-correct for unsupported types (§3.6) and keeps redelivery idempotent; `inbox.HANDLERS` is where increments 5/6 register, and the dedup row + handler run in one transaction so a failed delivery can be retried. **Remote-user mirrors:** new `User.actor_url` (the home Person URL; partial unique index for non-local users) is the mirror's identity — keyids resolve to it and it is the home-instance wire id served back to other instances, satisfying R41's deferred reconstruction for the only remote objects that exist in increment 4 (Persons); Film/Status object mirrors do not exist until increment 6, where they should carry the home URL as received (the integer `remote_id` alone cannot reconstruct a host). Mirror localname = `<preferredUsername>@<netloc>` — local accounts can never contain `@` (R12), so mirrors cannot collide with locals or with same-named users on other domains; `localname` widened 30→255 (signup keeps R12's ≤30 for local accounts). Mirrors carry display_name + public key only — no summary/avatar in v0.1, create-only (no document re-fetch on later deliveries, **no key rotation**: a rotated key fails verification until the mirror is refreshed). **Security notes:** keyid-driven fetch is open by design (federation requires it; the signature authenticates what was fetched); scheme restricted to http/https; no private-IP block (it would break same-host multi-instance testing) — accepted v0.1 limitation. Owner may veto.
- **R43 — Follow/Undo(Follow) handling, both directions (M4 increment 5).** The follow M2M (`User.follows`, `related_name="followers"`) is the single source of truth for the relationship; recording a row is what feeds the existing feed query (it already reads `user.follows` — **no feed-query change**) and the followers/following collections. **Inbound** (a remote user follows/unfollows one of ours): the registered `Follow`/`Undo` handlers record the M2M **from the verified sender** resolved from the signature keyid — never the activity's self-declared `actor`, which an attacker could forge (the signature is the authority; pinned by a forged-actor test). An Undo acts only when its object is a Follow; any other shape, or an unresolvable "followed" actor, is ignored gracefully (§3.6). The followed actor is resolved among users this instance already knows (`resolve_known_actor`: local by R40 path on this host, remote by existing mirror) — no first-contact fetch as a side effect of processing a follow. **Outbound** (a local user follows/unfollows a remote user): `follow_user`/`unfollow_user` record the M2M and deliver a signed Follow / Undo(Follow) to the followed user's home inbox (R39 signatures via a new `delivery` module — a single synchronous POST, no retry queue in v0.1; the M2M is recorded before delivery so a failed send leaves the follow in place locally). The follower must be local (it signs with its own key; a mirror has none); an unfollow is a no-op when not already following (no spurious fetch or Undo). **Activity ids are unique per event** (uuid fragment) — a deterministic (follower, followed) id would make a re-follow dedup as a redelivery of the first on the receiving instance. **Mirror inbox:** new `User.inbox_url` (`social/0006`) stores the home inbox advertised by the Person document, populated at mirror creation (R42 create-only — no refresh); delivery falls back to the `<actor_url>/inbox` path convention (holds for ReelTalk + Mastodon) when a document advertised none. **Collections:** `_person_collection` now includes remote mirrors as minimal Person documents whose id is the mirror's home actor URL (R42), alongside full local Person documents. **No route/UI in this increment** — the follow button and remote-user discovery are M5; the outbound mechanism is a directly-testable library function ready for that surface. Owner may veto.
- **R44 — Status broadcast + shelf events, remote object mirrors (M4 increment 6).** **Remote identity:** `Film.remote_url` + `Status.remote_url` persist the home wire URL as received, with partial unique indexes (`remote_url != ""`) on both models — R42's deferred note resolved: an integer `remote_id` alone can't reconstruct a host, so the full URL is the mirror's identity (and its dedup key). A film mirror is a row whose `remote_url` is set (no `local` flag on Film); mirrors are create-only with no document refresh or key rotation (R42) and skip poster download. **Wire types:** custom **ShelfEvent** object — id stable per (user, film, shelf identifier), so the *object* dedups across re-shelve cycles while the Create/Delete *activity* ids stay uuid-per-event (a re-shelve must not be swallowed as a redelivery). `update_activity` uses the same split (uuid activity id per edit, stable object id); `delete_activity`'s id is fully stable — a status is deleted at most once in v0.1 — and rides the tombstone's Note inline so the receiver keys the deletion by origin id. **Inbound:** `Create`/`Update`/`Delete` handlers attribute everything to the verified sender (never `attributedTo`). The single Note type maps back by shape (rating ⇒ Review/ReviewRating, content-without-rating ⇒ Comment, no film ref ⇒ standalone); a review mirror also mirrors the author's Watched shelf row (the R35 feed renders "watched" with stars). Film references resolve local-by-path → existing mirror by `remote_url` → fetch-and-mirror, and **D7 `find_match` runs before creating a film mirror** so the same movie never gets two rows. An unresolvable film reference raises `RemoteObjectError` → the transaction rolls back including the dedup row (retryable on redelivery — never silently dropped). Film deletions are ignored gracefully in v0.1 (related rows are PROTECTed). **Outbound:** broadcast goes to *remote* followers only (local followers read local rows), signed with the author's key via `delivery.deliver_activity`; a per-follower failure is dropped (no retry queue in v0.1 — a dead follower must not fail the user's request; the local write commits first, so only the remote copy lags). File import broadcasts nothing (§3.5). **Surface:** `/status/<id>/` serves the Note document to AP clients only (R41's deferred route; 404 for browsers/tombstones/mirrors). No review-delete view in v0.1, so `broadcast_status_delete` is library-only until M5 adds one. `inbox_for()`'s fallback keeps R43's no-trailing-slash convention. Owner may veto.
- **R45 — Two-instance local federation topology + verification state kept (M4 increment 7).** Instance B runs this repo as compose project `reeltalk-b` (`docker compose -p reeltalk-b --env-file .env.b up -d web db`; `.env.b` self-declares `ENV_FILE=.env.b`, and docker-compose.yml's `env_file:` is now `${ENV_FILE:-.env}` — the `--env-file` flag alone only feeds interpolation, not container env). B has its own named volumes (never touching A's `pgdata`), port 3031, DOMAIN=`reeltalk-b.test` (host `/etc/hosts` → 127.0.0.1 — the name exists for humans and webfinger acct URIs; containers never resolve it). All cross-instance and live-verification traffic goes over the published ports via the host LAN IP (`http://192.168.1.138:3030` / `:3031`): each container's localhost is its own and compose projects have separate networks; one canonical address per instance keeps every absolute actor/film/status URL consistent — mirror identity is keyed on the exact home URL, so mixed hosts would fragment identities. A stays DOMAIN=`localhost` (webfinger matches the acct-URI domain against `settings.DOMAIN`, not the request host, so IP-based discovery works). The exit-bar verification state (mutual follows + mirrors + exchanged reviews between minnix@A and fedb@B; fixture films Arrival/Interstellar) is **kept** — B is a standing peer for M5's follow UI; third-party stub artifacts were removed. No worker on B, no TMDB key (verification films are manual). Owner may veto (e.g., tear B down after M5: `docker compose -p reeltalk-b down -v`).
- **R46 — Film posters federate on mirror creation (M4 follow-up, owner bug 2026-09-13).** A freshly created film mirror downloads the remote Film document's `image` poster (best-effort); R44's "skip poster download" limit is superseded for new mirrors. Deliberate boundaries: **only on creation** — existing mirrors are not backfilled (create-only, R42) and D7-matched local rows never receive a federated poster (a local row's metadata stays local-owner-controlled); **failure never fails delivery** — unsupported scheme / network error / non-2xx / >10 MiB / non-image payload logs a warning and the mirror simply has no poster (the film-document fetch keeps its stricter `RemoteObjectError` semantics, since an unanchored review must stay retryable). Defensive details: http/https only, Content-Length pre-check + capped streamed read (a hostile body cannot exhaust memory), Pillow `verify()` before storing (a forged "poster" payload is never written to the media volume), storage name = sanitized URL basename with a generated fallback. Owner may veto (e.g., drop the size cap or the image verification).
- **R47 — Human profile page at the actor URL (M5 increment 1).** `/user/<localname>/` keeps R40's content negotiation for ActivityPub clients but supersedes the browser 302-to-films: browsers get a real profile page (avatar or placeholder, display name + `@handle`, bio, films link; "Edit profile" button on one's own page). The route moves to the root URLconf (`user-profile`) ahead of the AP include and matches a new `PROFILE_LOCALNAME_RE` — R40's local charset plus `@` and `:`, because mirror localnames are `<preferredUsername>@<netloc>` (the netloc carries non-default ports); **the films route adopts the same pattern**, which fixes remote users' films pages 404'ing before M5. Local lookup stays case-insensitive (R40), mirrors match verbatim (disjoint namespaces — `@` is not in R12's local charset). **AP clients get 404 on a mirror profile**: the mirror's canonical Person document lives on its home instance (R42) and this instance must not serve a second identity for it. Consequence: webfinger's `profile-page` rel link now lands on the HTML profile (previously the 302 target). Owner may veto.
- **R48 — Profile editing shape (M5 increment 1; owner sign-off 2026-09-13).** `/preferences/profile/` (login-gated, local users only — a mirror is redirected with a message; its home instance owns it) edits display name, bio, and avatar. The bio is markdown rendered at write time through the existing `render_markdown` (the LinkDomain allowlist gates outbound links exactly as in reviews/descriptions), with the source kept in new `User.raw_summary` so later edits pre-fill markdown, not markup (R18's raw-source pattern; migration `social/0007`). No avatar removal in v0.1 (re-upload replaces). Letterboxd comparison made explicit per D17: this matches LB's /edit/profile (avatar + bio) shape — the owner chose to include it so profiles have a write path and federation has visible content to exchange. Owner may veto.
- **R49 — Lazy mirror profile refresh (M5 increment 1).** A remote user's profile page triggers a best-effort fetch of the home Person document when the mirror is missing an avatar or bio: fill-missing only (display name, summary, avatar), never a refresh of values already held. Summary HTML from the wire is sanitized through `core.utils.sanitize_html` (the same allowlist as locally rendered markdown); avatars download through the shared defensive image fetch (2 MiB cap + Pillow verify — R46's poster logic, now `mirrors.fetch_image_bytes`). A LocMemCache throttle (1 h per actor URL) keeps a passive page view from becoming an outbound-fetch loop against a home instance that never fills those fields; any failure is silent (the page renders with what we have). **Identity fields are never touched — no key rotation, no inbox re-derivation (R42):** a rotated key in the document is ignored, so signature verification keeps working against the stored key. Consequence: a remote user who later edits their bio is not picked up until the throttle + missing-field condition re-aligns (v0.1 accepted; mirrors stay create-only for identity). Owner may veto (e.g., the throttle window or the fill-missing-only posture).
- **R50 — Follow/unfollow button on the profile page (M5 increment 2).** Placement: right-aligned `.profile-actions` slot in the profile head row — "Edit profile" for self, a Follow / Following POST form for other authenticated viewers; hidden on one's own profile and for anonymous visitors. Semantics: local target → plain `User.follows` M2M add/remove (no delivery — same instance); remote target → the existing signed `follow_user` / `unfollow_user` delivery (R43). POST routes `/user/<localname>/follow|unfollow/` share the profile's extended localname pattern (R47), so mirror handles (`<user>@<netloc>`, port included) match; GET → 405, login-gated, redirect back to the profile with a message. Only resolvable profiles can be followed — an unknown handle is a 404 (discovery at /find/ creates an unknown remote's mirror first; the button only renders on resolvable profiles). A down home instance at delivery time records the local state and shows a warning instead of 500'ing the request (same posture as status broadcasts, R44 — no retry queue in v0.1; the M2M is recorded before delivery, so the remote's copy lags). v0.1 limit accepted: following a blocked user is not gated — blocking lands in increment 3. Owner may veto.
- **R51 — Remote-user discovery at /find/ (M5 increment 2).** Login-gated single input `user@domain` (exactly one `@`, both parts non-empty after strip). A same-domain handle (case-insensitive against `settings.DOMAIN`) resolves the local account directly (iexact; stored spelling wins, R40) — no network. Any other domain goes through webfinger (RFC 6454) over real HTTP: GET `<scheme>://<domain>/.well-known/webfinger?resource=acct:<user>@<domain>`; **scheme rule** — an explicit non-default port in the domain means plain http at that port (LAN instances published on arbitrary ports with no TLS), otherwise https is tried first with http as fallback; a 404 from a reachable server is a definitive "no such user" while only network failures fall through to the next scheme. The self link (rel=self, type application/activity+json) gives the actor URL → `ensure_mirror` (existing mirror by actor_url, else created from the Person document on first contact — R42) → redirect to the mirror's profile. **Resolve-and-redirect only: submitting never auto-follows** — one POST changes at most one relationship, and the follow action stays on the profile page. Failures (malformed input / unknown local user / unreachable domain / malformed JRD) re-render the form with a message and keep the submitted value. The webfinger server side compares the acct domain against `settings.DOMAIN` as a string, so it works unchanged with IP:port domains (R52). Owner may veto.
- **R52 — DOMAIN switch to canonical IP:port (amends R45's topology note; owner-approved 2026-09-13).** Both instances' gitignored operator env files now carry `DOMAIN=192.168.1.138:3030` (A) / `DOMAIN=192.168.1.138:3031` (B), so webfinger discovery works end-to-end in both directions over real HTTP with zero DNS hacks, and local users' displayed handles (`User.username`) + webfinger subjects use the same canonical addresses as R45's mirror actor URLs. No stored data changes — DOMAIN is never persisted in rows (the username property, webfinger subject, and about page all compute it at request time); existing mirrors keep their R45 canonical `actor_url`s (built from the Host header, not DOMAIN). Both ALLOWED_HOSTS already cover the IP; CSRF_TRUSTED_ORIGINS already covers both origins. Settings made port-tolerant: new `DOMAIN_HOST` (bare host) feeds the ALLOWED_HOSTS default + DEFAULT_FROM_EMAIL (a From address can't carry a port); BASE_URL appends WEB_PORT only when DOMAIN has no explicit port. Each instance's web restarted after its env edit. Owner may veto.
- **R53 — Block/unblock user button on the profile page (M5 increment 3).** Placement: the `.profile-actions` slot beside the follow button — a Block / Blocked POST form for other authenticated viewers, hidden on one's own profile and for anonymous visitors (you cannot block yourself; the route rejects it). Semantics: **read-side state only** — a plain `User.blocks` M2M add/remove. POST routes `/user/<localname>/block|unblock/` share the profile's extended localname pattern (R47) so mirror handles match; GET → 405, login-gated, redirect back to the profile with a message. Only resolvable profiles can be blocked — an unknown handle is a 404 (the button only renders on resolvable profiles, as with follow). **Blocking a remote user is local-only in v0.1 (accepted limit):** no Block activity is delivered over federation — a remote target is handled exactly like a local one (a local M2M change, nothing sent to the home instance), so the blocked user's home instance never learns of the block and keeps federating their content to everyone else; only *this* instance's reads filter it out. This matches the read-side nature of blocking (it changes what the blocker sees, not what exists). Owner may veto (e.g., require a federation Block activity later — that would be an M6+ delivery concern).
- **R54 — Feed filter rule: blocking a user removes them from the feed entirely (M5 increment 3).** The home-feed membership is now `User.feed_member_ids()` = **self plus followed users, minus blocked users**, read by both `Status.feed_for` and `feed_entries`. Consequence: a blocked user's **statuses and shelf events alike** disappear from the blocker's feed — one rule covers both, so there is no path for a blocked user's "added Y to their Watchlist" or review to leak in. Self is always present (you cannot block yourself). **Interaction with follow (R50):** following a blocked user stays allowed and is not gated or auto-reversed by blocking — but while the block stands, that user's content stays hidden (membership = followed − blocked). Unblocking restores them immediately if still followed. Rejected: filtering only statuses (would let shelf events through) or only shelf events (would let reviews through) — the membership-level exclusion is simpler and total. Owner may veto.
- **R55 — Block/unblock film control on the film page (M5 increment 3).** Placement: the `.film-actions` row (authenticated viewers only, like the shelve controls) — a subtle inline Block / Blocked form at the end of the row. POST routes `/film/<id>/block|unblock/` toggle the local-only `User.blocked_films` M2M; login-gated, redirect back to the film page with a message. **v0.1 read-side effect is exclusion from search** — already wired in M2 (`_blocked_tmdb_ids` for TMDB rows by `tmdb_id`, `_local_film_ids` for local rows by id); this increment only adds the surface to set the state. The film page itself stays reachable by direct URL (blocking a film hides it from discovery, not from a known link). Deliberately **out of scope**: hiding a blocked film's shelf events / others' reviews from the feed — that is a film-level *feed filter* (a distinct §3.2 "feed filters" concept), not a user block, and lands only if the owner wants it. Owner may veto.
- **R56 — Film page hides blocked users' reviews (M5 increment 3).** `film_detail` excludes reviews whose author is in the viewer's `User.blocks` — for **authenticated viewers only** (blocking is per-logged-in-user state, so anonymous visitors still see every review, and a visitor who hasn't blocked anyone sees all). The D5 review list is otherwise unchanged (all non-deleted review/rating entries, newest first, R21). This is the film-page half of R54's "blocking removes a user's content" rule; the two are independent surfaces (feed via `feed_member_ids`, film page via this exclusion) so each can be reasoned about separately. Owner may veto.
- **R57 — Review-delete surface: author-only soft-delete + broadcast (M5 increment 4).** Placement: a subtle "Delete" form right-aligned in the review's `.review-head` row on the film page, rendered **only** for an authenticated viewer who is the author of that review (`review.user_id == user.id`) — hidden for other users' reviews and anonymous visitors. (An author's own status is always `local=True`, since mirrors can't authenticate, so the template needs no separate local check; the route enforces it regardless.) Route: `POST /status/<id>/delete/` (login-gated, POST-only → 405 on GET), whose lookup is scoped to `user=request.user, local=True, deleted=False` — so another user's review, a remote mirror, or an already-deleted tombstone all **404** (author-only is the security boundary). Semantics: `Status.delete()` soft-delete (tombstone keeps identity/`origin_id`, content cleared) + `broadcast_status_delete` to the author's remote followers (the M4 increment 6 path; a dead follower drops its send, R44 posture), then redirect back to the film page. **The film stays on the Watched shelf** — v0.1 has no unwatch (R19); deleting removes only the review, leaving a bare "watched" feed entry (R35's documented consequence) and allowing a re-review via the finish modal (D5's partial index ignores the tombstone). **Federation:** a local user deleting their own review broadcasts Delete to remote followers; a mirror's review is not deletable locally (no button + route rejects non-local). Independent of R56 (which hides blocked users' reviews read-side) — deletion is an author-only write on one's own content. v0.1 note: there is no confirmation dialog (consistent with every other mutating button — shelve/block/etc.) and no undelete path; both are accepted, owner may veto (e.g. add a confirm or an undo). Owner may veto.
- **R58 — Daily `pg_dump` backup job shape (M6 increment 1).** A `backup_database` management command (the repo's first) dumps the configured DB with `pg_dump --format=custom` into `BACKUP_DIR` (default `/app/backups`, the backups volume) and prunes to the newest `BACKUP_RETENTION` dumps (default 7). Connection params are read from Django's live `connection.settings_dict`, so it targets whatever DB the app points at — the real one in production, the test DB under pytest — which is also what makes the end-to-end path unit-testable; the password goes through `PGPASSWORD`, never argv (no process-list leak). Filenames are zero-padded UTC timestamps (`<db>-YYYYMMDDTHHMMSSZ.dump`) so lexicographic order == chronological, and retention prunes by sorting on the name. Scheduling: a dedicated `backup` compose service runs a lean daily sleep loop (`backup-loop.sh`, wakes at `BACKUP_TIME`, default 03:17 in the container TIME_ZONE) — chosen **over** a Django-Q2 scheduled task so backups run independent of web/worker/queue health and no qcluster `--beat` process is added, while the dump logic stays in one tested command (the service is just a thin daily wake; a failed run logs a warning and retries next day). `postgresql-client` (PostgreSQL License, §4.7) is added to the image for `pg_dump`/`pg_restore`; `/app/backups` is created appuser-owned + declared a VOLUME so the named volume initializes writable by the runtime user (the artifact-volume ownership fix, PLAN.md §6). Custom format over plain SQL: smaller, gzip-compressed, `pg_restore`-able. Owner may veto (esp. the dedicated-service-over-scheduler choice and the 7-dump retention default).
- **R59 — Onboarding is a static getting-started page, not an interactive tour (M6 increment 2; owner decision 2026-09-15).** The PLAN.md §3.7 "guided tour" item ("[polish, with owner]") was resolved **with the owner** per D17 (no solo UX calls). Options put to them, anchored on how Letterboxd actually onboards (verified: their `/welcome/` is a single static scrollable page — no spotlight/coach-mark steps over the live UI): (a) a Letterboxd-style **static welcome page**, (b) a short dismissible first-run overlay (the brief's "likely shape"), or (c) no tour. The owner chose **(a)**. Shape: a public `GET /welcome/` walking the core loop in four numbered steps — find a film (TMDB search, the primary add-film flow) → add to Watchlist → mark watched (rating required + optional review) → follow people — reached right after signup (the `signup` view now redirects there instead of `index`; the first-run `setup` wizard is unchanged) and via a persistent low-key "Getting started" footer link on every page. Anonymous visitors get a Sign-up CTA; signed-in users see their handle + a live Find-people link. **Deliberate boundaries:** manual create/edit stays out of the tour (search is primary — kept low-key per the owner); it is a plain template over `base.html` with **no JS** (R6) and no new library, which also sidesteps the Shepherd.js license re-obtain flagged in PLAN.md §4.2; real visual styling stays deferred to the artwork item (D17). Rejected: (b) the interactive overlay — more work (hand-written vanilla-JS spotlight/positioning), diverges from Letterboxd, and buys little over a page for a four-step loop; (c) no tour — leaves new users with no pointer past signup. Owner confirmed 2026-09-15.
- **R60 — Grindhouse / midnight-movie visual identity, co-created with the owner (M6 artwork, D17; owner decisions 2026-09-15).** The M6 artwork item rebrands ReelTalk from the neutral M1 CSS to an original late-70s/early-80s grindhouse aesthetic — explicitly **not** Letterboxd-styled (Letterboxd is the structure/UX template only; see the owner's 2026-09-15 clarification). Four decisions confirmed with the owner before building: **(1) Palette** — seven swatches read off the reference spec sheet, approved as proposed: Blood Red `#a02e24` (primary accent), Amber `#d1581f`, Mustard `#dda32b` (ratings/badges), Dirty Cream `#ebe3d3` (body text + paper surfaces), Teal `#2f6b68`, Brown `#6e4a2c` (borders/surfaces), Deep Black `#171310` (page background). **(2) Typography** — three roles per the spec (condensed-bold rough display; hand-painted script/letterpress for wordmark + accents; readable body sans), **self-hosted WOFF2 under `static/fonts/` via `@font-face`** (no CDN — self-hosted fediverse instance; no build step per R6), SIL OFL fonts (AGPLv3-safe per REWRITE.md rule 4): display = **Anton**, accent/script = **Kaushan Script**, body stays the **system-ui** stack for legibility. **(3) Branding** — a **neon marquee treatment** for the ReelTalk wordmark in the header (glowing display type + thin amber rule) plus an **original icon favicon** (film-reel / ticket-stub, blood-red on deep-black). **(4) Texture** — **medium**: halftone + distressed-paper accents on cards/poster frames/buttons; **hard rule: body copy and form fields stay clean and legible**, grit lives only on display headings, accents, buttons, and surface textures. Built in five owner-reviewed sub-steps (palette → fonts → header/wordmark/favicon → components → texture). **Alternatives kept at the ready** if the owner wants to try them after live review: a softer-contrast or bolder/neon palette variant; a Monoton neon-tube wordmark instead of Kaushan Script; system-fonts-only (no downloads); light or heavy texture levels. Owner may veto any of it; nothing here is hard to reverse.
